# EXISTING BEADS_RUST STRUCTURE — THE SPEC

<!-- Phase 1 document. THE contract of the port. Rule: after reading this
     file, an implementer must NOT need the original's source. Behavior is
     described as WHAT (inputs → outputs, verbatim strings, exact numbers),
     never HOW (no "uses a dict", no code). Every clause is numbered
     S<section>.<n> and cites its provenance `file:line` in the original;
     port defs, laws and golden cases refer to clauses by number.
     Data (formats, strings, constants) is quoted verbatim. Algorithms are
     described behaviorally with an | input | output | examples table.
     A gap discovered later is an OQ entry, resolved by RUNNING the
     original on a new case (captured as a golden), then amended here. -->

Provenance: the original's source pinned at tag `v0.6.0` (commit `b1cfebe05437463e91a353cf2bedafac27266f5b`, tree `45cc06f56529d614d2602a327f469a8478fea17e`), read from `legacy/BEADS_RUST_v0.6.0/`; behavior from the captured goldens (`goldens/MANIFEST.txt`) and from runs of the pinned `br 0.6.0` binary in the `scripts/ws-run.sh` sandbox. Extracted 2026-09-20 by seven section-owning extractors; assembled by `scripts/merge-spec-parts.py` from `docs/spec-parts/` (the part files are kept).
Coverage: see §12.

## S1. Command-line surface

<!-- from docs/spec-parts/S1_S9.md -->

| S1.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1.1 | The binary is `br`. The invocation form is `br [GLOBAL OPTIONS] <COMMAND> [COMMAND OPTIONS] [POSITIONALS]`, and the port's contract adds the global `--no-db` to every invocation: `br --no-db <command> …`. Exactly one subcommand is required; there is no default command. | `src/cli/mod.rs:691` (`#[command(name = "br", …)]`), `src/cli/mod.rs:694` | usage_none, list_plain |
| S1.2 | Arguments are read from the process argv as UTF-8 strings; an argument that is not valid UTF-8 terminates the process inside the argument reader before any command runs (Rust `std::env::args`, probe RS-20). No golden exercises a non-UTF-8 argument; the corpus is entirely UTF-8. | `src/main.rs:35` (`Cli::parse()`) | (case to add: `usage_non_utf8_arg` — argv `["\xff"]`) |
| S1.3 | The twelve global options are accepted at any position — before the subcommand, after it, or after a positional — because every one is declared `global = true`: `--db <DB>` (path), `--actor <ACTOR>` (string), `--json` (flag), `--no-daemon` (flag), `--no-auto-flush` (flag), `--no-auto-import` (flag), `--allow-stale` (flag), `--lock-timeout <LOCK_TIMEOUT>` (unsigned 64-bit), `--no-db` (flag), `-v`/`--verbose` (repeat-counted flag), `-q`/`--quiet` (flag), `--no-color` (flag). Measured: `br --no-db --json list` and `br --no-db list --json` produce identical stdout. | `src/cli/mod.rs:698`–`src/cli/mod.rs:744` | list_json, list_quiet, actor_flag_create |
| S1.4 | Under `--no-db` the globals `--db`, `--no-daemon`, `--no-auto-flush`, `--no-auto-import`, `--allow-stale` and `--lock-timeout` parse and are accepted but reach no observable behavior: every database path they select is skipped. `--no-db` itself selects the JSONL-only store. | `src/main.rs:81` (`storage_enabled = ctx.is_initialized() && !ctx.no_db()`), `src/main.rs:2238` | list_plain, where_plain |
| S1.5 | `-v` is a counting flag: `-v` sets verbosity 1, `-vv` sets 2, and repeating it is not an error (unlike `--json`, S1.12). Verbosity only reaches the `tracing` subscriber on stderr, which the sandbox pins to `RUST_LOG=error`, so no golden's bytes change with it. | `src/cli/mod.rs:735`, `src/main.rs:41` | (case to add: `verbose_twice` — `["-vv", "list"]` on `@fx=basic`) |
| S1.6 | Output mode is selected by this ordered test, first match wins: (1) `--json`, or a command-local `--robot` on one of `close`, `reopen`, `ready`, `scheduler`, `blocked`, `stats`/`status`, `defer`, `undefer`, `orphans`, `changelog`, `sync`, `vcs-status`, `dep import`, `coordination status`, `gate`, `capacity`, `doctor --robot-triage` → **JSON**; (2) `--quiet` → **Quiet**; (3) `BR_OUTPUT_FORMAT`, else `TOON_DEFAULT_FORMAT`, parsed case-insensitively after trimming as `json`→JSON, `toon`→TOON, `text`/`plain`/`csv`→no decision here; (4) `--no-color`, or a non-empty `NO_COLOR`, or `TERM` equal to `dumb` ignoring case, or a stdout that is not a terminal → **Plain**; (5) otherwise **Rich**. Measured: `--json` beats `--quiet` (`br --no-db --quiet --json count` on `@fx=basic` prints `{"count":6}` and LF). | `src/output/context.rs:858`–`src/output/context.rs:875`, `src/output/context.rs:764`, `src/cli/mod.rs:1649`, `src/cli/mod.rs:1715` | list_json, list_quiet, count_json, blocked_json |
| S1.7 | Rich (TTY) and TOON output are outside the port's surface (PLAN §3 exclusions "Rich (TTY) output", "TOON output"). Every captured golden is produced with stdout piped and `NO_COLOR=1`, so step (4) of S1.6 always fires and the only modes the port reproduces are Plain, JSON and Quiet. | PLAN §3 exclusion rows; `src/output/context.rs:767` | every case; named examples: list_plain (Plain), list_json (JSON), list_quiet (Quiet) |
| S1.8 | Quiet mode suppresses the command's normal stdout entirely while leaving the exit code unchanged: `br --no-db list --quiet` on `@fx=basic` writes zero bytes to stdout and stderr and exits `0`, although the same command in Plain mode lists seven issues. | `src/output/context.rs:905`, `src/cli/commands/where.rs:41` | list_quiet |
| S1.9 | Quiet mode does not suppress errors. Measured: `br --no-db --quiet show proj-zzz` on `@fx=basic` writes `Error: Issue not found: proj-zzz` LF `Hint: Run 'br list' to see available issues.` LF to stderr and exits `3`. | `src/main.rs:1875` (`handle_error` ignores the mode except for JSON) | error_show_missing, (case to add: `quiet_error_show_missing`) |
| S1.10 | Both option-value spellings are accepted: `--flag value` (separate argv words) and `--flag=value` (one word). Measured: `br --no-db list --limit=1 --json` on `@fx=basic` produces exactly the bytes of `list_limit_one`. | clap derive defaults, `src/cli/mod.rs:1885` | list_limit_one, (case to add: `list_limit_equals` — `["list", "--limit=1", "--json"]`) |
| S1.11 | Option names are never abbreviated. Measured: `br --no-db list --li 1` fails with `error: unexpected argument '--li' found` LF LF `  tip: a similar argument exists: '--limit'` LF LF `Usage: br list --limit <LIMIT>` LF LF `For more information, try '--help'.` LF on stderr, exit `2`. The usage line names the *suggested* option, not the one that was typed. | clap's behavior, observed by running the original through the sandbox (re-measured 2026-09-20: the seven stderr lines quoted, exit `2`, stdout empty); no golden holds these bytes yet. Source side: `src/cli/mod.rs:692` (the `#[command(name = "br", …)]` attribute sets no long-option inference, so clap's default of exact names applies), `src/cli/mod.rs:1885`–`src/cli/mod.rs:1886` (`--limit`, the option clap suggests) | (case to add: `usage_abbrev_flag` — `["list", "--li", "1"]`) |
| S1.12 | A flag whose action is "set true" is refused when it appears twice. Golden `usage_json_twice` (`["list","--json","--json"]`): stderr is `error: the argument '--json' cannot be used multiple times` LF LF `Usage: br list [OPTIONS]` LF LF `For more information, try '--help'.` LF, stdout empty, exit `2`. | `src/cli/mod.rs:707` | usage_json_twice |
| S1.13 | A repeatable option accumulates instead of refusing. Measured on `@fx=basic`: `list -p 1 -p 2 --json` returns the union of priorities 1 and 2 (four issues), and `list --status open --status closed --json` returns six issues. | `src/cli/mod.rs:1830`, `src/cli/mod.rs:1858` | list_priority_one, list_status_open, list_label_two_and, list_label_any |
| S1.14 | A literal `--` ends option parsing; the remaining words become positionals. Measured: `br --no-db show -- proj-170` on `@fx=basic` is byte-identical to `show_plain` (amended 2026-09-20: the first text named `proj-mta`, which is not the issue `show_plain` shows). The tip in S1.19 advertises this form. | clap's behavior, observed: `goldens/usage_unexpected_flag.err:3` (the tip `use '-- --priority'` that advertises the form); `src/cli/mod.rs:1960`–`src/cli/mod.rs:1961` (`ShowArgs::ids`, the positional list that receives the words after `--`). Re-measured through the sandbox 2026-09-20: `show -- proj-mta` is byte-identical to `show proj-mta`, and `show -- proj-170` is byte-identical to `goldens/show_plain.out` — the argv of show_plain (`goldens/cases.tsv:85`) names `proj-170`, not `proj-mta`, so the clause's "same panel as `show_plain`" holds only with `proj-170` | show_plain, (case to add: `show_double_dash` — `["@fx=basic", "show", "--", "proj-170"]`, expected bytes = show_plain) |
| S1.15 | An unknown top-level option is a parse failure, exit `2`, stderr only, stdout empty. Golden `usage_unknown_flag` (argv `["--no-such-flag"]`, invoked as `br --no-db --no-such-flag`): `error: unexpected argument '--no-such-flag' found` LF LF `  tip: a similar argument exists: '--no-auto-flush'` LF LF `Usage: br --no-db --no-auto-flush <COMMAND>` LF LF `For more information, try '--help'.` LF. The usage line lists the option already consumed (`--no-db`) followed by the suggested option, which is an oddity, not a typo (hand-over to S10). | clap's behavior, observed: `goldens/usage_unknown_flag.err:1` (error line), `goldens/usage_unknown_flag.err:3` (tip), `goldens/usage_unknown_flag.err:5` (usage line), `goldens/usage_unknown_flag.err:7` (help pointer); `goldens/usage_unknown_flag.exit:1` is `2`; `goldens/usage_unknown_flag.out` is zero bytes. Source side: `src/main.rs:35` (`Cli::parse()`), `src/cli/mod.rs:715`–`src/cli/mod.rs:716` (`--no-auto-flush`, the suggested global), `src/cli/mod.rs:732` (`--no-db`) | usage_unknown_flag |

| S1.19 | An option that is valid globally but not for the chosen command is refused with a quoting tip. Golden `usage_unexpected_flag` (argv `["show","--priority","1"]`): `error: unexpected argument '--priority' found` LF LF `  tip: to pass '--priority' as a value, use '-- --priority'` LF LF `Usage: br show [OPTIONS] [IDS]...` LF LF `For more information, try '--help'.` LF, exit `2`. | `src/cli/mod.rs:1958` (`ShowArgs` has no priority option) | usage_unexpected_flag |
| S1.20 | An option that takes a value but is given none at the end of argv is refused without a usage block. Golden `usage_limit_noval` (argv `["list","--limit"]`): `error: a value is required for '--limit <LIMIT>' but none was supplied` LF LF `For more information, try '--help'.` LF, exit `2`. | `src/cli/mod.rs:1886` | usage_limit_noval |
| S1.21 | A value that does not parse into the option's type is refused by the parser, before any workspace is touched, with the Rust parse error appended after a colon and a space. Golden `usage_limit_bad` (argv `["list","--limit","x"]`): `error: invalid value 'x' for '--limit <LIMIT>': invalid digit found in string` LF LF `For more information, try '--help'.` LF, exit `2`. `--limit` is an unsigned pointer-width integer, so `+1` is accepted (measured: same bytes as `list_limit_one`), `-1` is not a value at all but an unknown short option (`error: unexpected argument '-1' found`), and `0` is accepted and means unlimited (S1.31). | `src/cli/mod.rs:1886`, probe RS-10 | usage_limit_bad, list_limit_one |
| S1.22 | A value outside a bounded integer type is refused with a range message. Measured: `br --no-db list --priority-min 300` gives `error: invalid value '300' for '--priority-min <PRIORITY_MIN>': 300 is not in 0..=255` LF LF `For more information, try '--help'.` LF, exit `2`, because `--priority-min` is an 8-bit unsigned value. The in-range refusal `9` is *not* a parse failure but a validation failure (S9.12). | `src/cli/mod.rs:1862` | (case to add: `usage_priority_min_range` — `["list","--priority-min","300"]`), list_priority_min_max |

| **`list`** | | | |
| S1.31 | `br list` takes no positionals. Its options are: `-s`/`--status <STATUS>` (repeatable, no comma splitting), `-t`/`--type <TYPE>` (repeatable), `--assignee <ASSIGNEE>`, `--unassigned`, `--id <ID>` (repeatable), `-l`/`--label <LABEL>` (repeatable, AND), `--label-any <LABEL_ANY>` (repeatable, OR), `-p`/`--priority <PRIORITY>` (repeatable), `--priority-min <PRIORITY_MIN>` (8-bit), `--priority-max <PRIORITY_MAX>` (8-bit), `--title-contains <TITLE_CONTAINS>`, `--desc-contains <DESC_CONTAINS>`, `--notes-contains <NOTES_CONTAINS>`, `-a`/`--all`, `--limit <LIMIT>` (pointer-width unsigned; default unlimited, and `0` also means unlimited), `--offset <OFFSET>` (default `0`), `--sort <SORT>`, `-r`/`--reverse`, `--deferred`, `--overdue`, `--long`, `--pretty`, `--tree`, `--wrap`, `--format <text|json|csv|toon>`, `--stats`, `--fields <FIELDS>`. Measured: `--limit 0` returns all seven issues of `@fx=basic` with `"limit":0` and `"has_more":false` in the envelope. | `src/cli/mod.rs:1827`–`src/cli/mod.rs:1943`, `src/cli/mod.rs:30` (`DEFAULT_LIST_LIMIT = 0`) | list_plain, list_json, list_json_again, list_all, list_all_json, list_status_open, list_status_closed, list_status_in_progress (the `-s` short form), list_type_bug, list_priority_one, list_priority_min_max, list_assignee, list_unassigned, list_label, list_label_two_and, list_label_any, list_title_contains, list_limit_one, list_limit_offset, list_sort_title, list_reverse, list_deferred, list_long, list_fields, list_id_filter |

An empty-string id is neither: it is `INVALID_ID` (S9.6). | S1.57 | `BEADS_DIR`, when set and not blank after trimming, replaces the upward walk: its value must itself be an existing `.beads` directory. Measured: `BEADS_DIR=/mnt/proj/.beads br --no-db count` on `@fx=basic` prints `6` LF and exits `0`; `BEADS_DIR=/mnt/nowhere` gives `Error: Configuration error: BEADS_DIR not found or not a .beads directory: /mnt/nowhere` LF on stderr, exit `7`. | `src/config/mod.rs:262`, `src/config/mod.rs:285`, `src/config/mod.rs:691` Captured (2026-09-24, OQ-023, harness mode `@env`): `env_beads_dir_where`, `env_beads_dir_list` (run from a subdirectory), `env_beads_dir_missing`. | (case to add: `env_beads_dir_ok`, `error_env_beads_dir_missing`), env_beads_dir_where, env_beads_dir_list, env_beads_dir_missing |
| S1.58 | With no `BEADS_DIR`, the workspace is found by walking up from the current directory and taking the first directory that contains a `.beads` subdirectory; the walk then follows up to ten `.beads` redirects. Reaching the filesystem root with no hit is `NOT_INITIALIZED` (S9.3). The sandbox always runs in `/mnt/proj`, so the resolved directory is `/mnt/proj/.beads` and the derived prefix is `proj`. | `src/config/mod.rs:299`–`src/config/mod.rs:320` | where_plain, error_no_workspace_list |

An unrecognized value is ignored entirely and the next rule of S1.6 applies. Measured on `@fx=basic`: `BR_OUTPUT_FORMAT=json br --no-db count` prints `{"count":6}` LF; `BR_OUTPUT_FORMAT=toon` prints `count: 6` LF; `BR_OUTPUT_FORMAT=bogus` prints `6` LF; `TOON_DEFAULT_FORMAT=json` prints `{"count":6}` LF. | `src/cli/mod.rs:1649`–`src/cli/mod.rs:1670` Captured (2026-09-24, OQ-086): the same bytes as `--json`, on `count`, `where` and an error envelope. | count_plain, count_json, (case to add: `env_output_format_json`, `env_output_format_bad`), env_output_format_count, env_output_format_where, env_output_format_error |
| S1.62 | `NO_COLOR` forces Plain mode when it is set to a non-empty value; `TERM` equal to `dumb` ignoring case does the same; a non-terminal stdout does the same. All three are tested with the same helper, so the answer cannot differ between commands. The sandbox sets `NO_COLOR=1` and pipes stdout, so both conditions hold in every golden. | `src/output/context.rs:764`–`src/output/context.rs:768` | every case; named examples: list_plain, show_plain, ready_plain, stats_plain (all Plain, none carrying an escape byte) |
| S1.63 | Error coloring is decided separately from output mode, against **stderr**: color is used only when `--no-color` is absent, `NO_COLOR` is empty/unset, `TERM` is not exactly `dumb`, and stderr is a terminal. In the sandbox stderr is a pipe, so every `Error:`/`Hint:` line is uncolored. When color is on, the prefixes are `\x1b[31mError:\x1b[0m ` and `\x1b[33mHint:\x1b[0m ` — out of the port's Plain/JSON surface. | `src/main.rs:1855`–`src/main.rs:1872`, `src/error/structured.rs:609`–`src/error/structured.rs:627` | every error case; named examples: error_show_missing, error_no_workspace_list, error_close_missing, error_update_missing (each `.err` is the bare `Error:` line then the bare `Hint:` line, no escape byte) |
| S1.64 | The acting actor for `create`, `q`, `update`, `close`, `reopen`, `defer`, `undefer`, `delete`, `dep` and `label` is resolved in this order: `--actor` (when non-blank), then the `actor` key of the merged configuration (project `.beads/config.yaml`, user config, and any `BD_ACTOR` environment variable, which enters configuration through the `BD_`-prefix rule), then `USER` trimmed, then the literal `unknown`. Measured: `actor_flag_create` with `--actor robot-7` stores `"created_by":"robot-7"`; without it the sandbox's `USER=tester` gives `"created_by":"tester"`. ### S1.68 The argument parser, measured (amendment 2026-09-20)

Every clause below was written from captured output, not from the source: the provenance column names
the golden line that shows it. Where a clause states something no golden shows, it says so and names the
case to add.

| S1.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1.68 | An unknown short option is refused as `error: unexpected argument '-z' found`. The message names the offending letter alone even when it sits inside a cluster (`-az` reports `-z`, although `-a` is a valid option of `list`). There is never a similarity tip, and the usage line is always the `[OPTIONS]` form of S1.70, whatever else is present. The `--` advice of S1.19 is printed exactly when the level takes positionals. A negative number is an unknown short option: `list --limit -1` reports `-1`. | `goldens/usage_unknown_short.err:1`, `goldens/usage_unknown_short_in_cluster.err:1`, `goldens/usage_value_negative.err:1` | usage_unknown_short, usage_unknown_short_in_cluster, usage_value_negative |
| S1.69 | Short options combine into one word. A letter whose option takes no value lets the cluster continue. A letter whose option takes a value ends the cluster: the rest of the word is its value (`-p1` is `--priority 1`; one leading `=` is dropped), and when nothing follows the letter the next word is its value (`-rs open` is `--reverse --status open`). A counted flag repeats inside one cluster (`-vv`). | `goldens/usage_short_combined.out:1`, `goldens/usage_short_attached_value.out:1`, `goldens/usage_verbose_counted.out:1` | usage_short_combined, usage_short_attached_value, usage_verbose_counted |
| S1.70 | The usage line of a refusal is `Usage: br`, then the words that lead to the level (`list`, `dep add`), then EITHER ` [OPTIONS]` OR the options present at this level, in first-seen order, each spelled as in S1.72; then every positional of the level (S1.71); then ` <COMMAND>` when a subcommand must follow. `[OPTIONS]` stands exactly when the refusal names no present argument. Refusals that name the present arguments: an unknown long option (the present ones, then the suggested one of S1.81); an unexpected value (S1.75: the present ones and the flag itself); an exclusion (S1.74: the present ones except the second of the pair); missing positionals (S1.24: the present ones, then the missing ones). Refusals that never name any: a repeated option (S1.12), an unknown short option (S1.68), an unexpected positional (S1.73), an unrecognized subcommand (S1.17) and a missing subcommand (S1.16). "Present" counts this level only: a global given before the command is not present at the command's level, and at the top level the contract's own `--no-db` is present (`Usage: br --no-db <COMMAND>`). A value whose option was refused or is still waiting is not present (`list --limit --zzqq` prints `Usage: br list [OPTIONS]`). The `[OPTIONS]` form of every in-scope command is captured by the 31 `usage_badflag_*` cases. | `goldens/usage_unknown_flag_top_plain.err:3`, `goldens/usage_unknown_flag_after_used.err:3`, `goldens/usage_value_pending_then_unknown.err:3`, `goldens/usage_badflag_dep_add.err:5` | usage_unknown_flag_top_plain, usage_unknown_flag_after_used, usage_value_pending_then_unknown, usage_badflag_create, usage_badflag_q, usage_badflag_update, usage_badflag_close, usage_badflag_reopen, usage_badflag_defer, usage_badflag_undefer, usage_badflag_delete, usage_badflag_show, usage_badflag_list, usage_badflag_search, usage_badflag_count, usage_badflag_stats, usage_badflag_ready, usage_badflag_blocked, usage_badflag_dep_add, usage_badflag_dep_remove, usage_badflag_dep_list, usage_badflag_dep_tree, usage_badflag_dep_cycles, usage_badflag_label_add, usage_badflag_label_remove, usage_badflag_label_list, usage_badflag_label_list_all, usage_badflag_label_rename, usage_badflag_comments_add, usage_badflag_comments_list, usage_badflag_epic_status, usage_badflag_epic_close_eligible, usage_badflag_where, usage_badflag_version |

| S1.77 | The integers the argument parser itself checks. Unsigned 64-bit (`--limit`, `--offset`, `--max-depth`, `--lock-timeout`): one optional `+`, then at least one ASCII digit and nothing else, else `invalid digit found in string` (a `-` is not a digit here); the empty string is S1.76; above 18446744073709551615 the reason is `number too large to fit in target type`; `--limit +1` is `--limit 1`. Ranged (`--priority-min` and `--priority-max` 0..=255; `--activity-hours` 0..=4294967295; `--estimate` -2147483648..=2147483647): the value is first read as a signed 64-bit integer (one optional `+` or `-`, digits; `invalid digit found in string`, `cannot parse integer from empty string`, `number too large to fit in target type`, `number too small to fit in target type`), then held to the range with the reason `N is not in LO..=HI`, N printed without `+` and without leading zeros. `1.5` is not an integer: `create T -e 1.5` is refused with `invalid digit found in string`; `create T -e 2147483648` with `2147483648 is not in -2147483648..=2147483647`. | S1.80 | Similarity between a typed word and a candidate is the Jaro similarity over code points. With lengths la and lb, the window radius is max(la, lb) / 2 − 1 (integer division, never below 0). Each code point of the typed word, left to right, is matched to the first not yet matched equal code point of the candidate whose index differs from its own by at most the radius. With m matches, and t half (integer division) the number of positions at which the two matched sequences, each read in its own order, differ, the similarity is (m/la + m/lb + (m − t)/m) / 3, and 0 when m is 0. A candidate is similar when its similarity is above 0.7. Measured: `lst` is similar to `lint` (2 matches: 0.7222) and to `list` (3 matches: 0.9167) and to no other top-level word. The original computes in 64-bit floating point; the port compares exact fractions, which can differ only for a similarity within one rounding step of 0.7 (OQ-106). | `goldens/usage_similar_subcommand.err:3` | usage_similar_subcommand |
| S1.81 | An unknown long option is compared (S1.80) with every long name the level answers to, in declaration order: its own options with their aliases, the twelve globals, `help`, and at the top level `version`. A suggestion replaces the `--` advice of S1.19. The name compared is the text between `--` and the first `=`. Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-107): `list --lon` suggests `--long` (`usage_similar_lon`). | `goldens/usage_similar_flag_in_cmd.err:3`, `goldens/usage_unknown_flag.err:3`, `goldens/usage_unknown_flag_used_positional.err:3` | usage_similar_flag_in_cmd, usage_unknown_flag, usage_unknown_flag_used_positional, usage_similar_lon |

A value given as the NEXT word (`--limit x`) is judged only when the following argument is read, or at the end of argv; a value attached with `=` or inside a short cluster is judged at once. When the following argument is itself unknown (an unknown long or short option, or a word the level has no place for), that argument's refusal wins and the value's refusal is never reported: `list --limit --zzqq` reports `--zzqq`, and `list --limit -1` reports `-1`. ## S2. Inputs

<!-- from docs/spec-parts/S2_S5a.md -->

### S2.A Workspace discovery under `--no-db`

| S2.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S2.1 | Every in-scope command resolves the workspace directory before anything else, and `--no-db` does not change the resolution — it only changes what is opened afterwards. The order is: (1) `--db <path>` when that path has a `.beads` or `_beads` component, that component being the workspace; (2) the first non-empty of the environment variables `BD_DB`, `BD_DATABASE`, `BEADS_DB`, in that order, treated the same way; (3) the environment variable `BEADS_DIR` when it does not trim to empty; (4) the upward walk of S2.2. Steps 1–3 short-circuit the walk entirely. No `BR_`-prefixed variable takes part in discovery | `src/config/mod.rs:502`, `:572`, `:679`, `:690`, `:284` | where_plain, where_json |
| S2.2 | The walk starts at the process working directory. At each level it tests `<dir>/.beads` first and `<dir>/_beads` second and accepts the first that is a directory; it then moves to the parent and repeats until the parent cannot be popped, that is, all the way to the filesystem root. There is no git-root stop, no `$HOME` stop and no required marker file: a bare `.beads/` directory with nothing in it is accepted. A working directory that cannot be read is an I/O failure, not a "not initialized" failure Measured outside the sandbox (2026-09-22; the sandbox always runs at the workspace root): from `myrepo/src/deep` the original finds `myrepo/.beads` and a `create` writes there, with `source_repo` `myrepo` and `source_repo_path` the directory that holds `.beads`; a bare `_beads` workspace is found the same way and `where` reports `<root>/_beads`; with no workspace up to the root the answer is S2.8. | `src/config/mod.rs:300`, `:309`, `:57` Captured (2026-09-24, OQ-103, harness mode `@cwd`): from `sub/` and `sub/dir/` the walk finds `/mnt/proj/.beads`, and a record created there carries `source_repo_path` `/mnt/proj`. | where_plain, create_min, list_plain, where_from_subdir, list_from_subdir, create_from_subdir |
| S2.3 | The only two recognized directory names are `.beads` and `_beads`, compared byte for byte against the final path component | `src/config/mod.rs:57` | where_plain |
| S2.4 | `BEADS_DIR` names the workspace directory directly. It is accepted on the single condition that the path is a directory; it is not required to be named `.beads` or `_beads` Measured outside the sandbox (2026-09-22): `BEADS_DIR=<root>/.beads` from an unrelated directory creates in that workspace with `source_repo_path` `<root>`, and `where` reports the `BEADS_DIR` path. | `src/config/mod.rs:690`, `:712` | (case to add: `env_beads_dir` — needs a ws-run mode that adds one environment variable to the cleared set) |
| S2.5 | A `redirect` file inside the resolved directory relocates the workspace. It must be a regular file of at most 4096 bytes; the whole content is UTF-8 and is trimmed as one string, never split into lines. Content that is empty after trimming is ignored and the original directory is kept. An absolute target is used as given; a relative target resolves against the `.beads` directory itself, so `.` stays put. Each hop is canonicalized and followed again, at most ten hops | `src/config/routing.rs:189`, `:204`, `:222`, `:256`, `:28` | (case to add: `redirect_basic` — needs a ws-run fixture sidecar `<fixture>.redirect`) |

The primary tree is derived by reading `gitdir:` from the `.git` file and then the `commondir` file beside it, with a fallback that strips a trailing `worktrees/<name>`; a common directory not named `.git` yields no relocation. This is the only way `metadata.json` participates in discovery | `src/config/mod.rs:385`, `:412`, `:435` | (case to add: `worktree_linked` — needs a ws-run fixture that plants a `.git` file) |

JSON mode writes the envelope of S5.21 to stdout with `"code": "NOT_INITIALIZED"`, `"message": "Beads not initialized: run 'br init' first"`, `"hint": "Run: br init"`, `"retryable": false`, `"context": null`. Read commands and `create` behave identically, because both sit in the auto-import command set whose startup failure is returned unchanged | `src/error/mod.rs:242`, `:444`, `src/error/structured.rs:512`, `:1347`, `src/main.rs:71`, `:1617` | error_no_workspace_list, error_no_workspace_list_json, error_no_workspace_create |

### S2.B The issue-id prefix

| S2.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S2.10 | Under `--no-db` the prefix that new ids use is resolved once at startup, in this order: (1) the merged configuration value under `issue_prefix`, `issue-prefix` or `prefix` — key lookup lower-cases and maps `_` to `-`, so `Issue_Prefix` matches — normalized by S2.13 and fatal when it normalizes to empty; (2) when a store file exists, the prefix of the first usable record in it (S2.11); (3) the sanitized, abbreviated name of the directory that contains `.beads` (S2.12); (4) the literal `br` Measured (2026-09-22): a nested key does not count (`sync:` then `  prefix: zz` leaves the directory rule in force, `prefix_config_nested`); with both `issue_prefix` and `prefix` configured the original chose between them at random, 5 of 8 runs `prefix` and 3 of 8 `issue_prefix` (`prefix_config_both` is UNSTABLE on the floor; DISC-010). | `src/config/mod.rs:5677`, `:5703`, `:5396` | where_plain, where_json, create_min, q_basic, prefix_config_create, prefix_config_create_json, prefix_config_nested, prefix_config_both, prefix_store_create, prefix_store_q |
| S2.11 | Scanning the store for a prefix walks the lines in file order and skips a blank line, a line that is not JSON, a record whose `status` is `tombstone`, a record without a string `id`, and an `id` that does not split. The prefix is everything before the **last** `-` in the id, so `document-intelligence-0sa` yields `document-intelligence`. The first line that survives wins | `src/config/mod.rs:5826`, `src/util/id.rs:356` | edge_precision_list_json, list_json, prefix_store_create, prefix_store_q, prefix_store_show_partial |
| S2.12 | Deriving a prefix from a directory name applies S2.13 first, then: a result of six characters or fewer is used unchanged; otherwise the name is split on every non-alphanumeric ASCII character and, when two or more non-empty segments remain and their initials form more than one character, those initials are the prefix (`beads_bend` → `bb`); otherwise the first three ASCII-alphanumeric characters are the prefix (`beadsrustproject` → `bea`); if even that is empty, the normalized string is used. Under the pinned environment the workspace is `/mnt/proj`, whose name `proj` is four characters, so the prefix is `proj` Measured outside the sandbox (a create in ten scratch workspaces, 2026-09-22; the sandbox workspace is always `proj`): `beads_bend` → `bb`, `beadsrustproject` → `bea`, `My Project!` → `myp`, `a` → `a`, `123abc` → `123abc`, `je_private_skills_repo` → `jpsr`, `Ünï-code` → `n-code`, `sixsix` → `sixsix`, `seven77` → `sev`, `x.y.z-w` → `xyzw`; `source_repo` is the directory name unchanged in every case. | `src/util/id.rs:692`, `:596` | where_plain, where_json, create_min_json, prefix_config_nested |
| S2.13 | Prefix sanitization trims the input, then lower-cases each character with the ASCII rule and keeps only `a`–`z`, `0`–`9`, `_`, `-`, `.`, `:` and `#`; every other character, including all non-ASCII, is dropped rather than replaced. At most 64 surviving characters are kept, then trailing `_ - . : #` are stripped. Leading digits and leading separators survive Measured: `issue-prefix: "Web App!"` mints `webapp-…` (`prefix_config_quoted`). | `src/util/id.rs:570`, `:9` | prefix_config_quoted |
| S2.14 | A configured `issue_prefix` that sanitizes to the empty string is fatal — it does not fall back to `br`: `issue_prefix "<raw value>" contains no usable ASCII letters, digits, or separators`, with the raw value rendered in Rust debug form, that is double-quoted with escapes Measured: the refusal is `CONFIG_ERROR`, exit 7, `"hint": null`, `"context": null`, prefixed `Configuration error: ` (`error_prefix_config_bad`, `error_prefix_config_bad_json`), and every command refuses it, reads included (`error_prefix_config_bad_list`), except `where` (S2.15). Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-024): `issue_prefix: '!!!'` refuses: `Configuration error: issue_prefix "!!!" contains no usable ASCII letters, digits, or separators`, exit 7. | `src/util/id.rs:615` | error_prefix_config_bad, error_prefix_config_bad_json, error_prefix_config_bad_list, error_create_empty_prefix |
| S2.15 | `br where`'s reported `prefix` uses a different rule from S2.10 and reports the raw configured value with no sanitization: configured value first; else a full scan of the store that reports a prefix only when every record agrees; else the private database's `config` table; else the prefix of the first stored row. Over the corpus the rules agree on `proj` Measured: `where` reports the configured value raw (`prefix: Web App!`, `prefix_config_quoted_where`; `!!!`, `prefix_config_bad_where`), and an unconfigured empty store reports no prefix at all: plain `where` has no `prefix:` line and `--json` no `prefix` member (`where_empty`, `where_empty_json`). | `src/cli/commands/where.rs:84`, `:184`, `:143` | where_plain, where_json, prefix_config_where, prefix_config_quoted_where, prefix_config_bad_where, prefix_store_where, where_empty, where_empty_json |

### S2.C `metadata.json`, `config.yaml` and the resolved paths

| S2.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S2.16 | `<beads_dir>/metadata.json` is optional; absent means all defaults and no message. The keys that change in-scope behavior are `database` (default `beads.db`; a blank value falls back to the default; an absolute value is used as is, otherwise it is joined to the cache directory) and `jsonl_export` (default `issues.jsonl`, honored only when it differs from the default and is not an excluded name). `backend` and `deletions_retention_days` are parsed and do not affect the in-scope path | `src/config/mod.rs:115`, `:62`, `:5873`, `:5905` | where_plain, where_json |
| S2.17 | A `metadata.json` that is not valid JSON aborts the command with `JSON error: <serde message>`; the file name is not part of the message | `src/config/mod.rs:115`, `src/error/mod.rs:255` | (case to add: `error_bad_metadata_json`) |
| S2.18 | `config.yaml` is read from four places and merged lowest to highest: `~/.beads/config.yaml`, then `~/.config/beads/config.yaml` (falling back to `~/.config/bd/config.yaml` when the first is absent), then `<beads_dir>/config.yaml`, then the environment layer; the command line is merged on top of all four. A missing `HOME` makes the two user layers empty without error. Each document is flattened to dotted keys and every key has `-` canonicalized to `_` | `src/config/mod.rs:6538`, `:6196`, `:6205`, `:6223`, `:7337`, `:7444` | where_plain, prefix_config_create, prefix_config_quoted, prefix_config_nested |

| S2.20 | The `config.yaml` keys that change in-scope behavior are `issue_prefix` (aliases `issue-prefix`, `prefix`; S2.10); `min_hash_length` 3, `max_hash_length` 8 and `max_collision_prob` 0.25 for id minting; `default_priority` 2 and `default_type` `task`; `display.color`; `sync.auto_flush` with its legacy inverted spelling `no-auto-flush`; `sync.auto_import`; `sync.history_enabled`; **`no_db`** with aliases `no-db` and `no.db`, so a project file can put a workspace into JSONL-only mode with no flag at all; `db` / `database`; `actor`; `claim_exclusive`; `lock_timeout` in milliseconds, default 30000; and `external_projects.<name>`. There is no `quiet` key and no output-format key — the output mode comes from flags and from `BR_OUTPUT_FORMAT`, `TOON_DEFAULT_FORMAT` and `NO_COLOR` only | `src/config/mod.rs:7111`, `:5545`, `:5560`, `:6866`, `:6890`, `:6930`, `:6072`, `:6147` | list_quiet, list_json |
| S2.21 | The store path is resolved, in order, from the environment variable `BEADS_JSONL`; an explicit non-default `metadata.json` `jsonl_export`; a sibling of an explicit database override; an existing `<beads_dir>/issues.jsonl` and then the legacy `<beads_dir>/beads.jsonl`; and finally the default `<beads_dir>/issues.jsonl`. Under the pinned environment the answer is always `/mnt/proj/.beads/issues.jsonl` | `src/config/mod.rs:5893`, `:5899`, `:192` | where_json, where_plain |
| S2.22 | The `last-touched` sidecar is never read at startup. It is read only by `show`, `close`, `update` and `reopen`, and only when the command was given no issue id, and by `delete` only to clear a pointer at a deleted issue. Its path is `<beads_dir>/last-touched` unless `BEADS_CACHE_DIR` is set and non-empty, in which case it is that directory's `last-touched`. A read takes the first line and trims it; a file larger than 4096 bytes, a non-UTF-8 file and an absent file all read as the empty string, with no message | `src/util/mod.rs:57`, `:112`, `:29`, `:47` Measured (2026-09-24, OQ-008, OQ-084, harness mode `@ls`): a read-only run creates no file in `.beads/`. | update_last_touched, scn_last_touched, ls_read_list, ls_read_show, ls_nostore_list |

### S2.D The `issues.jsonl` grammar as the loader accepts it

| S2.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S2.24 | The store is strict UTF-8 text, one JSON object per line. A line is everything up to and including `\n`; its content is trimmed of leading and trailing whitespace before parsing, so a `\r\n` terminator and stray spaces or tabs are tolerated. A line that is empty after trimming is skipped silently and **still advances the line counter**. A missing final newline on the last record is accepted. A file of zero bytes, and a file of nothing but blank lines, both load as an empty store with no message Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-101): a store that is not UTF-8 is `I/O error: stream did not contain valid UTF-8`, IO_ERROR, exit 8. | `src/sync/mod.rs:13823`, `:13828`, `:13833` | empty_list, empty_list_json, empty_ready, empty_ready_json, empty_blocked, empty_blocked_json, empty_stats_json, empty_count, empty_search, empty_dep_cycles, error_store_not_utf8, error_store_not_utf8_json |
| S2.25 | A leading UTF-8 byte-order mark is **not** stripped on the load path: U+FEFF is not whitespace, so it survives the trim of S2.24 and reaches the JSON parser. The result is the invalid-JSON refusal of S2.27 reading `Configuration error: Invalid JSON at line 1: expected value at line 1 column 1`. Only `br doctor`, which is out of scope, knows how to strip a BOM Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-019): as stated; the column is the BOM's first byte. | `src/sync/mod.rs:13833`, `src/cli/commands/doctor.rs:4964` | (case to add: `error_jsonl_bom` on a fixture whose first three bytes are `EF BB BF`), error_jsonl_bom, error_jsonl_bom_json |
| S2.26 | A git merge-conflict marker anywhere in the file refuses the whole load **before any line is parsed as JSON** and before any database work, exit code **7**, code `CONFIG_ERROR`. A marker is a line whose trimmed text starts with the seven characters `<<<<<<<` (type `Start`), starts with `=======` (type `Separator`), or starts with `>>>>>>>` (type `End`); no trailing space is required and the diff3 base marker `\|\|\|\|\|\|\|` is not detected. At most the first five markers are listed, in file order, one per line, each as `<store path>:<1-based line> <type>` followed by ` (<remainder of the marker line, trimmed>)` for `Start` and `End` — an empty remainder prints as ` ()` — and by nothing for `Separator`. The whole message is, for the `conflict` fixture, verbatim and newline-joined: `Configuration error: Merge conflict markers detected in /mnt/proj/.beads/issues.jsonl.` / `/mnt/proj/.beads/issues.jsonl:2 Start (HEAD)` / `/mnt/proj/.beads/issues.jsonl:4 Separator` / `/mnt/proj/.beads/issues.jsonl:6 End (branch)` / `Resolve conflicts before importing.`. In Plain mode the whole block goes to stderr behind a single `Error: ` prefix; in JSON mode it is one `"message"` string with `\n` escapes. Nothing is written and no partial result is printed | `src/sync/mod.rs:10291`, `:10334`, `:10357`, `:10381`, `:14630` | error_conflict_markers, error_conflict_markers_json |
| S2.27 | A line that is not valid JSON refuses the whole load, exit code **7**, code `CONFIG_ERROR`, message `Configuration error: Invalid JSON at line <n>: <serde_json message>`, where `<n>` is the 1-based line number in the file (blank lines included in the count) and the serde message reports its own line and column relative to that single trimmed line. For the `malformed` fixture, whose second line is the 40-character fragment `{"id":"proj-aab","title":"truncated line`, the message is exactly `Configuration error: Invalid JSON at line 2: EOF while parsing a string at line 1 column 40`. The load aborts: nothing is skipped, nothing is written, and the first, valid record is not reported Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-102): `trailing comma`, `expected `:``, `expected `,` or `}``, `key must be a string`, `expected ident` (at the first mismatching byte of a keyword), `invalid escape`, `control character (\u0000-\u001F) found while parsing a string`, `trailing characters`, `unexpected end of hex escape`, `EOF while parsing an object`, each at the column serde_json reports; a line that is an array fills the members in order, so `[1]` is `invalid type: integer `1`, expected a string`. Amended (2026-09-24, OQ-136): an invalid `\uXXXX` escape is reported at the column of the FOURTH byte after the `u` (serde_json takes the four bytes before it judges them: `\u00zz`, `\uzzzz` and `\u0z",` all report the same column), or as `EOF while parsing a string` at the end of the line when fewer than four bytes remain; a character that can begin no JSON token (an upper-case or non-ASCII letter, a raw control character) is judged where it stands, at its FIRST byte: `expected value` in value position, `key must be a string` in key position, and nothing after it is reported first; a keyword prefix that runs to the end of the text (`tru`) is `EOF while parsing a value` at the end. | `src/sync/mod.rs:13801` | error_malformed_jsonl, error_malformed_jsonl_json, error_jsonl_trailing_comma, error_jsonl_colon, error_jsonl_comma, error_jsonl_key, error_jsonl_ident, error_jsonl_escape, error_jsonl_ctrl, error_jsonl_trailing, error_jsonl_array, error_jsonl_int_id, error_jsonl_prio_word, error_jsonl_surrogate, error_jsonl_eof_object, error_load_u_escape_bad, error_load_dep_meta_stray |
| S2.28 | The members with no default are `id`, `title`, `created_at` and `updated_at`; every other member may be absent. An absent `status` reads as `open`, an absent `priority` as `2`, an absent `issue_type` as `task`, absent `labels` / `dependencies` / `comments` as empty, absent `ephemeral` / `pinned` / `is_template` as false, and every other absent member as "not set". A record missing one of the four required members is the same refusal as S2.27, carrying serde's own wording inside the frame: `Configuration error: Invalid JSON at line <n>: missing field \`title\` at line 1 column <c>` Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-025): `missing field `title` at line 1 column 138` for a record of 138 bytes (the column is the end of the record). Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-102): a value of the wrong type is `invalid type: <integer `n` or floating point `x` or string "s" or boolean `b` or null or sequence or map>, expected <a string or i32 or i64 (a comment id) or a boolean or a sequence or an RFC 3339 formatted date and time string>` at the value's last byte (a container: the byte before its bracket); an integer beyond i32 is `invalid value: integer `n`, expected i32`. | `src/model/mod.rs:462`, `:495`, `:645`, `src/sync/mod.rs:13801` | edge_precision_list_json, (case to add: `error_jsonl_missing_title`), error_jsonl_missing_title, error_jsonl_type_created_int, error_jsonl_type_created_word, error_jsonl_type_labels_str, error_jsonl_type_labels_int, error_jsonl_type_ephemeral_str, error_jsonl_type_deps_int, error_jsonl_type_comments_obj, error_jsonl_type_priority_float, error_jsonl_type_priority_null, error_jsonl_type_desc_int, error_jsonl_type_desc_bool, error_jsonl_type_estimate_str, error_jsonl_type_id_null, error_jsonl_type_title_arr, error_jsonl_type_title_obj, error_jsonl_type_priority_big, error_jsonl_type_priority_neg_float, error_jsonl_type_dep_bad_member, error_jsonl_type_comment_bad, error_jsonl_type_status_int |
| S2.29 | Unknown object members are accepted silently and are dropped: neither the issue, the dependency nor the comment record declares `deny_unknown_fields`, and the write-back of S5 re-serializes only the known members. The `precision` fixture's `"unknown_field":"is it kept?"` survives a read — `show` echoes the raw line (S5.30) — and disappears the first time any command rewrites the store | `src/model/mod.rs:461`, `:894`, `:930` | edge_precision_show_json, edge_precision_rewrite |
| S2.30 | A record that parses but fails the validation rules of S2.41–S2.54 refuses the load with `Validation failed for issue <id> at line <line>: <messages joined by a comma and a space>`, exit code 4 Corrected (2026-09-23, OQ-025): the refusal is `Configuration error: Validation failed for issue <id> at line <n>: …`, CONFIG_ERROR, exit **7** (not 4), `context` null. Every message of the record is joined by `, ` in this measured order: id; title (empty, over 500, NUL); the NUL rule of description, design, acceptance_criteria, prerequisites, notes; status then issue_type (over 50, NUL); assignee, owner, created_by (over 200, NUL); external_ref (over 200 bytes, white space, NUL); source_system; labels (over 64, then each label by index in BYTE ORDER of the sorted labels); priority; estimated_minutes; closed_at before created_at. `show` does not validate (`error_load_bad_id_show`). The port applies it on the import path only. | `src/sync/mod.rs:13806`, `src/validation/mod.rs:77` | (case to add: `error_jsonl_bad_id_format` on a fixture whose `id` has no hyphen), error_jsonl_bad_id_format, error_load_title_blank, error_load_title_long, error_load_priority, error_load_estimate_neg, error_load_estimate_big, error_load_closed_before, error_load_assignee_long, error_load_extref_space, error_load_extref_long, error_load_nul_title, error_load_status_long, error_load_labels_many, error_load_label_bad, error_load_two_rules, error_load_two_rules_json, error_load_all_a, error_load_all_b |
| S2.31 | `status` is read as a string and matched case-insensitively against `open`, `in_progress`, `inprogress`, `blocked`, `deferred`, `draft`, `closed`, `tombstone`, `pinned`; `inprogress` is an alias that is written back as `in_progress`. Any other spelling becomes a custom status, **lower-cased**, so `QaReview` loads as `qareview`. A second, later fold then maps the custom values `done`, `complete`, `completed`, `finished` and `resolved` (trimmed, ASCII-lower-cased) to `closed`, which is why such a record also picks up a `closed_at` under S2.36 Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-022): `InProgress`, `inprogress` → `in_progress`; `QaReview` → `qareview`; `done` → `closed` with a `closed_at`; the write-back keeps them. | `src/model/mod.rs:76`, `:87`, `src/sync/mod.rs:13726` | list_status_in_progress, (case to add: `edge_status_spellings` on a fixture carrying `InProgress`, `inprogress`, `QaReview` and `done`), edge_status_spellings, edge_status_spellings_rewrite |
| S2.32 | `issue_type` is read the same way and matched case-insensitively against `task`, `bug`, `feature`, `epic`, `chore`, `docs`, `question`; any other spelling becomes a custom type, lower-cased (`Odd_Type` → `odd_type`) | `src/model/mod.rs:209`, `:220` | list_type_bug, create_custom_type |
| S2.33 | A dependency's `type` is read the same way and matched case-insensitively against `blocks`, `parent-child`, `conditional-blocks`, `waits-for`, `related`, `discovered-from`, `replies-to`, `relates-to`, `duplicates`, `supersedes`, `caused-by`; any other spelling becomes a custom type, lower-cased (`Odd-Dep` → `odd-dep`). A custom type is then repaired once: every `_` is replaced by `-` and the result re-read, and the repair is adopted only when it lands on one of the eleven known types, so `parent_child` becomes `parent-child` while `odd_dep` stays `odd_dep`. Only the first four types are blocking. Note the asymmetry the goldens show — a stored custom dependency type loads without complaint while `dep add -t <custom>` refuses (S9) | `src/model/mod.rs:288`, `:337`, `src/sync/mod.rs:13662` | dep_list_json, dep_add_custom_type |
| S2.34 | `priority` in the store is a bare JSON integer read with no range check at load time; the 0–4 range is enforced by validation (S2.43) and by the argument parser. The command-line forms accept surrounding whitespace and an optional `P` or `p` prefix — the token is upper-cased before the prefix is stripped — so `P3`, `p3` and `3` are one value | `src/model/mod.rs:148`, `:174` | list_priority_one, create_priority_p_form, error_create_bad_priority |
| S2.35 | Every timestamp member (`created_at`, `updated_at`, `closed_at`, `due_at`, `defer_until`, `deleted_at`, `compacted_at`, a dependency's `created_at`, a comment's `created_at`) is an RFC 3339 string with a mandatory zone. Fraction digits of any width are accepted (`…05Z`, `…05.1Z`, `…05.100Z`, `…05.000000001Z`) and a numeric offset is accepted and converted to UTC on load, so `2026-01-01T12:00:04+02:00` is the instant `2026-01-01T10:00:04Z`. The value is kept as an instant, so what is written back is the canonical form of S5.12, not the source text Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-102): a timestamp string that does not parse is reported at its closing quote in chrono's words: `premature end of input` (it stops early), `input contains invalid characters`, `trailing input`, `input is out of range` (a well-formed spelling with month 13, February 30, hour 25). | `src/model/mod.rs:519`, `:526`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:18607` | edge_precision_list_json, edge_precision_rewrite, edge_precision_ready, edge_precision_sort_created, error_jsonl_ts_short, error_jsonl_ts_month, error_jsonl_ts_trail, error_jsonl_ts_feb30, error_jsonl_ts_nozone, error_jsonl_ts_hour, error_jsonl_ts_empty, error_jsonl_ts_slash |
| S2.36 | Loading applies these repairs, in this order, to every record, before validation and before the record is usable: `labels` is sorted ascending by byte order and de-duplicated; the dependency-type repair of S2.33; duplicate dependencies sharing `(issue_id, depends_on_id, type)` collapse to the one with the greatest `created_at`, first occurrence winning a tie, with the survivors left in file order; byte-identical duplicate comments collapse to their first occurrence; the closed-status fold of S2.31; an `id` containing `-wisp-` sets `ephemeral` to true; a `closed` or `tombstone` record with no `closed_at` gains `closed_at` equal to its `updated_at`, and any other record's `closed_at` is discarded; `external_ref` that is whitespace-only becomes "not set" and otherwise is trimmed — it is the only string member that is trimmed; and an `updated_at` earlier than `created_at` is raised to `created_at`. The last two repairs mean the corresponding validation rules (S2.44, S2.46) can never fire from a loaded record | `src/sync/mod.rs:13652`, `:13656`, `:13676`, `:13708`, `:13726`, `:13737`, `:13742`, `:13752`, `:13764` | edge_precision_rewrite, show_closed, create_ephemeral |
| S2.37 | A dependency's `metadata` that is absent, or is a string that is empty or only whitespace, loads as "not set"; any other string is kept verbatim. The `basic` fixture's `"metadata":"{}"` is a non-blank string and survives unchanged | `src/model/mod.rs:46`, `:919` | dep_list_json, update_status_json |
| S2.38 | An optional string member present as `""` loads as an empty string, not as "not set" — `show`, which reads the file directly, echoes `"description":""` and `"assignee":""` back. What survives a *write-back* is a different question, answered by S5.9 | `src/model/mod.rs:474`, `:507` | edge_precision_show_json, edge_precision_rewrite |
| S2.39 | `labels` is read as the array in the file for the direct-read path of `show`, duplicates and file order intact (`["zeta","alpha","alpha"]`), while the loaded model sorts and de-duplicates under S2.36. The two paths therefore disagree on the same record | `src/model/mod.rs:645`, `src/cli/commands/show.rs:893`, `src/sync/mod.rs:13656` | edge_precision_show_json, edge_precision_list_json |
| S2.40 | Two lines carrying the same `id` refuse the load with `Duplicate issue id '<id>' in <absolute store path> at line <n>`, naming the **second** occurrence's 1-based line number, before any write. The `show` fast path raises the identical text Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-021): the text is prefixed `Configuration error: `, code CONFIG_ERROR, exit 7; `list`, `count` and `show` refuse alike. | `src/sync/mod.rs:13863`, `src/cli/commands/show.rs:823` | (case to add: `error_jsonl_duplicate_id` on a fixture with two `proj-aaa` lines), error_jsonl_duplicate_id, error_jsonl_duplicate_id_show, error_load_empty_optionals_dup |
| S2.41 | A record whose `id` prefix does not match the workspace prefix would normally refuse with `Prefix mismatch at line <n>: expected '<prefix>', found issue '<id>'`, tombstones exempt — but the `--no-db` open sets `skip_prefix_validation`, which makes the check unconditionally false, so under this port's contract a foreign-prefix id round-trips untouched and is never renamed | `src/sync/mod.rs:13854`, `src/config/mod.rs:5419`, `:5761` | edge_precision_list_json, list_json |
| S2.42 | A record with `"status":"tombstone"` loads as a row, counts in `stats` as a tombstone, is skipped by the prefix scan of S2.11 and is written back (S5.16). When a tombstone is already present locally under the same id, an incoming record for that id is skipped with the reason `Tombstone protection: <id>`. A record with `"ephemeral":true`, and any record whose `id` contains `-wisp-` (which S2.36 turns into an ephemeral), is parsed and counted and takes part in the duplicate check, then is dropped before any storage work — it is never queryable and never written | `src/sync/mod.rs:13617`, `:14113`, `:14288`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:15005` | empty_stats_json, stats_json, create_ephemeral, delete_cascade |

### S2.E Validation limits and their verbatim messages

Each message below is a validation failure: code `VALIDATION_FAILED`, exit code **4**, Plain mode on stderr as `Error: Validation failed: <field>: <message>` with a trailing newline, JSON mode as the envelope of S5.21. Several failures on one record are joined by `, `; the goldens exercise one at a time.

| S2.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S2.43 | `title`: a title that is empty or only whitespace produces `title: cannot be empty`; a title of more than 500 **characters** (Unicode scalar values, not bytes) produces `title: exceeds 500 characters`. Exactly 500 characters is accepted | `src/validation/mod.rs:21`, `:158`, `:162` | error_create_empty_title, error_create_blank_title, error_create_long_title, edge_create_max_title |
| S2.44 | `id`: an empty id produces `id: cannot be empty`; an over-long id produces `id: exceeds <max> characters`; an id that is not `prefix-hash` shaped produces `id: invalid format (expected prefix-hash)` Measured (2026-09-23): the maximum is 105 characters, and an over-long id also reports the format (`id: exceeds 105 characters, id: invalid format …`); refused shapes: `proj-`, `-aab`, `PROJ-AAB`, `proj-a_b`, `proj-a b`, `proj-aab.x`; accepted: hyphenated lower-case words (`proj-my-thing-89a`) and numeric dotted children (`proj-uly.1`). Bounds (2026-09-23, `edge_load_id_*`): at least two hyphen-separated lower-case alphanumeric words before the first `.` (any number of hyphens: `a-b-c-d-e-f-g-h-i-j-k`, `123-abc`), the LAST word at most 40 characters (40 loads, 41 is refused; a 50-character middle word is fine), everything before the last hyphen at most 64 characters (64 loads, 65 is refused), and digits after each `.` (not counted in the 40). Corrected (2026-09-23, found by running the port on real stores): the rule splits at the LAST hyphen; the part before it allows lower-case letters, digits, `_`, `.` and `-` (`my_proj-13w`, `_proj-13w`, `a.b-13w`, `p_1-13w`, `franken_agent_detection-13w` load; `MyProj-13w` and `my_proj-a_b` are refused). | `src/validation/mod.rs:78`, `:82`, `:87` | (case to add: `error_jsonl_bad_id_format`), error_load_id_empty, error_load_id_long, error_load_id_trailing, error_load_id_leading, error_load_id_upper, error_load_id_underscore, error_load_id_space, error_load_id_child_word, error_load_id_upper_show, error_load_bad_id_show, edge_load_id_hash8, edge_load_id_hash9, edge_load_id_hash12, edge_load_id_hash16, edge_load_id_hash17, edge_load_id_hash32, edge_load_id_hash33, edge_load_id_hash64, edge_load_id_hash65, edge_load_id_hash100, edge_load_id_slug_segments, edge_load_id_prefix_long, edge_load_id_many_hyphens, edge_load_id_digits_prefix, edge_load_id_hash40, edge_load_id_hash48, edge_load_id_hash49, edge_load_id_hash50, edge_load_id_hash51, edge_load_id_hash52, edge_load_id_hash56, edge_load_id_hash60, edge_load_id_hash63, edge_load_id_mid_long, edge_load_id_last_long, edge_load_id_slug57, edge_load_id_slug_child, edge_load_id_hash41, edge_load_id_hash42, edge_load_id_hash43, edge_load_id_hash44, edge_load_id_hash45, edge_load_id_hash46, edge_load_id_hash47, edge_load_id_head64, edge_load_id_head65, edge_load_id_head80, edge_load_id_head100, edge_load_id_hyphens11, edge_load_id_prefix_underscore, edge_load_id_prefix_real, edge_load_id_prefix_upper, edge_load_id_prefix_dot, edge_load_id_prefix_underscore_lead, edge_load_id_hash_underscore_prefix, edge_load_id_prefix_underscore_child, edge_load_id_prefix_digits_underscore |
| S2.45 | `priority`: a value outside 0–4 produces `priority: must be 0-4`. The argument parser reports the narrower `Priority must be 0-4, got: <value>` with code `INVALID_PRIORITY` before validation is reached | `src/validation/mod.rs:98`, `src/model/mod.rs:183` | error_create_bad_priority, error_create_bad_priority_json, error_update_bad_priority |
| S2.46 | `updated_at` earlier than `created_at` produces `updated_at: cannot be before created_at`; S2.36 raises the value first, so only a command-supplied timestamp can reach this rule | `src/validation/mod.rs:103` | (case to add: `error_jsonl_updated_before_created`) |
| S2.47 | `estimated_minutes`: a negative value produces `estimated_minutes: cannot be negative`; a value above 525960 produces `estimated_minutes: exceeds maximum (525960 minutes / ~1 year)` | `src/validation/mod.rs:113`, `:118` | create_full_json, (case to add: `error_create_estimate_too_big`) |
| S2.48 | `closed_at` invariants: a `closed` issue with no `closed_at` produces `closed_at: closed issues must set closed_at`; a `closed_at` on an issue that is neither `closed` nor `tombstone` produces `closed_at: only closed or tombstone issues may set closed_at`; a `closed_at` earlier than `created_at` produces `closed_at: cannot be before created_at`. S2.36 repairs the first two before they can fire on a loaded record | `src/validation/mod.rs:127`, `:134`, `:141` | update_status_closed, show_closed, close_reason_json |
| S2.49 | `assignee`, `owner`, `created_by` and `source_system`: more than 200 characters produces `<field>: exceeds 200 characters` | `src/validation/mod.rs:22`, `:185` | actor_flag_create, (case to add: `error_create_long_assignee`) |
| S2.50 | `external_ref`: more than 200 **bytes** produces `external_ref: exceeds 200 characters`; any Unicode whitespace character in the value produces `external_ref: cannot contain whitespace` | `src/validation/mod.rs:210` | create_full_json, (case to add: `error_create_external_ref_space`) |
| S2.51 | A NUL byte in `title`, `description`, `design`, `acceptance_criteria`, `prerequisites`, `notes`, `status`, `issue_type`, `assignee`, `owner`, `created_by`, `external_ref`, `source_system` or a comment body produces `<field>: cannot contain NUL bytes`. The long-text members have no length limit at all — only the NUL rule applies to them | `src/validation/mod.rs:230`, `:167` | create_unicode, (case to add: `error_create_nul_description`) |
| S2.52 | A custom `status` longer than 50 characters produces `status: custom status exceeds 50 characters`; a custom `issue_type` longer than 50 characters produces `issue_type: custom issue type exceeds 50 characters` | `src/validation/mod.rs:23`, `:253`, `:266` | create_custom_type, (case to add: `error_create_long_custom_type`) |
| S2.53 | `labels`: more than 64 labels on one issue produces `labels: exceeds 64 labels`; an individual label that fails S2.54 produces `labels: label at index <i>: <label message>` with a 0-based index | `src/validation/mod.rs:24`, `:275`, `:283` | (case to add: `error_create_65_labels`) |
| S2.54 | A single label must be non-empty (`label: cannot be empty`), at most 50 bytes (`label: exceeds 50 characters`), and built only from ASCII alphanumerics, `-`, `_` and `:`; anything else produces `label: invalid characters (only alphanumeric, hyphen, underscore, colon allowed)`. The `create` and `label add` paths report that message directly, without the `labels: label at index …` wrapper | `src/validation/mod.rs:425`, `:429`, `:433` | error_create_bad_label, error_label_add_invalid, label_add, label_rename |
| S2.55 | A comment must have a positive `id` (`id: must be positive`), a non-blank `issue_id` (`issue_id: cannot be empty`), a non-blank body (`content: cannot be empty`), a non-blank `author` (`author: cannot be empty`) and an `author` of at most 200 bytes (`author: exceeds 200 characters`). Comment bodies have no length limit. On import a comment whose `id` is not positive is given a fresh one, and a comment whose `id` collides with an existing row's is re-minted rather than refused; two comments on the *same* issue carrying one id are refused with `duplicate import comment id <n>`, and a comment whose `issue_id` disagrees with its record's id is refused with `comment issue_id '<a>' does not match import issue '<b>'` | `src/validation/mod.rs:462`, `:466`, `:470`, `:478`, `:482`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:20794`, `:20942` | error_comments_add_empty, comments_add, comments_add_author, comments_list_json |
| S2.56 | A dependency must not point at itself (`depends_on_id: issue cannot depend on itself`), both ends must exist (`issue_id: issue not found`, `depends_on_id: dependency target not found`), a blocking type must not close a cycle (`depends_on_id: would create dependency cycle`), and the edge must not already exist (`depends_on_id: dependency already exists`). The `dep add` command reports its own wording for the cycle and duplicate cases (S9) Resolved (2026-09-23, OQ-094): on `dep add` the missing target fails target resolution first (exit 3, `error_dep_add_missing`); this wording is not reached on that path. | `src/validation/mod.rs:373`, `:381`, `:386`, `:396`, `:404` | error_dep_add_self, error_dep_add_missing, error_dep_add_cycle, error_dep_add_cycle_json, dep_add_duplicate, dep_add_external |
| S2.57 | **The import check.** Every command but `show` imports the store, and the import refuses a record that holds the empty string `""` in any of `agent_context`, `bypass_reason`, `source_repo`, `source_repo_path`: `Sync conflict: Import semantic verification failed: issue <id> does not match its normalized JSONL payload (differing fields: <members, alphabetical, joined by `, `>); rolling back the import`, code `SYNC_CONFLICT`, exit 6, `hint` null, `retryable` false, `context` `{"message": <the message without its `Sync conflict: ` prefix>}`. It runs after every line has loaded and validated; the first such record in file order is named. `""` in any other optional string loads and drops on write-back (S5.9). `show` reads the file directly and prints the `""` members back. Amended (2026-09-24, OQ-131): `dependencies` is a differing member too, when any of the record's dependencies names a target that is neither `external:…` nor the id of a stored record (any dependency type; a tombstone is stored; a target further down the file is stored), and it sorts among the others (`differing fields: dependencies, source_repo`). `show` skips this check as well. "The first such record in file order" holds only when one record fails: with several, the original names one at random (DISC-012), and the port names the first in file order. Amended (2026-09-24, round 12, OQ-134): an ephemeral record (S2.42, including a `-wisp-` id) is dropped before the import's checks: neither S2.57 nor S2.58 applies to it, and it is not a stored target (`r12_dep_to_ephemeral_count`, `r12_dep_from_ephemeral_count`); a tombstone and a closed record are stored targets; the dangling rule holds for a custom dependency type and for a tombstone's own edges | `goldens/edge_empty_optionals_list.err:1`, `goldens/edge_empty_optionals_list_json.out:1`, `goldens/edge_empty_source_repo.err:1` (measured 2026-09-23, OQ-018, OQ-088); `goldens/error_load_dep_dangling.err:1`, `goldens/error_load_dep_dangling_empty.err:1` (measured 2026-09-24, OQ-131) | edge_empty_optionals_list, edge_empty_optionals_list_json, edge_empty_optionals_rewrite, edge_empty_optionals_rewrite_json, edge_empty_optionals_show, edge_empty_source_repo, error_load_dep_dangling, error_load_dep_dangling_json, error_load_dep_dangling_related, error_load_dep_dangling_empty, edge_load_dep_external, edge_load_dep_dangling_show, r12_load_dangle_both_json, r12_load_dangle_tombstone, r12_load_dangle_custom, r12_dep_tomb_target_ready_json, r12_dep_to_ephemeral_count, r12_dep_from_ephemeral_count, r12_show_mix_list_json |
| S2.58 | **The import's dependency edges.** Before S2.57's check, the import walks the records in file order and, within a record, its dependencies in order; the first dependency that breaks a rule refuses the whole load. (1) A dependency whose `issue_id` is not the record's `id`: `Validation failed: dependency.issue_id: dependency issue_id '<issue_id>' does not match import issue '<id>'`, code `VALIDATION_FAILED`, exit 4, `hint` null, `retryable` true, `context` `{"field": "dependency.issue_id", "reason": <the text after the second `: `>}`. (2) Otherwise, a dependency whose target is the record's own `id`: `Issue cannot depend on itself: <id>`, `Hint: An issue cannot depend on itself`, code `SELF_DEPENDENCY`, exit 5, `retryable` false, `context` `{"id": <id>}`. (3) Otherwise, a `parent-child` dependency (after the type repair of S2.33) whose target starts with `external:`: `Validation failed: depends_on_id: parent-child dependencies must link local issues: <target>`, code `VALIDATION_FAILED`, exit 4, `retryable` true, `context` `{"field": "depends_on_id", "reason": <the text after the second `: `>}` (the words of S4.357; added 2026-09-24, round 12, OQ-134). Rule (1) is checked before rule (2) on the same dependency (`dependency issue_id 'proj-b1'` on an edge `proj-b1 → proj-b1` of record `proj-a1`). A record that breaks either rule is named even when an earlier record fails S2.57 (`error_load_dep_dangling_then_self`). `show` checks neither Amended (2026-09-24, OQ-136): between rules (2) and (3), a dependency whose `metadata` is set (S2.37: absent or blank is not set) and is not one JSON value refuses the load: `Validation failed: dependencies[<k>].metadata: dependency metadata must be valid JSON for issue <id> -> <target> (type=<type>); found <text>: <serde message> at line <l> column <c>`, code `VALIDATION_FAILED`, exit 4, `hint` null, `retryable` true, `context` `{"field": "dependencies[<k>].metadata", "reason": <the text after the second `: `>}`. `<k>` is the edge's place in the record's array AS STORED, from 0 (not the sorted order of S5.14); `<type>` is the repaired type (S2.33: `BLOCKS` → `blocks`); `<text>` and the position are those of S4.350. Any JSON value passes (`[1]`, `7`, `null`, ` {} `). The metadata rule precedes a later edge's self-dependency and the `external:` rule on the same edge; an ephemeral record is not checked (S2.42); `show` skips it with the rest of the import. Every other command that reads the store (queries and mutations alike) is refused. | `goldens/error_load_dep_self.err:1`, `goldens/error_load_dep_issue_id.err:1`, `goldens/error_load_dep_other_then_self.err:1`, `goldens/error_load_dep_dangling_then_self.err:1` (measured 2026-09-24, OQ-131) | error_load_dep_self, error_load_dep_self_json, edge_load_dep_self_show, error_load_dep_issue_id, error_load_dep_issue_id_json, error_load_dep_other_then_self, error_load_dep_dangling_then_self, r12_load_dangle_self, r12_load_self_other_json, r12_load_dangle_then_other, r12_load_self_related, r12_ext_pc_load_json, r12_ext_pc_then_self, error_load_dep_meta_count, error_load_dep_meta_list_json, edge_load_dep_meta_show, error_load_dep_meta_update, error_load_dep_meta_before_self, error_load_dep_meta_before_ext, error_load_dep_meta_lines, error_load_dep_meta_lines_json, error_load_dep_meta_stray |


## S3. Data model

<!-- from docs/spec-parts/ARCHITECT_S3_S11_S12.md -->

Types and their invariants as the outputs reveal them. Numeric widths feed
`docs/NUMERIC_PLAN.md`; the exact bytes of each record are S5's.

| S3.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S3.1 | An **issue** is a record of 44 serialized members, always in this order: `id`, `title`, `description`, `design`, `acceptance_criteria`, `prerequisites`, `notes`, `status`, `priority`, `issue_type`, `assignee`, `owner`, `estimated_minutes`, `created_at`, `created_by`, `updated_at`, `closed_at`, `close_reason`, `closed_by_session`, `bypassed_policy`, `bypass_reason`, `policy_gates_fired`, `due_at`, `defer_until`, `external_ref`, `source_system`, `source_repo`, `source_repo_path`, `agent_context`, `deleted_at`, `deleted_by`, `delete_reason`, `original_type`, `compaction_level`, `compacted_at`, `compacted_at_commit`, `original_size`, `sender`, `ephemeral`, `pinned`, `is_template`, `labels`, `dependencies`, `comments`. A 45th member, `content_hash`, exists on the record and is never serialized (its arithmetic: S4.51) | `src/model/mod.rs:462-650` (`content_hash` at `:468`) | `create_full_json`, `show_json`, `list_json` |
| S3.2 | Seven members are present in every serialized issue: `id`, `title`, `status`, `priority`, `issue_type`, `created_at`, `updated_at`. `compaction_level` is also always present, as an integer, with an absent value written as `0`. Every other scalar member is omitted when it has no value. After a store rewrite `source_repo` and `original_size` are present on every record as well (the rule and its bytes: S5, OQ-002) | `src/model/mod.rs:464-526`, `:623-624`, `:26` | `create_min_json`, `edge_precision_rewrite` |
| S3.3 | The three flags `ephemeral`, `pinned`, `is_template` are booleans that are omitted when false and absent means false everywhere | `src/model/mod.rs:20`, `:636-642` | `create_ephemeral`, `list_json` |
| S3.4 | `labels` is a collection of strings, `dependencies` of dependency records, `comments` of comment records; each is omitted when empty. Their order inside a record is S5's and S6's | `src/model/mod.rs:646-650` | `show_json`, `show_comments_json`, `list_label` |
| S3.5 | A **dependency** is a record of 7 members in this order: `issue_id`, `depends_on_id`, `type`, `created_at`, `created_by`, `metadata`, `thread_id`; the last three are optional. `depends_on_id` may name something that is not a stored issue (an `external:` reference or a missing id) | `src/model/mod.rs:896` | `dep_add_json`, `show_json`, `dep_add_external` |
| S3.6 | A **comment** is a record of 5 members in this order, all always present: `id` (a signed 64-bit integer; how it is assigned without a database is S4c's), `issue_id`, `author`, `text`, `created_at`. `text` may hold interior newlines and tabs | `src/model/mod.rs:933` | `comments_list_json`, `comments_add_json`, `show_comments_json` |
| S3.7 | `status` is an **open set** of lower-case strings. The named members are `open`, `in_progress`, `blocked`, `deferred`, `draft`, `closed`, `tombstone`, `pinned`; any other string is a custom status and is carried as written. Terminal statuses are `closed` and `tombstone` | `src/model/mod.rs:60` | `create_status_in_progress`, `update_status_closed`, `list_all_json` |
| S3.8 | `issue_type` is an **open set**: `task`, `bug`, `feature`, `epic`, `chore`, `docs`, `question`, and any other string as a custom type | `src/model/mod.rs:196` | `create_custom_type`, `list_type_bug` |
| S3.9 | A dependency `type` is one of 11 kebab-case strings: `blocks`, `parent-child`, `conditional-blocks`, `waits-for`, `related`, `discovered-from`, `replies-to`, `relates-to`, `duplicates`, `supersedes`, `caused-by`. `dep add` refuses any other string on stdout/stderr per S9 with `VALIDATION_FAILED`, exit 4, naming these 11 in this order (golden `dep_add_custom_type`; the refusal's bytes are S4's mutation clauses and S9's). The model names the first four as blocking; how each takes part in the BLOCKED relation is S4's "The BLOCKED relation". What the loader does with a type outside the 11 found in `issues.jsonl` is not exercised by the corpus (case to add: `edge_unknown_dep_type_list` — `["@fx=unknown_dep_type","list","--json"]` on a fixture whose one dependency has `"type":"inspired-by"`) | `src/model/mod.rs:272`, `:337` | `dep_add_custom_type`, `dep_add_related`, `blocked_json` |
| S3.10 | `priority` is an integer from 0 (critical) to 4 (backlog), default 2, serialized as a bare number and rendered `P{n}` in text | `src/model/mod.rs:152` | `create_priority_p_form`, `error_create_bad_priority`, `list_plain` |
| S3.11 | Every timestamp member (`created_at`, `updated_at`, `closed_at`, `due_at`, `defer_until`, `deleted_at`, `compacted_at`, and the `created_at` of dependencies and comments) is an instant in UTC with nanosecond resolution; parsing, ordering and printing are S4's time clauses | `src/model/mod.rs:519-626` | `edge_precision_list_json`, `edge_precision_ready` |
| S3.12 | Events (the audit trail of a database workspace) are not members of an issue record and are not in `issues.jsonl`: no in-scope output carries them | `src/model/mod.rs:962` | `show_json` |
| S3.13 | An **error** is a five-member record `code`, `message`, `hint`, `retryable`, `context` inside `{"error": …}`; its bytes and every instance are S9's | `src/error/structured.rs:593` (S9.1) | `error_show_missing_json`, `error_show_ambiguous_json` |
| S3.14 | An issue `id` is `<prefix>-<hash>` with optional `.<n>` child segments; its generation, validation, length limits and partial resolution are S4's identifier clauses. Ids are unique within a store, and the store's line order is by id | `src/util/id.rs:216`, `src/util/id.rs:306` (S4.1 and following) | `create_min`, `scn_child_ids`, `scn_dup_title` |
| S3.15 | A **store** is the sequence of issue records of `.beads/issues.jsonl`, one per line, plus the sidecar `.beads/last-touched` holding one issue id. Nothing else under `.beads/` is read or written by the in-scope surface of the port (the original's other sidecars: S8, DISC-002, DISC-004) | `src/sync/mod.rs:10779` (S5.A), `src/util/mod.rs:29` (`last-touched`) | `create_min`, `update_last_touched`, `scn_last_touched` |


## S4. Algorithms (behavioral)

<!-- from docs/spec-parts/S4a_S7.md -->

### Issue-id generation

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.1 | The id seed is a byte string built by concatenating exactly five length-prefixed parts, in this order: title, description, creator, timestamp, nonce. Each part is written as the part's **byte** length in decimal, then the single byte `:` (U+003A), then the part's raw UTF-8 bytes. There is no separator between parts and no terminator. Absent description and absent creator are each the empty string, which still contributes the two bytes `0:`. The length counts UTF-8 **bytes**, not characters — shown by golden `create_unicode`, whose 10-character title `日本語のタイトル 🚀` contributes the prefix `29:` | title `First issue`, no description, creator `tester`, nanos `1767323045000000000`, nonce `0` → seed bytes `11:First issue0:6:tester19:17673230450000000001:0` → `proj-fyw` **✓recomputed**; title `日本語のタイトル 🚀`, description `quote " slash \ bell <U+0007> del <U+007F> nbsp <U+00A0>` (36 bytes, no trailing space), creator `tester` → seed begins `29:` and the description part begins `36:` → `proj-7x0` **✓recomputed** | `src/util/id.rs:276`, `src/util/id.rs:295` | create_min, create_min_json, create_unicode, create_full_json, scn_dup_title |
| S4.2 | The timestamp part is the issue's `created_at` rendered as a **decimal count of nanoseconds since 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z**, signed, with no padding, no sign for non-negative values, and no unit suffix. Under the pinned instant 2026-01-02 03:04:05 UTC this is the 19-byte string `1767323045000000000`. When the instant lies outside the representable nanosecond range the count is `0` (see S7.14) Measured with a fractional pinned instant (2026-09-22): the count carries the fraction's nanoseconds (`create_fraction_json` at `…05.25`). | instant `2026-01-02T03:04:05Z` → `1767323045000000000`; instant `2026-02-03T09:00:00Z` → `1770109200000000000` (both **✓recomputed** against their goldens) | `src/util/id.rs:283` | create_min, scn_dup_title, create_fraction_json, create_fraction_eighth, q_fraction |
| S4.3 | The nonce part is the collision counter rendered as an unsigned decimal string, starting at `0`. Nonce `0` contributes `1:0`, nonce `10` contributes `2:10`, nonce `2000` contributes `4:2000` | nonce `0` → `1:0`; nonce `2` → `1:2` | `src/util/id.rs:284` | scn_dup_title |
| S4.4 | The seed bytes are hashed with **SHA-256**. The first 8 bytes of the 32-byte digest are folded into one unsigned 64-bit value **most-significant byte first** (big-endian): the accumulator starts at 0 and, per byte in digest order 0..7, is shifted left 8 bits and the byte is combined in by bitwise OR. Digest bytes 8..31 are discarded | digest `f1 c4 …` → value `0xf1c4…` (first byte is the high byte) | `src/util/id.rs:306` | create_min |
| S4.5 | The 64-bit value is rendered in **base 36** with the alphabet `0123456789abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz` (index 0 is `0`, index 35 is `z`; lowercase only). The value 0 renders as the single character `0`. Otherwise digits are produced least-significant first by repeated remainder and truncating integer division by 36, then reversed, so the rendering carries no leading `0`. A 64-bit value needs at most 13 base-36 characters (`3w5e11264sgsf` is the maximum) | value `0` → `0`; value `1295` → `zz`; value `46656` → `1000` | `src/util/id.rs:331` | create_min |
| S4.6 | If the base-36 rendering is shorter than the requested `length`, it is left-padded with the character `0` to exactly `length`. Then the **last `length` characters** are taken (the least-significant base-36 digits), which is the whole padded string when padding happened. The hash portion is therefore always exactly `length` characters from the set `[0-9a-z]` | value rendering `3aq2fyw`, length 3 → `fyw`; rendering `7x0`, length 3 → `7x0`; a rendering shorter than 3 (value < 46656) is padded, e.g. `5` with length 3 → `005` | `src/util/id.rs:317` | create_min, create_priority_p_form |
| S4.7 | The generated id is the prefix, then one `-` (U+002D), then the hash portion: `<prefix>-<hash>`. In the pinned sandbox the workspace is `/mnt/proj`, so the prefix is `proj` and ids read `proj-<hash>` | prefix `proj`, hash `fyw` → `proj-fyw` | `src/util/id.rs:132` | create_min, q_basic, where_plain |
