An issue with a custom status or the status `pinned` contributes to none of the per-status counters except `pinned_issues`. | `basic` → `total_issues` 8, `open_issues` 5, `in_progress_issues` 1, `closed_issues` 1, `deferred_issues` 1, `draft_issues` 0, `tombstone_issues` 0, `pinned_issues` 0; empty store → all 0 | `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:419` | stats_plain, stats_json, empty_stats, empty_stats_json |
| S4.152 | **`blocked_issues`.** The number of distinct ids in the union of (a) issues whose stored status is literally `blocked` and (b) the keys of the BLOCKED relation (S4.159–S4.165) restricted to issues whose status is not `closed`/`tombstone`. The two sets are deduplicated by id. | `basic` → 2 (`proj-170`, `proj-uly`); after `proj-mta`, `proj-170`, `proj-uly` are closed → 0 | `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:466` | stats_plain, stats_json, scn_lifecycle |
| S4.153 | **`ready_issues`.** The number of issues that are status `open`, not ephemeral, whose id does not contain `-wisp-`, not pinned, not a template, whose `defer_until` is absent or ≤ the pinned now, that are not in the BLOCKED relation and have no unsatisfied external blocker. This is the `ready` candidate rule of S4.171 with the default status group and without `--include-deferred`. | `basic` → 3 (`proj-mta`, `proj-5u2`, `proj-b75`); after the lifecycle closes → 2 | `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:452` | stats_plain, stats_json, ready_plain, scn_lifecycle |
| S4.154 | **`average_lead_time_hours`.** For each issue whose status is `closed` **and** that carries a `closed_at`, the lead time is `closed_at − created_at` **truncated toward zero to whole hours** (the fractional part is discarded before averaging). The figure is the arithmetic mean of those whole-hour values as a floating-point number; it is absent (JSON key omitted, "Extended" section suppressed) when no closed issue carries a `closed_at`. | `basic`: only `proj-mkh`, created `10:00:07`, closed `13:00:00` → 2 h 59 m 53 s → truncated to 2 → mean 2.0 → `"average_lead_time_hours":2.0`, text `Avg Lead Time:          2.0 hours`. After `scn_lifecycle`: leads 2, 743, 743, 744 → mean 558.0 h → 23.25 days → text `23.2 days` Measured (2026-09-24, OQ-133): the subtraction keeps the fractions: a close 3599.78 s after its creation (`02:09:22.449598Z` to `03:09:22.232700Z`) is 0 whole hours, not 1 (`stats_lead_subsecond`; found by the real-store sweep on 8 of 138 stores). Measured (2026-09-24, round 12): equal instants are 0 hours; offsets (`+02:00`, `-01:30`) and micro- and nanosecond fractions are applied before the subtraction; a `closed_at` before `created_at`, even by 0.1 s or only after the offset is applied, refuses the load (exit 7, the record validation of S2.30). | `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:428`, `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:473`, `goldens/stats_lead_subsecond.out` | stats_plain, stats_json, scn_lifecycle, empty_stats, stats_lead_subsecond, r12_lead_edges_stats_json, r12_lead_micro_nano_json, r12_lead_offset_before, r12_lead_frac_before_json, r12_lead_days_stats |
| S4.155 | **`epics_eligible_for_closure`.** Over every issue of type `epic` whose status is not `closed`/`tombstone` and that is not a template, count those whose parent-child child count (S4.186) is greater than 0 and equals its closed-child count. | `basic` → 0 (`proj-uly` has one open child); after `proj-170` is closed → 1 Measured (2026-09-24, round 12): a tombstoned child counts among the closed children. | `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:521` | stats_json, scn_lifecycle, r12_epics_stats, r12_epics_blocked_json |
| S4.156 | **Conditional summary lines.** `Deferred`, `Tombstones`, `Pinned` and `Epics ready to close` lines appear only when their figure is greater than 0. The `Extended:` block appears only when the average lead time exists or the tombstone count is greater than 0. (Exact line spelling and column alignment: S5.) | empty store → the summary block has no `Deferred` line and no `Extended:` block; `basic` → `Deferred:               1` and an `Extended:` block | `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:1235` | empty_stats, stats_plain |

### The BLOCKED relation

The relation maps an issue id to a list of *blocker refs*. It is the single
source of truth for `blocked`, for `ready`'s exclusion, for `stats`'
blocked/ready counts, and for the claim and close guards. It is rebuilt from
the dependency graph on every read (under `--no-db` the cached projection is
always stale at start-up, so the compute path below is the one the goldens
exercise).

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.157 | **Step 1 — direct blockers.** For every dependency edge whose type is one of `blocks`, `conditional-blocks`, `waits-for`, whose target id does **not** begin with `external:`, and whose target is either absent from the store or has a status other than `closed`/`tombstone` and is not a template, add to the *source* issue the blocker ref `<target-id>:<target-status>`, where the status is the target's stored status or the literal `unknown` when the target does not exist. Edges of any other type (`related`, `parent-child`, custom types) never produce a direct blocker. Duplicate (source, ref) pairs collapse. | `proj-170 —blocks→ proj-mta` with `proj-mta` open → `proj-170` gets `proj-mta:open`; `proj-5u2 —related→ proj-170` contributes nothing Measured (2026-09-24, round 12): of the `external:` targets, only `blocks`, `conditional-blocks` and `waits-for` edges block, whatever the shape of the reference. | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:11333` | blocked_plain, blocked_json, dep_list, blocked_graph, blocked_graph_json, r10_n984_blocked, r10_parents_blocked_json, r12_ext_types_blocked_json, r12_ext_types_ready, r12_ext_names_blocked, r12_diamond_blocked_json |
| S4.158 | **External targets are not direct blockers here.** An edge to an `external:…` target is excluded from step 1 and handled separately (S4.166). A `parent-child` edge whose either endpoint begins with `external:` is excluded from step 2 as well. | `dep add proj-mta external:other-1` leaves `proj-mta` out of the step-1 map | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:11338`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:11429` | dep_add_external *(mutation case; the resulting edge exercises this rule)* |
| S4.159 | **Step 2 — parent-blocked propagation.** Taking the step-1 map's keys as the initial frontier, repeatedly: for each id in the frontier that is the *target* of one or more `parent-child` edges, add to each such edge's *source* (the child) the blocker ref `<parent-id>:parent-blocked`, and push that child onto the frontier if the ref was newly added. Each id is processed at most once. A child of a blocked parent therefore becomes blocked, and the effect reaches every descendant. `parent-child` alone never makes a child block its parent in this step. Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-041): the propagation reaches a grandchild: `proj-170.1` is not ready and `blocked` names its parent `proj-170` (`scn_parent_blocked`). | a child of `proj-170` (which is blocked by `proj-mta:open`) would receive `proj-170:parent-blocked` Measured (2026-09-24, round 12): the propagation runs through four levels, and a closed source that is still a key of the relation passes `:parent-blocked` to its child although `blocked` does not list it. | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:11628` | *(case to add: `blocked_parent_propagation` — a fixture with `epic → child → grandchild` plus a `blocks` edge on the epic)*, scn_parent_blocked, r12_propagate_blocked, r12_propagate_blocked_detailed, r12_propagate_ready_json, r12_propagate_stats_json |
| S4.160 | **Step 3 — epics blocked by open children.** For every `parent-child` edge whose *target* exists, is of issue type `epic`, and whose *source* (the child) exists, has a status other than `closed`/`tombstone` and is not a template, and where neither endpoint begins with `external:`, append to the epic the blocker ref `<child-id>:child-open`. The rule applies to epics only: a non-epic parent with open children is not blocked by them. | `proj-uly` is an epic and `proj-170` is its open child → `proj-uly` gets `proj-170:child-open`; after `proj-170` closes, `proj-uly` leaves the relation | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:11515` | blocked_plain, blocked_json, scn_lifecycle |
| S4.161 | **The order of the three steps is observable.** Propagation (step 2) runs *before* the epic rollup (step 3). An epic that becomes blocked only by its own open children therefore never propagates `:parent-blocked` down onto those same children, so the children of a blocked epic remain ready. Running step 3 first would remove them from `ready`. | after `create --parent proj-uly` twice and `--parent proj-uly.1` once, `proj-uly` is blocked (open children) yet `ready` still lists `proj-uly.1`, `proj-uly.2`, `proj-uly.1.1` | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:10977` | scn_child_ids |
| S4.162 | **Blocker-list normalization.** After the three steps each issue's blocker list is sorted ascending by byte order of the whole ref string and adjacent duplicates removed; an issue left with an empty list is dropped from the relation. The sort is over the annotated ref, so `<id>:child-open` sorts before `<id>:open` before `<id>:parent-blocked` before `<id>:unknown` for the same id. | `{proj-mta:open, proj-mta:open}` → `[proj-mta:open]` | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:10996` | blocked_json |
| S4.163 | **Blocker ref → displayed id.** Wherever a ref reaches an output it is reduced to an id by cutting at the **last** `:` (so an `external:other-1:blocked` ref yields `external:other-1`). `blocked_by` in JSON and the text `[…]` list carry those ids, in the normalized order of S4.162. | `proj-mta:open` → `proj-mta`; `blocked_by":["proj-mta"]` | `src/cli/commands/blocked.rs:462` | blocked_plain, blocked_json |
| S4.164 | **Start-blockers (claim / move to `in_progress`).** The blocker list minus every ref ending in `:child-open` and every ref ending in `:parent-blocked`. An epic with only open children is claimable; a child of a blocked parent is claimable; an issue with a real `blocks`/`conditional-blocks`/`waits-for` prerequisite is not. | `update proj-170 --claim` is refused (`proj-mta:open` survives the filter) | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:10754` | error_update_blocked_claim |
| S4.165 | **Close-blockers.** The blocker list minus every ref ending in `:parent-blocked` only — the `:child-open` rollup is kept, so an epic cannot close over open children while a finished child of a blocked epic can close. | `close proj-170` is refused; `close proj-uly` is refused while `proj-170` is open | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:10782` | error_close_blocked, error_close_blocked_json, close_epic_with_open_child |
| S4.166 | **External blockers (overlay).** After the local relation is built, every edge of type `blocks`/`conditional-blocks`/`waits-for` whose target begins with `external:` adds the ref `<target>:blocked` to its source unless the external project's status lookup reports the target satisfied; and every `parent-child` edge whose *source* begins with `external:` and whose target is a local epic adds a child-open style ref to that epic on the same condition. With no external project configured no target is ever satisfied, so such an edge always blocks. The overlay is merged into the existing entry, then the list is re-sorted and deduplicated and `blocked_by_count` recomputed; an issue not already in the relation is appended (and skipped if its status is terminal). Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-045): an `external:` blocks edge blocks its source with no external project configured: `Blocked by 1 open dependencies: [external:other-1]` (`scn_external_blocker`). | `proj-mta —blocks→ external:other-1` with no external project → `proj-mta` blocked by `external:other-1` | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:11949`, `src/cli/commands/blocked.rs:148` | *(case to add: `blocked_external` — a fixture carrying an `external:` blocking edge)*, scn_external_blocker |

### `blocked`

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.167 | **Candidate set.** `blocked` reports every issue that is a key of the BLOCKED relation (S4.157–S4.166) **and** whose status is not `closed`/`tombstone`. A closed issue that still has open blockers is not listed. | `basic` → `proj-170`, `proj-uly` Measured (2026-09-24, round 12): a template epic with an open child is listed. | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:11152` | blocked_plain, blocked_json, r12_dep_to_template_blocked |
| S4.168 | **`blocked` filters.** Applied in Rust after the relation is built, all conjunctive: `--type`/`-t` (repeatable, the record's type must be one of the parsed values), `--priority`/`-p` (repeatable, the full form of S4.107), `--label`/`-l` (repeatable, the record must carry **all** the labels). There is no status, assignee, id or title filter. | `blocked -t epic` → `proj-uly` only, header count 1; `blocked -p 1 --json` → `proj-170` only, `"total":1` | `src/cli/commands/blocked.rs:330` | *(case to add: `blocked_type` — `["@fx=basic","blocked","-t","epic"]`; `blocked_priority_json` — `["@fx=basic","blocked","-p","1","--json"]`)* |
| S4.169 | **`blocked` order.** After filtering, rows are sorted by priority ascending, then **blocker count descending**, then `created_at` descending, then id ascending. The blocker-count key sits between priority and `created_at`; it is absent from every other ordering in this section. | `basic` → `proj-170` (P1) then `proj-uly` (P2) | `src/cli/commands/blocked.rs:318` | blocked_plain, blocked_json |
| S4.170 | **`blocked` pagination.** `blocked`'s `--limit` default is **50**; 0 means unlimited. `total` is the filtered count *before* truncation and is what both the JSON envelope and the text header `🚫 Blocked issues (N):` report. `has_more` is `limit > 0 && shown < total`; `offset` is always 0 (`blocked` has no `--offset`). When truncation happened and output is text and not quiet, one stderr line follows: `[note] Showing 1 of 2 blocked issues. Use --limit 0 for all results.`. When nothing is blocked the JSON envelope is `{"issues":[],"total":0,"limit":50,"offset":0,"has_more":false}` and text prints `✨ No blocked issues`. | `blocked --json` → `"total":2,"limit":50,"offset":0,"has_more":false`; `blocked --limit 1` → header `(2)`, one row, the note on stderr | `src/cli/mod.rs:2796`, `src/cli/commands/blocked.rs:271` | blocked_json, empty_blocked, empty_blocked_json, *(case to add: `blocked_limit_one` — `["@fx=basic","blocked","--limit","1"]`)* |

### `ready`

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.171 | **Candidate rule.** An issue is a `ready` candidate when **all** of: its status is in the configured ready status group — the default group is exactly `[open]`, so `in_progress` is *not* ready; it is not a key of the BLOCKED relation (S4.157–S4.166); its `defer_until` is absent or ≤ the pinned now (S4.113); it is not pinned (an absent marker counts as not pinned); it is not ephemeral (absent counts as not ephemeral); its id does not contain the substring `-wisp-`; it is not a template. | `basic` → `proj-mta`, `proj-5u2`, `proj-b75`; `proj-ptp` excluded (in_progress), `proj-170` excluded (blocked by `proj-mta`), `proj-uly` excluded (open child), `proj-7vm` excluded (deferred, future `defer_until`), `proj-mkh` excluded (closed) | `src/cli/commands/ready.rs:131`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:10247` | ready_plain, ready_json, empty_ready, empty_ready_json, ready_graph, ready_graph_json, r10_n984_ready_limit, r10_n984_ready_json_oldest, r10_tree_ready_priority |
| S4.172 | **`--include-deferred`.** Adds the literal status `deferred` to the status group (unless already present, compared ASCII case-insensitively) **and** drops the `defer_until ≤ now` condition entirely — so a deferred issue with a future `defer_until` becomes a candidate. | `ready --include-deferred --json` → four rows, `proj-7vm` inserted between `proj-5u2` and `proj-b75` by its `created_at` 10:00:05 | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:1612`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:10261` | ready_include_deferred |
| S4.173 | **`ready` filters.** Conjunctive with S4.171: `--assignee` (with no value it resolves to the current actor; with a value it is exact equality), `--unassigned` (assignee absent or empty; mutually exclusive with `--assignee`), `-l`/`--label` (AND, S4.126), `--label-any` (OR, S4.127), `-t`/`--type` (repeatable, OR), `-p`/`--priority` (full form of S4.107), `--parent <id>` (direct parent-child children of that id), `-r`/`--recursive` (with `--parent`: all transitive parent-child descendants, breadth-first with a visited set so cycles terminate), `--epic <id>` (sugar for `--parent <id> --recursive`). A `--parent` whose membership set is empty yields no rows. Measured on fixture `parents` (2026-09-22): the parent itself is never a member, even when it is ready (`ready_parent_nested`); its id resolves like any partial id (`ready_parent_partial`), and one that resolves to nothing refuses with `ISSUE_NOT_FOUND`, exit 3 (`error_ready_parent_missing`); `-r` is `--recursive`, not a reverse flag (`ready_parent_short_r`); `--recursive` without `--parent` changes nothing (`ready_recursive_only`); the members keep the ready order of the sort policy. | `ready -t task` → `proj-mta`, `proj-b75`; `ready -l db --json` → `proj-mta`; `ready --unassigned` → all three (none of the ready rows has an assignee) | `src/cli/commands/ready.rs:136`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:10078` | ready_type, ready_label, ready_unassigned, ready_parents, ready_parent, ready_parent_json, ready_parent_recursive, ready_parent_recursive_json, ready_parent_short_r, ready_parent_nested, ready_parent_partial, ready_parent_none, ready_parent_none_json, ready_epic, ready_epic_json, ready_recursive_only, error_ready_parent_missing, error_ready_parent_missing_json |
| S4.174 | **Sort policy `hybrid` (default).** Two buckets: bucket 0 is priority ≤ 1 (P0 and P1), bucket 1 is everything else. Order by bucket ascending, then `created_at` **ascending** (oldest first), then id ascending. Priority is *not* a key inside a bucket, so a P3 can precede a P2. | `basic` → 1. `proj-mta` (P1, bucket 0); 2. `proj-5u2` (P3, created 10:00:03); 3. `proj-b75` (P2, created 10:00:06) — the P3 before the P2. `precision` (all P2) → `aae`, `aaa`, `aac`, `aab`, `aad`, `aaf`, `aag`, `aah` | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:18186`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:10334` | ready_plain, ready_json, edge_precision_ready |
| S4.175 | **Sort policy `priority`.** Priority ascending, then `created_at` ascending, then id ascending. | `ready --sort priority` → `proj-mta` (P1), `proj-b75` (P2), `proj-5u2` (P3) | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:10339` | ready_sort_priority |
| S4.176 | **Sort policy `oldest`.** `created_at` ascending, then id ascending; priority is ignored. | `ready --sort oldest` → `proj-mta` (10:00:00), `proj-5u2` (10:00:03), `proj-b75` (10:00:06) | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:10344` | ready_sort_oldest |
| S4.177 | **`ready` pagination.** `ready`'s `--limit` default is **0** — unlimited. A positive limit truncates the sorted candidate list after external-blocker filtering; there is no offset and no page envelope (JSON output is a bare array). When truncation happened and output is text and not quiet, one stderr line follows: `[note] Showing 1 of 3 ready issues. Use --limit 0 for all results.` — the second number is the pre-truncation candidate count. | `ready --limit 1` → header `(1 issue with no blockers)`, one row, the note on stderr; `ready --limit 0` → identical to `ready` | `src/cli/mod.rs:2691`, `src/cli/commands/ready.rs:181` | ready_limit, ready_plain |
| S4.178 | **Empty-`ready` message selection.** When no candidate survives, text mode prints exactly one of three lines, chosen in this order: `✨ All work complete — no issues to work on` when the store holds no issue with a status other than `closed`/`tombstone` that is not a template; otherwise `✨ No ready issues match the requested filters or configured ready status group` when any of `--assignee`, `--unassigned`, `--label`, `--label-any`, `--type`, `--priority`, `--parent` is set or the ready status group is not the default `[open]`; otherwise `✨ No ready issues — remaining work is not currently actionable`. JSON mode prints `[]` in every case. Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-046, OQ-075): every open issue blocked or deferred prints `✨ No ready issues — remaining work is not currently actionable` (`scn_ready_all_blocked`). | empty store → the first line; `@fx=basic ready -l nosuch` → the second line | `src/cli/commands/ready.rs:293` | empty_ready, empty_ready_json, ready_parent_none (the second line, under `--parent`), ready_filtered_empty, scn_ready_all_blocked |

### `dep list`, `dep tree`, `dep cycles`

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.179 | **`dep list` direction and selection.** `--direction down` (the default) collects the issue's own dependency edges; `up` collects the edges pointing at it; `both` collects both, in that order (down first). Every edge type is included, external targets too. `--type`/`-t` keeps only edges whose type string equals the normalized filter value. Each row carries the edge's `issue_id`, `depends_on_id`, `type` plus the *other* endpoint's title, status and priority (absent endpoint → the placeholder metadata of the dangling-target path). | `dep list proj-170` → 2 rows; `dep list proj-mta --direction up --json` → 1 row `{"issue_id":"proj-170","depends_on_id":"proj-mta","type":"blocks",…}`; `dep list proj-mta --direction both` → the same single dependent row under the header `Dependencies and dependents of proj-mta (1):` | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:922`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:14170` | dep_list, dep_list_json, dep_list_up, dep_list_up_json, dep_list_up_type_json, dep_list_both, dep_list_both_json, dep_list_both_basic, r10_tree_dep_list_up |
| S4.180 | **`dep list` order.** The query order is: the other endpoint's priority ascending with a missing endpoint treated as priority 2, then that endpoint's `created_at` descending, then the other endpoint's id ascending. **JSON output emits that order unchanged.** Text output re-sorts by priority ascending, then `issue_id` ascending, then `depends_on_id` ascending, then dependency type ascending — so a `--direction both` text listing interleaves dependencies and dependents by priority while JSON keeps them grouped. | `dep list proj-170` → `proj-mta` (blocks, P1) then `proj-uly` (parent-child, P2) in both modes | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:14179`, `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:1239` | dep_list, dep_list_json, dep_list_up, dep_list_up_json, dep_list_both, dep_list_both_json |
| S4.181 | **`dep list` with no edges.** Text mode prints `No dependencies for proj-ptp` for direction `down`, `No dependents for …` for `up`, `No dependencies or dependents for …` for `both`, and exits 0. JSON mode prints `[]`. | `dep list proj-ptp` → `No dependencies for proj-ptp` | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:1010` | dep_list_none, dep_list_graph_parent, dep_list_graph_none, dep_list_up_none, dep_list_up_none_json, dep_list_both_none |
| S4.182 | **`dep tree` adjacency.** The whole dependency edge set is loaded once and turned into two maps: child→targets (`down`) and target→children (`up`); each adjacency list is sorted by id and deduplicated. `--direction both` concatenates the two lists, then sorts and deduplicates. Edge type is ignored — every dependency type participates in the tree. An `external:` node is never expanded. | `dep tree proj-uly` (down) → `proj-uly` alone: the parent-child edge runs child→parent, so the epic has no dependencies of its own. `dep tree proj-uly --direction up` → `proj-uly`, then `proj-170`, then `proj-5u2` | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:1302` | dep_tree, dep_tree_json, dep_tree_leaf, scn_child_ids, dep_tree_up_basic (the `up` walk of `proj-uly` reaches `proj-170` then `proj-5u2`, as this clause predicted), dep_tree_up, dep_tree_up_json, dep_tree_both, dep_tree_both_json |
| S4.183 | **`dep tree` traversal order.** Depth-first, pre-order: the root is emitted first at depth 0 with no parent; a node's children are sorted by S4.184 and then visited left to right, each child's whole subtree emitted before the next sibling. Node keys are assigned `n0`, `n1`, … in emission order and a child records its parent's key and id. | `dep tree proj-uly --direction up` emits `proj-uly` (depth 0), `proj-170` (depth 1), `proj-5u2` (depth 2) | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:1435` | dep_tree_json, *(case to add as in S4.182)*, r10_tree_dep_tree_up, r10_tree_dep_tree_down, r10_parents_dep_tree_both, r10_tree_dep_tree_root_up, r10_tree_dep_tree_grand_down |
| S4.184 | **`dep tree` sibling order.** Siblings are ordered by the target's priority ascending (a node with no metadata ranks last), then by a status rank `open` 0, `in_progress` 1, `blocked` 2, `deferred` 3, `closed` 4, `deleted`/`tombstone` 5, anything else 6 (no metadata ranks last), then by title ascending byte order, then by id ascending byte order. Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-049): P0, P1, then the open P2 before the closed P2 (`scn_dep_tree_shapes`). | `dep_tree_repeat` separates the keys: `proj-0i4` and `proj-t11` share priority 2, so the STATUS RANK decides (`open` 0 before `in_progress` 1) and it decides against the title order; `dep_tree_diamond` and `dep_tree_up_basic` separate title from id (`proj-170` before `proj-mta` at equal priority and status, although `mta` is the larger id) | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:1809` | dep_tree_repeat, dep_tree_repeat_json, dep_tree_diamond, dep_tree_up_basic, scn_dep_tree_shapes |
| S4.185 | **`dep tree` depth limit, shared nodes and cycles.** `--max-depth` defaults to **10**. A node is expanded only when its depth is below `max_depth`, it is not external, and its id has not been expanded anywhere else in this traversal. A node whose id *is* already expanded is still emitted under each parent but carries `repeat` true when it has neighbours, and its subtree is not repeated. A node that is not expanded but has neighbours carries `truncated` true; an expanded node at exactly `max_depth − 1` whose children would exceed the limit also carries `truncated` true. A node already on its own ancestor path is skipped entirely, which terminates cycles. The expansion slot is claimed only by an occurrence that really expands, so a shallower later occurrence is never demoted. In text output `truncated` renders the prefix `├── (truncated) `, `repeat` renders `├── (shown above) `, and an ordinary non-root node `├── `; the depth-0 node has no prefix; indentation is two spaces per depth level. JSON emits every node with `id`, `title`, `depth`, `parent_id` (`null` at the root), `priority`, `status`, `truncated`, `repeat`. Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-043, OQ-065): in a diamond the second occurrence prints `(truncated) ` and in JSON carries `"truncated":true,"repeat":true`; at `--max-depth 1` a node with more below prints `(truncated) ` (`scn_dep_tree_shapes`). | `dep tree proj-uly --json` → `[{"id":"proj-uly","title":"Auth epic","depth":0,"parent_id":null,"priority":2,"status":"open","truncated":false,"repeat":false}]` | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:1293`, `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:1758` | dep_tree, dep_tree_json, dep_tree_leaf, dep_tree_repeat_json (`truncated` and `repeat` TRUE on the same node), dep_tree_max_depth_json (nothing ever expands `proj-in9`, so both occurrences are `truncated` with `repeat` FALSE — the expansion slot is claimed only by an occurrence that really expands), dep_tree_max_depth_0 (the ROOT itself carries `truncated`), dep_tree_diamond_json (a shared node with NO neighbours carries neither flag), dep_tree_back_edge (an ancestor is skipped entirely, with no node and no marker), scn_dep_tree_shapes |
| S4.186 | **`dep cycles` edge set.** The cycle graph is built from exactly two edge families, regardless of `--blocking-only` (the flag is a compatible alias and changes nothing): every edge of type `blocks`, `conditional-blocks` or `waits-for`, oriented source→target; and every `parent-child` edge oriented **reversed**, parent→child. `related` and custom types are never counted. Both endpoints of each edge become graph nodes even when they have no outgoing edges, and each adjacency list is sorted and deduplicated. | `basic` → edges `proj-170→proj-mta` and `proj-uly→proj-170`; no cycle | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:19182` | dep_cycles, dep_cycles_json, empty_dep_cycles, dep_cycles_graph, dep_cycles_graph_json |
| S4.187 | **`dep cycles` witness and ordering.** Strongly connected components are computed; components are each sorted by id ascending and the component list is sorted ascending. A one-node component reports a cycle only when the node has a self-edge, reported as `[id, id]`. A larger component reports one witness: starting from the component's lowest id, the first neighbour (in sorted order) that is inside the component and is not the start itself is followed to a path back to the start, and the witness is `[start, …path…]` ending at the start. The witness list is then sorted ascending, comparing the id vectors element by element. Text mode numbers them from 1 and joins ids with ` -> `. | no cycle → text `✓ No dependency cycles detected.`, JSON `{"cycles":[],"count":0,"active_count":0,"archived_closed_count":0,"total_count":0,"blocking_only":false,"include_closed":false,"scope":"active"}` | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:19242`, `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:2124` | dep_cycles, dep_cycles_json, empty_dep_cycles, *(case to add: `dep_cycles_found` — a `cycles` fixture with `a→b→c→a` on `blocks` edges)* |
| S4.188 | **`dep cycles` active vs archived.** A component in which every node's status is `closed` or `tombstone` is *archived*; all others are *active*. `active_count` and `archived_closed_count` are the two witness counts and `total_count` their sum, all reported regardless of `--include-closed`. Without `--include-closed` the reported `cycles` list holds the active witnesses only, `count` is `active_count`, `scope` is `active`, and the keys `active_cycles`/`archived_closed_cycles` are omitted when empty; when archived cycles exist but are hidden, text mode says `No active dependency cycles detected. N archived closed-only cycle(s) hidden; rerun with --include-closed to inspect them.`. With `--include-closed`, `cycles` is the concatenation of both lists re-sorted ascending, `count` is its length, `scope` is `active_and_archived`, and both extra key arrays are emitted when non-empty. A non-zero `active_count` sets the process exit code to the cycle-detected code *after* the output is written, so the JSON stream stays a single clean object; archived-only cycles never change the exit code. | `dep cycles --json` on `basic` → the object in S4.187 with exit 0 | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:1960` | dep_cycles_json, *(case to add as in S4.187)* |

### `epic status` and `epic close-eligible`

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.189 | **The epic set and its order.** `epic status` lists every issue of type `epic` that survives the visibility rule with `include_closed` false and `include_deferred` true (so a deferred epic is listed, a closed or tombstoned one is not) and that is not a template, ordered by the default order of S4.115 (`priority ASC, created_at DESC, id ASC`). When the set is empty text mode prints `No open epics found` and JSON prints `[]`. Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-050): the deferred epic is listed and the closed one is not (`scn_epic_status_deferred`). | `basic` → one epic, `proj-uly`; `epic status --eligible-only` on `basic` → `No open epics found` (the only epic is not eligible) | `src/cli/commands/epic.rs:260` | epic_status, epic_status_json, scn_epic_status_deferred |
| S4.190 | **Child counting.** For each epic, `total_children` is the number of `parent-child` edges whose target is that epic and whose source exists and is not a template; `closed_children` is how many of those sources have status `closed` or `tombstone`. Children are counted by dependency edge, so only *direct* children count: a grandchild whose `parent-child` edge points at an intermediate child is not counted for the epic. Dotted id shape plays no part in this count. | `basic` → `proj-uly`: 1 total, 0 closed. After `create --parent proj-uly` twice and `create --parent proj-uly.1` once → 3 total (`proj-170`, `proj-uly.1`, `proj-uly.2`; `proj-uly.1.1` counts for `proj-uly.1`, not for `proj-uly`), 0 closed | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:12318` | epic_status, epic_status_json, scn_child_ids, r10_tree_epic_status, r10_tree_epic_status_json |
| S4.191 | **Eligibility and progress.** `eligible_for_close` is true exactly when `total_children > 0` and `closed_children == total_children`. The displayed percentage is `min(closed, total) * 100 / total` computed in integers with truncation, and 0 when `total` is 0. | `proj-uly` with 0/1 → `eligible_for_close` false, `Progress: 0/1 children closed (0%)`; after its only child closes → true, `1/1 children closed (100%)` plus the line `   Eligible for closure` | `src/cli/commands/epic.rs:276`, `src/cli/commands/epic.rs:435` | epic_status, epic_status_json, scn_lifecycle |
| S4.192 | **`--eligible-only`** filters the list of S4.189 down to the eligible epics after the counts are computed; an empty result takes the empty-set path of S4.189. | `epic status --eligible-only` on `basic` → `No open epics found` | `src/cli/commands/epic.rs:75` | *(case to add: `epic_status_eligible_only` — `["@fx=basic","epic","status","--eligible-only"]`)* |
| S4.193 | **`epic close-eligible` selection (mutation — selection rule only).** It computes the same list as S4.189 and keeps exactly the eligible epics of S4.191. Those are the epics it would close, with the fixed close reason `All children completed`. Under `--dry-run` nothing is written; text mode prints `Would close N epic(s):` followed by one `  - <id>: <title>` line per epic in the order of S4.189, and JSON prints the same epic-status objects. With no eligible epic and no `--dry-run`, text prints `No epics eligible for closure` and JSON prints `{"closed":[],"count":0}`. | `epic close-eligible --dry-run` on `basic` → `Would close 0 epic(s):` and no further line | `src/cli/commands/epic.rs:121` | *(case to add: `epic_close_eligible_dry_run` — `["@fx=basic","epic","close-eligible","--dry-run"]`)* |

### `show` — what is gathered, and in what order

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.194 | **Target selection.** The positional ids are the targets, in the order given on the command line; duplicates are not collapsed. With no positional id the single target is the id stored in `.beads/last-touched`, and an empty/absent sidecar is the error `no issue IDs provided and no last-touched issue`. Each target is resolved (exact id, then dotted-child/partial/hash resolution — the resolution algorithm itself is outside this section) and the records are emitted in the requested input order, not in any store order. | `show proj-mta proj-5u2` → `proj-mta` block then `proj-5u2` block | `src/cli/commands/show.rs:236`, `src/cli/commands/show.rs:396` | show_two, show_plain, show_graph_root, show_graph_root_json |
| S4.195 | **Related data gathered per target, and the order each is gathered in.** For each resolved target, in this order: (1) the issue record itself; (2) a single presence probe answering five questions — has labels, has dependency edges, has dependent edges, has comments, has parent-child children — plus the *parent*, taken as the target of the target's own `parent-child` edges, the last one by insertion order when there is more than one; (3) labels, only when present, ordered ascending by byte order of the label; (4) dependencies, only when present, the `--direction down` relation and order of S4.179/S4.180; (5) dependents, only when present, the `--direction up` relation and order of S4.179/S4.180; (6) comments, only when present, ordered by `created_at` ascending then comment id ascending; (7) events — **not** gathered by `show` (`include_events` is false, so the event list is always empty); (8) a parent-child subtree rollup, only when the target has children. An absent group is an empty list, and S5 suppresses its heading. Resolved (2026-09-23, OQ-096): `show --json` prints labels as the file holds them, unsorted and with duplicates (`edge_precision_show_json`). | `show proj-170 --json` → `"labels":["auth","backend"]`, then `"dependencies":[proj-mta (blocks), proj-uly (parent-child)]`, then `"dependents":[proj-5u2 (related)]`, then `"parent":"proj-uly"`; `show proj-uly` → no Dependencies block, one Dependents block; `show proj-ptp` → one comment | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:15717`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:15879` | show_plain, show_json, show_epic, show_comments, show_comments_json, show_closed, show_deferred_json, show_unicode, show_unicode_json |
| S4.196 | **The `parent` field.** It is the *target* of the issue's own `parent-child` edge, i.e. the issue this record is a child of — not its children. It is present only when such an edge exists. Children are reachable only through the dependents list and the rollup. | `proj-170` has a `parent-child` edge to `proj-uly` → `"parent":"proj-uly"`; `proj-uly` itself has no `parent` key and lists `proj-170` under dependents | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:15888` | show_json, show_epic |
| S4.197 | **The subtree rollup.** Gathered only when the target is the target of at least one `parent-child` edge. It walks the parent-child subtree level by level with a visited set seeded with the target itself, so a cycle terminates and the target never counts as its own descendant, and it tallies descendant statuses. The derived status is the furthest-along non-terminal descendant status by the project's declared status order, or by the built-in ladder `draft < deferred < open < blocked < in_progress` when none is declared; statuses outside the order rank lowest and a tie resolves to the lexicographically greatest name. When every descendant is terminal the rollup status is `closed`. | `proj-uly` with one open child → a rollup is computed; `proj-mta` (no children) → no rollup | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:15790` | show_epic, *(case to add: `show_rollup_json` — `["@fx=basic","show","proj-uly","--json"]`)* |
| S4.198 | **`show` gathers no blocked information.** The BLOCKED relation is not consulted by `show`; a blocked issue's record carries its dependency edges only, with each edge's current status. | `show proj-170` lists `proj-mta (blocks) … [open]` but never names it a blocker | `src/cli/commands/show.rs:539` | show_plain, show_json |


<!-- from docs/spec-parts/S4c.md -->

### S4.200–S4.219 Cross-cutting rules for every mutating command

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.200 | **Actor resolution.** The actor is the first non-empty of: the global `--actor <name>` option; the `actor` key of the merged config layer (`.beads/config.yaml`); the environment variable `USER` (trimmed); otherwise the literal `unknown`. The same resolved actor lands in `created_by` on `create`/`q`, in `created_by` of every dependency edge the command writes, in `deleted_by` on `delete`, and in `assignee` on `update --claim`. | `USER=tester`, no `--actor` → `"created_by":"tester"` · `--actor robot-7` → `"created_by":"robot-7"` | `src/config/mod.rs:7054` (`actor_from_layer`), `src/config/mod.rs:7063` (`resolve_actor`), `src/cli/mod.rs:702` (`--actor` is a global option) | create_min, actor_flag_create, update_claim, delete_basic, actor_flag_dep_add (the new edge's `created_by`), actor_flag_delete (`deleted_by`), actor_flag_label_add (measured 2026-09-20: `label add` records no actor anywhere, so `--actor` changes nothing in its output or its store line) |
| S4.201 | **Comment author resolution is a different ladder.** For `comments add` the author is the first non-blank of: `--author <name>`; the config layer's `actor` key (which the global `--actor` option feeds, and which — unlike S4.200 — has no `$USER` fallback at this stage); the first non-blank of the environment variables `BD_ACTOR`, `BEADS_ACTOR`, `USER`, `LOGNAME`, `USERNAME`, in that order; otherwise the literal `unknown`. The net effect under the pinned environment is the same as S4.200, reached one rung lower. | `--author zoe` → `"author":"zoe"` · `--actor robot-7` → `"author":"robot-7"` · neither, `USER=tester` → `"author":"tester"` | `src/cli/commands/comments.rs:449` (`resolve_author`), `:467` (`resolve_author_from_env`), `:106`, `src/config/mod.rs:6154` | comments_add, comments_add_author, actor_flag_comment |
| S4.202 | **The write-back happens once per invocation, after every id has been processed** — never per id. The command mutates an in-memory store, marking each touched issue dirty, and at the end calls one export that rewrites `.beads/issues.jsonl` in full. When the dirty set is empty and no explicit flush marker is armed, the export does not run at all. | `update proj-mta proj-5u2 -p 4` → one store dump containing both changes | `src/config/mod.rs:4914` (`flush_no_db_if_dirty`; returns early when `dirty_issue_count == 0` and `needs_flush` is not `true`), `src/cli/commands/close.rs:1322`, `src/cli/commands/create.rs:164` | update_two_ids, close_two, delete_cascade |
| S4.203 | **A mutation that changes nothing leaves the store's bytes untouched.** No dirty issue ⇒ no export ⇒ no `--- .beads/issues.jsonl ---` dump. This holds for `update` whose every named value already equals the stored value, for `dep add` of an existing edge, for `label add` of an existing label, for `label remove` of an absent label, for `dep remove` of an absent edge, for `reopen` of a non-closed issue, for `undefer` of a non-deferred issue, and for every refusal. | `update proj-mta -p 1` on a P1 issue → stdout only, no store dump | `src/config/mod.rs:4914`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:7808` (`set_clauses.is_empty()` returns before any write) | update_noop_same_value, dep_add_duplicate, label_add_existing, label_remove_absent, dep_remove_absent, reopen_open, undefer_not_deferred |
| S4.204 | **`updated_at` becomes the invocation's instant, and only when at least one stored column actually changes.** It is stamped inside the storage update, not supplied by the command, so all ids of one batch receive the same pinned instant in a pinned run. When nothing changes, `updated_at` keeps its old value. | fixture `updated_at":"2026-01-01T10:00:00Z"`, `update proj-mta --status in_progress` at `2026-01-02T03:04:05Z` → `"updated_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z"` · `update proj-mta -p 1` (already P1) → `"updated_at":"2026-01-01T10:00:00Z"` | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:7808`–`7825` | update_status, update_noop_same_value, update_two_ids |
| S4.205 | **A mutation rewrites every record, not only the changed ones.** The export re-emits each issue from the in-memory model, so untouched records lose unknown fields and gain the export defaults. Records whose own fields did not change keep their `updated_at`. This normalization is owned by S2/S5; the mutation surface only guarantees that it happens on every write-back. | `update proj-aaf -p 1` on fixture `precision` → `proj-aaa`…`proj-aah` all re-emitted: `unknown_field` dropped, `"description":""` and `"assignee":""` dropped, labels `["zeta","alpha","alpha"]` → `["alpha","zeta"]`, `…05.1Z` → `…05.100Z`, `12:00:04+02:00` → `10:00:04Z`, absent `source_repo` → `"."`, absent `compaction_level`/`original_size` → `0` | `src/config/mod.rs:4914` (export of the whole store), OQ-002 | edge_precision_rewrite |
| S4.206 | **Store line order after any mutation is ascending byte order of `id`.** New records take their place by id, not at the end. | after `create Full issue` on fixture `basic`: `proj-170, proj-5u2, proj-7vm, proj-b75, proj-bsx, proj-mkh, proj-mta, proj-ptp, proj-uly` · after `create --parent proj-uly`: `…, proj-uly, proj-uly.1` · with three children: `proj-uly, proj-uly.1, proj-uly.1.1, proj-uly.2` | Goldens (the `--- .beads/issues.jsonl ---` dump): `goldens/create_full_json.out:7` (the new `proj-bsx` lands between `proj-b75` at `goldens/create_full_json.out:6` and `proj-mkh` at `goldens/create_full_json.out:8`), `goldens/create_with_parent.out:11` (`proj-uly.1` after `proj-uly`), `goldens/scn_child_ids.out:42`–`goldens/scn_child_ids.out:45` (`proj-uly`, `proj-uly.1`, `proj-uly.1.1`, `proj-uly.2`). Source, the `--no-db` flush `src/config/mod.rs:4914` → export: `src/sync/mod.rs:10619` (`ORDER BY id ASC` over the export ids), `src/sync/mod.rs:11296` with `src/storage/sqlite.rs:15006` (small stores are hydrated by one full scan, also `ORDER BY id ASC`), `src/sync/mod.rs:10634` (batched hydration re-sorts by `id`) (handover to S6) | create_full_json, create_with_parent, scn_child_ids |
| S4.207 | **`.beads/last-touched` is one line: the id followed by one `\n`, and nothing else.** The file is created with mode `0600`, truncated on each write, and written best-effort: a failure to write it never fails the command and never changes the exit code. | `create First issue` → file bytes `proj-fyw\n` | `src/util/mod.rs:86` (`set_last_touched_id`), `src/util/mod.rs:29` (`LAST_TOUCHED_FILE = "last-touched"`) | create_min, update_status, OQ-008 |
| S4.208 | **`last-touched` records the LAST SUCCESSFULLY ACTED-ON id, not the last id on the command line.** Ids that were skipped do not become the last-touched id; when every id was skipped, the file is not written at all. | `update proj-mta proj-5u2 -p 4` → `proj-5u2\n` · `undefer proj-7vm proj-mta` (mta skipped) → `proj-7vm\n` · `reopen proj-mta proj-mkh --reason Redo` (mta skipped) → `proj-mkh\n` · `reopen proj-mta` alone (skipped) → file not written | `src/cli/commands/close.rs:746`, `src/cli/commands/reopen.rs:139`, `src/cli/commands/update.rs:525` | update_two_ids, close_two, reopen_open, (case to add: `undefer_mixed` — `["@fx=basic","undefer","proj-7vm","proj-mta"]`) |
| S4.209 | **`last-touched` is written even when the mutation changed nothing**, for the commands that reach their per-id body: `update` (including the no-change and the no-flag forms), `dep add` of a duplicate edge, `dep remove` of an absent edge, `label add` of an existing label, `label remove` of an absent label. It is NOT written when the command aborts before that point (any refusal) and not by `reopen`/`undefer`/`defer`/`close` skips. | `dep add proj-170 proj-mta` (edge exists) → no store dump, last-touched `proj-170\n` | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:594`, `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:778`, `src/cli/commands/update.rs:525` | update_noop_same_value, dep_add_duplicate, dep_remove_absent, label_add_existing, label_remove_absent |
| S4.210 | **`delete` never writes `last-touched`; it only removes the file**, and only when the stored id is one of the ids just deleted. | `delete proj-5u2` on fixture `basic` (no `last-touched` present) → no last-touched dump at all | `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:329`–`336` (`clear_last_touched` when the stored id is in the deleted set), `src/util/mod.rs:145` | delete_basic, delete_json, delete_cascade, delete_with_dependents_force |
| S4.211 | **`label rename` does not write `last-touched`** although it mutates several issues. | `label rename backend server --json` → store dump, no last-touched dump | Golden: `goldens/label_rename.out:2` is the only dump marker (`--- .beads/issues.jsonl ---`); the ten-line file holds no `--- .beads/last-touched ---` marker. Source: `label_rename` spans `src/cli/commands/label.rs:597`–`src/cli/commands/label.rs:693` (mutation at `src/cli/commands/label.rs:639`, flush at `src/cli/commands/label.rs:667`) and holds no `set_last_touched_id` call; the file's only two calls are `src/cli/commands/label.rs:253` (`label add`) and `src/cli/commands/label.rs:334` (`label remove`) | label_rename |
| S4.212 | **Id resolution is all-or-nothing and happens before any mutation.** When any positional id of a multi-id `update`, `close`, `delete` (or the single id of the other commands) cannot be resolved, the whole command fails with `Error: Issue not found: <input>` on stderr, hint `Hint: Run 'br list' to see available issues.`, exit **3**, and the store is not rewritten — even when the other ids were resolvable. An id that matches several issues fails the same way with `Error: Ambiguous ID '<input>': matches ["<id>", …]` and hint `Hint: Provide more characters of the ID`, exit **3**. | `update proj-mta proj-zzz -p 0` → nothing written, exit 3 · `update m -p 1` → ambiguous, exit 3 | `src/util/id.rs:1002`, `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:123`–`128`, `src/cli/commands/close.rs:874` | error_update_missing, error_close_missing, error_delete_missing, error_reopen_missing, error_comments_add_missing, error_dep_add_missing, error_update_ambiguous, (case to add: `error_update_two_ids_one_missing` — `["@fx=basic","update","proj-mta","proj-zzz","-p","0"]`) |
| S4.213 | **Precondition *skips* are different from id-resolution failures: they are per id and the batch continues.** A skipped id produces a warning and the remaining ids are still mutated and written back. This applies to `close`, `reopen`, `undefer` and `defer`. | `close proj-mkh proj-mta` → `proj-mta` closed and written, `proj-mkh` skipped, exit 3 | `src/cli/commands/close.rs:915`–`1031`, `src/cli/commands/reopen.rs:247`–`269` | close_already_closed, error_close_blocked, close_epic_with_open_child, (case to add: `close_partial_skip` — `["@fx=basic","close","proj-mkh","proj-mta"]`) |
| S4.214 | **Events are not observable in `--no-db` mode.** Every mutation records audit events (`Updated`, `StatusChanged`, `Closed`, `Reopened`, `Deleted`, `DependencyAdded`, `DependencyRemoved`, `Commented`) and, for `close` under a policy file, `close_metadata` rows. None of these are part of the JSONL record, so no golden shows them and the port is not required to produce them. The only mutation-recorded artifact that DOES reach the JSONL is a comment (S4.315, S4.380). Likewise `content_hash` is recomputed on every change but is never exported. | `update proj-mta --status in_progress` → the store line gains no event or hash field | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:1820` (`record_field_change`), `src/storage/sqlite.rs:7822` (`content_hash` recomputed), fixture lines carry no `content_hash` | update_status, close_basic |
| S4.215 | **Exit-code families used by the mutation surface.** `0` success (including a preview and a no-op); `2` argument-parser refusal; `3` issue/operational (`ISSUE_NOT_FOUND`, `AMBIGUOUS_ID`, `NOTHING_TO_DO`, `CLOSE_INCOMPLETE`); `4` validation (`VALIDATION_FAILED`, `INVALID_PRIORITY`, `INVALID_STATUS`, `INVALID_TYPE`); `5` dependency (`CYCLE_DETECTED`, `SELF_DEPENDENCY`, `HAS_DEPENDENTS`, `DUPLICATE_DEPENDENCY`, `DEPENDENCY_NOT_FOUND`); `6` sync/JSONL; `7` config; `8` I/O. | `create X -p 9` → 4 · `dep add proj-mta proj-170` → 5 · `close proj-170` → 3 | `src/error/structured.rs:233`–`276` | error_create_bad_priority, error_dep_add_cycle, error_close_blocked, usage_update_alone |
| S4.216 | **Plain-mode messages go to the streams the goldens show:** successes and previews on stdout (with the glyphs `✓` U+2713, `⊘` U+2298, `⏱` U+23F1 and a following space); `Warning: …` and `Error: …`/`Hint: …` on stderr. In JSON mode the machine payload is printed to stdout *before* an error is raised, so a partially-failing command prints both a payload line and the pretty `{"error":{…}}` block. | `close proj-170 --json` → `{"closed":[],"skipped":[…]}` then the pretty error block, exit 3 | `src/cli/commands/close.rs:750`–`798` | error_close_blocked_json, close_already_closed |
| S4.217 | **No mutating command creates `.beads/` or any file other than `issues.jsonl` and `last-touched` within this surface.** When `.beads/` does not exist, the command fails at workspace discovery before touching anything. | `@fx=none create X` → workspace error, nothing created | Golden: `goldens/error_no_workspace_create.err:1`–`goldens/error_no_workspace_create.err:2` (`Error: Beads not initialized: run 'br init' first` / `Hint: Run: br init`), `goldens/error_no_workspace_create.exit:1` is `2`, `goldens/error_no_workspace_create.out` is zero bytes — no dump marker, so neither `issues.jsonl` nor `last-touched` came into existence (`scripts/ws_inner.py:133`, `dump_changes`, reports a file that appears). Source: `src/config/mod.rs:502` (`discover_beads_dir_with_cli`), `src/config/mod.rs:365` (the walk ends in `NotInitialized`); its call sites on this path are `src/main.rs:1010` (startup) and `src/cli/commands/create.rs:128` (`create`, ahead of opening the store) | error_no_workspace_create, OQ-005, OQ-008 |
| S4.218 | **Tombstoned issues are immutable to `update`.** An `update` naming an issue whose status is `tombstone` fails with `Error: Validation failed: issue_id: cannot update tombstone issue: <id>`, exit **4**, store unchanged. | — | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:7431`–`7436` | (case to add: `error_update_tombstone` — a two-step scenario: `delete` then `update` the same id) |
| S4.219 | **Only `blocks`, `conditional-blocks`, `waits-for` and parent→child propagation make an issue "blocked" for the purposes of the mutation guards** (`update --claim`, `close` without `--force`). `related`, `discovered-from`, `replies-to`, `relates-to`, `duplicates`, `supersedes` and `caused-by` never block. An epic additionally rolls up its own still-open children, which blocks `close` but NOT `--claim` (the claim guard uses start-blockers, which omit the child rollup). | `update proj-170 --claim` → refused, blocker `proj-mta` (a `blocks` edge) · `update proj-uly --claim` after its child is open → permitted | `src/cli/commands/update.rs:1268`–`1275` (`get_start_blockers`), `src/storage/sqlite.rs:11336`–`11346` | error_update_blocked_claim, error_close_blocked, close_epic_with_open_child, scn_lifecycle |

### S4.220–S4.249 `create`

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.220 | **Defaults of a bare `create <title>`.** The new record carries `status` `open`, `priority` `2`, `issue_type` `task`, `created_at` = `updated_at` = the invocation instant, `created_by` = the resolved actor (S4.200), `source_repo` = the workspace directory's basename, `source_repo_path` = the absolute workspace directory, `compaction_level` `0`, `original_size` `0`. No other field is set. | `create "First issue"` at `2026-01-02T03:04:05Z` in `/mnt/proj` → `{"id":"proj-fyw","title":"First issue","status":"open","priority":2,"issue_type":"task","created_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z","created_by":"tester","updated_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z","source_repo":"proj","source_repo_path":"/mnt/proj","compaction_level":0,"original_size":0}` | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:484`–`535`, `src/cli/commands/create.rs:42` (`canonical_source_repo`), `src/cli/commands/create.rs:143`–`144` | create_min, create_min_json |
| S4.221 | `source_repo` is the last path component of the directory that holds `.beads/`; `source_repo_path` is that directory's absolute path. Both are computed once per invocation from the discovered beads directory, never from the record. | workspace `/mnt/proj` → `"source_repo":"proj"`, `"source_repo_path":"/mnt/proj"` | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:42`, `src/sync/mod.rs` (`canonical_source_repo_path`) | create_min, where_plain |
| S4.222 | **Title.** The positional `<title>` and `--title <t>` are mutually exclusive at the parser; the positional wins when both reach the logic. The title is stored verbatim (leading/trailing spaces are NOT trimmed off the stored value), but validation rejects a title whose trimmed form is empty and a title longer than 500 **characters** (Unicode scalar count, not bytes). | `create --title "Via flag"` → `"title":"Via flag"` · `create ""` and `create "   "` → refused · 500 `t` → accepted, 501 `t` → refused | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:406`–`414`, `src/validation/mod.rs:161`–`167` (`TITLE_MAX_CHARS` = 500) | create_title_flag, error_create_empty_title, error_create_blank_title, error_create_long_title, edge_create_max_title |
| S4.223 | **`-t/--type` accepts any string**, including types outside the built-in set; the value is stored verbatim in `issue_type`. There is no refusal for an unknown issue type at create time. | `create "Custom typed" -t spike` → `"issue_type":"spike"` | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:424`–`428`, `src/validation/mod.rs:272` (`validate_custom_issue_type`) | create_custom_type, create_full_json |
| S4.224 | **`-p/--priority` accepts the bare digit and the `P`-prefixed form**, case-insensitively, and stores the integer. Out-of-range values are refused (S4.239). | `-p 0` → `"priority":0` · `-p P3` → `"priority":3` | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:417`–`421` | create_full_json, create_priority_p_form |
| S4.225 | **`-d/--description` stores the value verbatim** including newlines, tabs and control characters; JSON encoding escapes them. `--description-file <PATH>` reads the file's entire content verbatim instead (`-` means stdin) and is mutually exclusive with `-d`; the guard message is `Validation failed: description_file: cannot be combined with --description`. The file is read exactly once, before any mutation. | `-d "quote \" slash \\ bell ␇ del ␡ nbsp  "` → `"description":"quote \" slash \\ bell \u0007 del \u007f nbsp  "` as the golden's bytes show | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:340` (`read_description_file`), `src/cli/commands/create.rs:366` (`resolve_create_description`) | create_unicode, (case to add: `create_description_file` — needs a fixture-side file) |
| S4.226 | `-a/--assignee` sets `assignee`; `--owner` sets `owner`. Both are stored verbatim. | `-a carol --owner dave` → `"assignee":"carol","owner":"dave"` | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:500`–`501` | create_full_json |
| S4.227 | **`-l/--labels` is comma-split and repeatable.** Each value is trimmed; empty pieces are dropped; duplicates are dropped keeping the first occurrence. Every surviving label is validated (S4.239) before anything is written. The stored array is then sorted and deduplicated by the export. | `-l one,two` → `"labels":["one","two"]` | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:689`–`696` (validation), `src/cli/commands/create.rs:773`–`779` (population), `src/cli/mod.rs:1089` (`value_delimiter = ','`) | create_full_json, error_create_bad_label |
| S4.228 | **`--parent <id>` does two things: it changes the id scheme, and it adds one `parent-child` edge on the CHILD.** The new id is `<resolved parent id>.<n>` where `n` is the next free child number (1 for the first child); nesting repeats the rule. The edge is written into the child's own `dependencies` array with `issue_id` = the child, `depends_on_id` = the parent, `type` `parent-child`. **The parent's record is not modified at all** — its `updated_at` is unchanged. | `create "Child task" --parent proj-uly` → id `proj-uly.1`, one edge `proj-uly.1 → proj-uly (parent-child)`, `proj-uly` byte-identical · second child → `proj-uly.2` · `--parent proj-uly.1` → `proj-uly.1.1` | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:600`–`625` (`generate_new_id`), `src/cli/commands/create.rs:781`–`791` (`populate_relations`) | create_with_parent, scn_child_ids |
| S4.229 | **`--deps` is comma-split and repeatable; each element is `<id>` or `<type>:<id>`.** A bare id means type `blocks`. `blocked-by:` is accepted and stored as `blocks`. The type must be one of the eleven standard names, otherwise the create is refused with `Validation failed: deps: Unknown dependency type: '<t>'. Allowed types: blocks, blocked-by, parent-child, conditional-blocks, waits-for, related, discovered-from, replies-to, relates-to, duplicates, supersedes, caused-by` (note this list includes `blocked-by`, unlike `dep add`'s list). Each target id is resolved; an unresolvable one fails the whole create. Several edges are written in argv order into the child's array, then sorted by the export. | `--deps proj-mta` → one edge `proj-tne → proj-mta (blocks)` · `--deps "parent-child:proj-uly,related:proj-mta"` → two edges, exported ordered `proj-mta (related)` then `proj-uly (parent-child)` | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:718`–`755` (`parse_create_dependency`), `src/cli/commands/create.rs:793`–`806` | create_with_deps, (case to add: `create_deps_typed` — `["@fx=basic","create","Multi","--deps","parent-child:proj-uly,related:proj-mta","--json"]`) |
| S4.230 | **`-e/--estimate` sets `estimated_minutes` (an integer)**, `--due` sets `due_at`, `--defer` sets `defer_until`, `--external-ref` sets `external_ref`. `--due`/`--defer` take the same date grammar as `defer --until` (S4.322): a bare `YYYY-MM-DD` resolves to **09:00:00** of that day in the process time zone. `--defer` does NOT change `status`; the created issue stays `open` with a `defer_until` set. | `-e 90 --due 2026-03-01 --external-ref gh-42` → `"estimated_minutes":90`, `"due_at":"2026-03-01T09:00:00Z"`, `"external_ref":"gh-42"` · `--defer 2027-01-01` → `"status":"open"`, `"defer_until":"2027-01-01T09:00:00Z"` | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:429`–`430`, `src/cli/commands/create.rs:498`–`507` | create_full_json, (case to add: `create_defer` — `["create","Defer me","--defer","2027-01-01","--json"]`) |
| S4.231 | **`-s/--status` sets the initial status.** `in_progress` is stored plainly. `closed` additionally sets `closed_at` to the create instant but leaves `close_reason` unset. `tombstone` additionally sets `deleted_at` to the create instant and `deleted_by` to the actor, but leaves `delete_reason` and `original_type` unset. All are written to the store (there is no `br update`-style terminal-state refusal on `create`). | `-s in_progress` → `"status":"in_progress"` · `-s closed` → `"status":"closed","closed_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z"`, no `close_reason` · `-s tombstone` → `"status":"tombstone","deleted_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z","deleted_by":"tester"`, no `delete_reason`/`original_type` | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:446`–`486`, `src/cli/commands/create.rs:518`–`524` | create_status_in_progress, (case to add: `create_status_closed` — `["create","Closed start","-s","closed","--json"]`; `create_status_tombstone` — `["create","Tombstone start","-s","tombstone","--json"]`) |
| S4.232 | **`--acceptance-criteria` (alias `--acceptance`) and `--prerequisites` are stored verbatim** in `acceptance_criteria` / `prerequisites`. `--agent-context` takes inline JSON, `@path.json`, or `@path.yaml`/`@path.yml` (converted to JSON); an empty string leaves the field unset. It is parsed and normalized to canonical JSON **before** any record is created, so an invalid context leaves no record behind. Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-058): the inline value is stored as serde_json prints it back: compact, keys in INPUT order, `1.50` as `1.5`, `-0` as `-0.0`, `1e3` as `1000.0`, `2.0` kept, a u64 integer kept, `\u00e9` decoded to `é`; `42` is stored as `"42"`; `""` stores nothing. Invalid inline JSON is `CONFIG_ERROR`, exit 7: `Configuration error: agent-context: inline argument is not valid JSON: key must be a string at line 1 column 2 (hint: use `--agent-context @path/to/instructions.yaml` for a file, or pass an empty string to clear)`, JSON `hint` null, `retryable` false, `context` null. The port prints floats only up to 15 significant digits with the decimal point between -3 and 15 places, and answers its own failure beyond that and for `@path` (a file read). | `--acceptance-criteria "It works."` → `"acceptance_criteria":"It works."` | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:504`–`505`, `src/cli/commands/create.rs:434`–`437`, `src/cli/commands/update.rs:1866` (`agent_context_update_from_arg`) | create_full_json, (case to add: `create_prerequisites`, `create_agent_context_bad` — `["create","X","--agent-context","{not json"]`), create_agent_context, create_agent_context_scalar, create_agent_context_empty, error_create_agent_context, error_create_agent_context_json |
| S4.233 | **`--slug <text>` changes the id shape only:** the id becomes `<prefix>-<normalized slug>-<hash>`. Normalization lowercases to ASCII alphanumerics, collapses every run of other characters to a single hyphen, strips leading and trailing hyphens, and caps the result at 48 characters. A `--parent` id ignores the slug entirely. Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-031, OQ-059): the 48-character cap gives `proj-abcdefghij-abcdefghij-abcdefghij-abcdefghij-abcd-u5m`; a slug that normalizes to empty gives the plain id (`create_slug_long`, `create_slug_empty`). | `--slug "Survey My Thing!!"` → id `proj-survey-my-thing-p00` | `src/cli/mod.rs:1048`–`1056`, `src/cli/commands/create.rs:598` (`generate_new_id`) | (case to add: `create_slug` — `["create","Sluggy","--slug","Survey My Thing!!","--json"]`), create_slug_long |

| S4.235 | **`--dry-run` writes nothing at all** — no store, no `last-touched` — and still allocates and prints the id the create would have used. Plain output is four to eight lines in a fixed order: `Dry run: would create issue <id>`, `Title: <title>`, `Type: <type>`, `Priority: P<n>`, then `Labels: <a, b>` when `-l` was given, `Parent: <id>` when `--parent` was given, `Dependencies: <raw --deps values>` when `--deps` was given. In JSON mode the in-memory record is printed instead, which **omits `original_size`** (it carries `compaction_level` but no `original_size`, unlike a real create, because the dry-run path serializes the model rather than the export view). | `create "Not written" --dry-run` → `Dry run: would create issue proj-8h9\nTitle: Not written\nType: task\nPriority: P2\n` · `--dry-run --json` → `{…,"compaction_level":0}` with no `original_size` | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:161`–`208`, `src/cli/commands/create.rs:558`–`561` | create_dry_run, create_dry_run_json, (case to add: `create_dry_run_full` — `["@fx=basic","create","Dry labels","--dry-run","-l","a,b","--parent","proj-uly","--deps","proj-mta"]`) |

| S4.237 | **`-f/--file <PATH>` switches `create` into a bulk markdown import** and is refused together with a title (`Validation failed: file: cannot be combined with title arguments`) or with `--external-ref` (`Validation failed: external_ref: --external-ref is not supported with --file`). The importer reads `## Title` headings and the first paragraph after each as the description, creates one issue per heading with the same defaults and the same `source_repo`/`source_repo_path`, resolves deferred parent and dependency references after all issues exist, and writes back once at the end; `last-touched` receives the LAST created id. An empty parse result prints an empty JSON array in JSON mode and nothing otherwise, and writes nothing. Measured (2026-09-23): the title conflict is judged first (`Validation failed: file: cannot be combined with title arguments`), then a name without the `.md` or `.markdown` extension (`Validation failed: file: must have .md or .markdown extension`, also for `-` and `/dev/null`), then a missing file (`Validation failed: file: file not found: nope.md`), all exit 4 with `context` `{"field","reason"}` in JSON. No case reaches the import itself: the sandbox stages no `.md` file (OQ-051). | — Corrected and measured (2026-09-24, OQ-051, harness mode `@file`): the import reads lines; `# ` lines and text before the first `## ` are ignored; each `## <title>` (trimmed) is one issue, in heading order; before any section, the FIRST NON-BLANK LINE after the heading is the description (not the paragraph); a `### <name>` section (name trimmed, case-insensitive) runs to the next heading and its value is its lines joined and trimmed as one text, empty meaning absent; `description` replaces the first line; `design` and `acceptance criteria` keep their lines; `assignee`; `type` lowercased, any word kept; `priority` trimmed, upper-cased, one leading `P` dropped, a digit 0–4 or the heading is refused with `✗ Failed to create <title>: Priority must be 0-4, got: <that text>` on stderr while the others are created; `labels` takes comma lists and `-`/`*` bullet lines; `notes`, `estimate`, `status`, `external ref` and unknown names are ignored; `### Parent` names a heading created EARLIER in the file (its exact title) or a stored or created id (full or short): the issue then gets the dotted child id and a `parent-child` edge; the title of a LATER heading creates the issue with a fresh id and adds the edge after every heading exists; anything else refuses that heading with `✗ Failed to resolve parent for <title>: Issue not found: <text>` (exit unchanged). `### Dependencies` and `### Deps` are split on commas and white space (a bullet dropped per line), each word `<type>:<ref>` when the type is known, else a `blocks` reference; a reference is a heading's exact title anywhere in the file, else an id; an unresolved one warns `warning: unresolved dependency '<word>' for issue <id>: Issue not found: <word>` and the run exits 5. The edges are added after all headings, in heading order, each checked for a cycle: the first refused one is announced as `warning: bulk dependency import failed (Cycle detected in dependencies: <path from its least id>); retrying dependencies one by one`, and each refused edge as `warning: failed to add dependency <from> → <to>: Cycle detected in dependencies: Adding dependency <from> -> <to> would create a cycle`; exit 5. Every edge carries the actor as `created_by`; the records carry none. The id seed is the title, the final description and an EMPTY creator, and the records carry no `created_by`. Plain output: `✓ Created <n> issues from <path>:` (`1 issues` too) and `  <id>: <title>` per issue; `--json`: one compact array of the created records. Exit 0. | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:110`–`122`, `src/cli/commands/create.rs:820`–`1430` | (case to add: `create_from_file` — needs a fixture-side markdown file; `error_create_file_with_title` — `["create","X","-f","plan.md"]`), stdin_create_file, stdin_create_file_json, error_create_file_with_title, error_create_file_missing, create_file_empty, create_file_md, create_file_md_json, create_file_md_edges, create_file_md_rules, create_file_md_one, create_file_md_parent, create_file_md_parent_json, create_file_md_parent_store, create_file_md_deps, create_file_md_deps_json, create_file_md_cycle, create_file_md_cycle3, create_file_md_mixed |
| S4.238 | **Validation order for `create`.** In order: `--file` conflicts; workspace discovery; strict-workflow status check; description resolution (`--description-file` read); priority parse; type parse; `--due`/`--defer` parse; `--agent-context` parse; `--parent` resolution (an unresolvable parent fails here); `--status` parse; id generation; issue-level validation (title empty, title length, priority range, `created_at ≤ updated_at`, estimate range, NUL bytes, `external_ref` ≤ 200 chars and no whitespace, label charset and count); relation validation (each `-l` label's charset, `--parent` ≠ self, each `--deps` type and self-reference); relation population; `--dry-run` early return; the store write; the write-back; `last-touched`. Nothing is written until every check above has passed. | `create Orphan --parent proj-zzz` → parent failure before any record exists | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:110`–`166`, `src/cli/commands/create.rs:405`–`560`, `src/validation/mod.rs:77`–`160` | error_create_missing_parent, error_create_bad_priority, error_create_bad_label |
| S4.239 | **`create` refusals, verbatim.** Priority out of range: stderr `Error: Priority must be 0-4, got: 9` + `Hint: Priority must be 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 4=backlog); list filters also accept ranges and comma lists such as 0-1 or 0,2.`, exit **4**; in JSON mode the pretty block with `"code": "INVALID_PRIORITY"`, the same message and hint, `"retryable": true`, `"context": {"priority": "9", "hint": …}`. Empty or all-blank title: stderr `Error: Validation failed: title: cannot be empty`, exit **4**, no hint line. Over-length title: `Error: Validation failed: title: exceeds 500 characters`, exit **4**. Bad label: `Error: Validation failed: label: invalid characters (only alphanumeric, hyphen, underscore, colon allowed)`, exit **4**. Unknown parent: `Error: Issue not found: proj-zzz` + `Hint: Run 'br list' to see available issues.`, exit **3**. In every case the store is untouched and `last-touched` is not written. | see the cases | `src/validation/mod.rs:164`, `:167`, `:441`; `src/error/structured.rs` | error_create_bad_priority, error_create_bad_priority_json, error_create_empty_title, error_create_blank_title, error_create_long_title, error_create_bad_label, error_create_missing_parent |

| S4.290 | **Step order.** `--bypass-policy`/`--bypass-reason` arity check → workspace discovery → empty-ids fallback to `.beads/last-touched` (same message and exit as S4.260) → the `--suggest-next` single-id check → id resolution (S4.212) → epic child counts → the pre-close blocked snapshot when `--suggest-next` → per-id precondition pass (skips collected) → the policy-gate pass → one atomic update of every surviving id → one write-back → `last-touched` → output → exit status. | — | `src/cli/commands/close.rs:639`–`803`, `src/cli/commands/close.rs:846`–`1346` | close_basic, scn_last_touched, usage_close_alone |
| S4.291 | **Fields written on a successful close:** `status` = `closed`; `closed_at` = the invocation instant; `close_reason` = the `--reason` value or, when absent, the literal `done` — it is always written; `closed_by_session` = the `--session` value when given, otherwise untouched; `updated_at` = the invocation instant. Nothing else changes: dependencies, labels, comments and `defer_until` are all preserved. | `close proj-mta` → `"status":"closed","closed_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z","close_reason":"done"` · `--reason Shipped` → `"close_reason":"Shipped"` · `--session sess-1` → additionally `"closed_by_session":"sess-1"` · closing a deferred issue keeps its `defer_until` | `src/cli/commands/close.rs:1135`–`1150`, `src/model/mod.rs:536` | close_basic, close_reason_json, (case to add: `close_session` — `["@fx=basic","close","proj-mta","--session","sess-1","--json"]`; `close_deferred` — `["@fx=basic","close","proj-7vm"]`) |
| S4.292 | **Per-id skip reasons, verbatim.** `issue not found`; `already <status>` for any terminal status (`already closed`, `already tombstone`); `<label> has <open>/<total> open children (use --force to close anyway)` where `<label>` is `epic` when the issue's type is `epic` and `parent issue` otherwise, `<open>` is `total − closed`; `<label> has <n> open dot-notation child issue(s): <first five ids joined by ", ">[, +<n−5> more] (use --force to close anyway)`; `blocked by: <blocker ids joined by ", "> — close the open blocker(s) first, or use --force to close anyway` (and, when the blocker list is empty, `blocked by dependencies — close the open blocker(s) first, or use --force to close anyway`). Each becomes `Warning: Skipped <id>: <reason>` on stderr in plain mode and `{"id":…,"reason":…}` inside `"skipped"` in JSON mode. Measured (2026-09-22): the dot-notation reason is judged after the open-children one and before the blockers, and only when no `parent-child` edge names the children (an edge-linked child gives the `<open>/<total>` reason instead); a dot-notation child is a DIRECT one, `<id>.<part>` with no further `.` (the grandchild `proj-b75.2.1` neither counts nor holds the close back), whose status is not `closed` or `tombstone`; the ids are listed in byte order (`proj-b75.1, proj-b75.10, proj-b75.11, proj-b75.2, …`); `--force` waives it (`scn_close_dotted`, `scn_close_dotted_many`). | `close proj-170` → `Warning: Skipped proj-170: blocked by: proj-mta — close the open blocker(s) first, or use --force to close anyway` · `close proj-uly` → `Warning: Skipped proj-uly: epic has 1/1 open children (use --force to close anyway)` · `close proj-mkh` → `Warning: Skipped proj-mkh: already closed` | `src/cli/commands/close.rs:930`, `:940`, `:957`–`961`, `:997`–`1002`, `:1106`–`1111` | error_close_blocked, close_epic_with_open_child, close_already_closed, scn_close_dotted, scn_close_dotted_many, scn_r9_dotted_modes |
| S4.293 | **When EVERY id was skipped the command fails with `NOTHING_TO_DO`, exit 3, and writes nothing.** stderr carries the per-id warnings, then `Error: Nothing to do: all <n> issue(s) skipped — <summary>` and one hint. `<summary>` lists up to five `<id>: <reason>` pairs joined by `"; "`, with `+<n−5> more` appended beyond five. | `close proj-170` → `Error: Nothing to do: all 1 issue(s) skipped — proj-170: blocked by: proj-mta — close the open blocker(s) first, or use --force to close anyway` | `src/cli/commands/close.rs:789`–`792`, `:812`–`830`, `src/error/mod.rs:275` | error_close_blocked, error_close_blocked_json, close_already_closed, close_epic_with_open_child |
| S4.294 | **When some ids closed and some were skipped the command still WRITES the closures and then fails with `CLOSE_INCOMPLETE`, exit 3.** The message is `Error: Partially applied: <closed> closed, <skipped> skipped — <summary>`. This is the one place in the mutation surface where a non-zero exit accompanies a rewritten store. | `close proj-mkh proj-mta` → `✓ Closed proj-mta: Set up database schema (done)` on stdout, the store rewritten with `proj-mta` closed, stderr `Warning: Skipped proj-mkh: already closed` and `Error: Partially applied: 1 closed, 1 skipped — proj-mkh: already closed`, exit 3 | `src/cli/commands/close.rs:793`–`798`, `src/error/mod.rs:288` | (case to add: `close_partial_skip` — `["@fx=basic","close","proj-mkh","proj-mta"]`) |

Close the blockers first, or re-run with --force to close anyway.`; otherwise if it contains `open children` or `child issue` → `Hint: Skipped issue(s) have open children. Close the children first, or re-run with --force to close anyway.`; otherwise → `Hint: Skipped issue(s) were already closed or not found.`. A mixed batch therefore always shows the blocked hint. | `close proj-170` → the blocked hint · `close proj-uly` → the children hint · `close proj-mkh` → the already-closed hint | `src/error/structured.rs:1055`–`1066` | error_close_blocked, close_epic_with_open_child, close_already_closed |
| S4.296 | **`--force` waives the open-children checks and the blocker check, and nothing else.** It does not waive "issue not found" or "already closed". | `close proj-170 --force --json` → `[{"id":"proj-170","title":"Implement user auth","status":"closed","closed_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05+00:00","close_reason":"done"}]`, store written, exit 0 | `src/cli/commands/close.rs:946`, `:1012`–`1017` | close_blocked_force |
| S4.297 | **`--suggest-next` is restricted to one target id** (`Validation failed: suggest-next: --suggest-next only works with a single issue ID`, exit 4) — the check runs after the last-touched fallback, so a bare `close --suggest-next` is legal. The computed set is: the blocked-id set taken **before** the write, minus the blocked-id set taken **after** the write, keeping only candidates whose status is then `open` or `in_progress`. Blockedness uses the edge types of S4.219. Each is emitted as `{"id","title","priority"}`. Plain mode prints `Unblocked <n> issue(s):` and then `  <id>: <title>` per row. | `close proj-mta --suggest-next --json` → `{"closed":[{"id":"proj-mta","title":"Set up database schema","status":"closed","closed_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05+00:00","close_reason":"done"}],"unblocked":[{"id":"proj-170","title":"Implement user auth","priority":1}]}` | `src/cli/commands/close.rs:654`–`658`, `:893`–`903`, `:1247`–`1313` | close_suggest_next |
| S4.298 | **`close` output shapes.** Plain success per id: `✓ Closed <id>: <title> (<close_reason>)` — the reason is always shown, `done` included. With `--suggest-next` the payload is the object of S4.297. With a skip present, JSON is `{"closed":[…],"skipped":[{"id","reason"},…]}`. When nothing at all was closable the plain line is `No issues to close.`. | `close proj-mta --reason Shipped --json` → `[{…,"closed_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05+00:00","close_reason":"Shipped"}]` while the store holds `"closed_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z"` | `src/cli/commands/close.rs:750`–`780`, `:1216` | close_basic, close_reason_json, close_two, close_suggest_next, error_close_blocked_json |
