| S4.312 | **`--reason <text>` is stored as a COMMENT, not a field.** The comment's text is exactly `Reopened: <reason>`; its author is the resolved actor and its `created_at` is the invocation instant. The reason is also echoed in the plain success line. Without `--reason` no comment is created. | `reopen proj-mkh --reason Regressed --json` → the record gains `"comments":[{"id":2,"issue_id":"proj-mkh","author":"tester","text":"Reopened: Regressed","created_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z"}]` · `reopen proj-mkh` → no comment | `src/cli/commands/reopen.rs:288`–`290`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:7541`–`7546` | reopen_closed_json, reopen_closed |

Plain output is `⊘ Skipped <id>: <reason>` (U+2298 then one space) on stdout; the store is not rewritten and `last-touched` is not written. **Unlike `close`, an all-skipped `reopen` still exits 0** — there is no `NOTHING_TO_DO` on this path. When nothing at all happened the plain line is `No issues to reopen.`. Resolved (2026-09-23, OQ-093): an id that resolves to nothing is the `Issue not found` error before the batch, exit 3 (`error_reopen_missing`), not an `issue not found` skip. | `reopen proj-mta` → `⊘ Skipped proj-mta: already open\n`, exit **0**, no dumps · `reopen proj-7vm` (deferred) → `⊘ Skipped proj-7vm: already deferred`, exit 0 | `src/cli/commands/reopen.rs:247`, `:255`–`258`, `:264`–`269`, `:190`–`194` | reopen_open, (case to add: `reopen_deferred` — `["@fx=basic","reopen","proj-7vm"]`) |

### S4.320–S4.329 `defer` / `undefer`

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.320 | **`defer` and `undefer` require explicit ids — there is NO last-touched fallback.** With none: `Error: Validation failed: ids: at least one issue ID is required`, exit **4**, nothing written. (Contrast `update`/`close`/`reopen`, S4.260.) | `defer` alone → exit 4 · `undefer` alone → exit 4 | `src/cli/mod.rs:2624`–`2630`, `src/cli/commands/defer.rs` | (case to add: `error_defer_no_ids` — `["@fx=basic","defer"]`; `error_undefer_no_ids` — `["@fx=basic","undefer"]`) |
| S4.321 | **A successful `defer` sets `status` to `deferred`, sets `defer_until` when `--until` was given, and bumps `updated_at`.** Deferring an already-deferred issue with a new `--until` changes only `defer_until` and `updated_at` (`status` is already `deferred`); the JSON reports `"previous_status":"deferred"`. | `defer proj-mta --until 2027-06-01 --json` → `"status":"deferred"`, `"defer_until":"2027-06-01T09:00:00Z"` · `defer proj-7vm --until 2028-01-01` on the already-deferred fixture issue → only `defer_until` and `updated_at` change | `src/cli/commands/defer.rs` | defer_until_date, defer_relative, (case to add: `defer_already_deferred` — `["@fx=basic","defer","proj-7vm","--until","2028-01-01","--json"]`) |
| S4.322 | **`--until` grammar and the 09:00 default.** Accepted forms, tried in this order: RFC 3339 (a `+00:00:00`-style zero offset is repaired); a bare date `YYYY-MM-DD`; a relative offset `+<n><unit>` or `-<n><unit>` with unit `m`, `h`, `d` or `w`; and the case-insensitive keywords `today`, `yesterday`, `tomorrow`, `next-week`/`nextweek`. **A bare date and the keywords `yesterday`/`tomorrow`/`next-week` resolve to 09:00:00 LOCAL time of that day; `today` resolves to 17:00:00 local time.** A relative offset is added to the invocation instant. The stored value is normalized to `Z`; the JSON output renders it with a `+00:00` offset. Under the pinned environment (`TZ=UTC`) local equals UTC. A bad unit gives `Validation failed: defer_until: invalid unit (use m, h, d, w)` and an out-of-range offset gives `Validation failed: defer_until: relative duration is out of supported range`. | `--until 2027-06-01` at `2026-01-02T03:04:05Z`, `TZ=UTC` → stored `"2027-06-01T09:00:00Z"`, printed `"2027-06-01T09:00:00+00:00"` · `--until +1h` → stored `"2026-01-02T04:04:05Z"`, printed `"2026-01-02T04:04:05+00:00"` | `src/util/time.rs:35`–`98` (`parse_flexible_timestamp`; the `9, 0, 0` literal is inlined at `:45`, `:77`, `:83`, `:89`, and `17, 0, 0` at `:71`), `src/cli/commands/defer.rs:251`–`256` | defer_until_date, defer_relative |
| S4.323 | **`defer` without `--until` sets `status` to `deferred` and CLEARS `defer_until`** — the update carries an explicit "set to nothing", so an existing `defer_until` is wiped and the key disappears from the record. The plain line names it: `⏱ Deferred <id>: <title> (indefinitely)` (U+23F1 then one space); with `--until` the suffix is ` (until <rfc3339>)`. In JSON, `defer_until` is omitted when absent. | `defer proj-mta` → `"status":"deferred"`, no `defer_until` key, `updated_at` bumped · `defer proj-7vm` (already carrying `defer_until`) → the key is removed | `src/cli/commands/defer.rs:310`–`316`, `:198`–`210` | defer_no_until, (case to add: `defer_clears_until` — `["@fx=basic","defer","proj-7vm","--json"]`) |
| S4.324 | **An invalid `--until` is refused once, before any issue is examined:** `Error: Validation failed: defer_until: invalid time format (try: +1h, -7d, tomorrow, next-week, or 2025-01-15)`, exit **4**, store unchanged, no id touched even when several were named. | `defer proj-mta --until not-a-date` → exit 4 | `src/util/time.rs:53`–`96`, `src/cli/commands/defer.rs:251`–`256` | error_defer_bad_until |
| S4.325 | **A successful `undefer` always REMOVES `defer_until`, and sets `status` to `open` only when the status was `deferred`.** An issue that merely carried a `defer_until` while `in_progress` keeps its status and just loses the field. `updated_at` is bumped either way. Plain line: `✓ Undeferred <id>: <title> (now open)`. JSON: `{"undeferred":[{"id","title","previous_status","status"}]}`. | `undefer proj-7vm --json` → `{"undeferred":[{"id":"proj-7vm","title":"Refactor config loader","previous_status":"deferred","status":"open"}]}`, record loses `defer_until`, `"status":"open"` | `src/cli/commands/defer.rs:607`–`622` | undefer_basic |
| S4.326 | **Per-id skips of `defer`/`undefer`, verbatim.** `defer`: `already deferred` — and only when BOTH the status is already `deferred` AND the stored `defer_until` already equals the requested one, so re-deferring to a different instant is a normal update; `cannot defer <status> issue` for a `closed` or `tombstone` issue; `issue not found`. `undefer`: `not deferred (status: <status>)` when the status is not `deferred` and no `defer_until` is stored; `cannot undefer <status> issue` for a terminal issue that still carries a stale `defer_until`; `issue not found`. Each prints `⊘ Skipped <id>: <reason>` on **stdout**, exit **0**; an all-skipped run rewrites nothing and does not write `last-touched`. When literally nothing happened the line is `No issues to defer.` / `No issues to undefer.`. | `undefer proj-mta` → `⊘ Skipped proj-mta: not deferred (status: open)\n`, no dumps | `src/cli/commands/defer.rs:279`–`308`, `:575`–`604`, `:211`–`218`, `:158`–`160`, `:464`–`466` | undefer_not_deferred |
| S4.327 | **Several ids: a skip never stops the rest, successes are printed before skips, one atomic batch and one write-back cover them all, and `last-touched` becomes the last SUCCESSFUL id.** A hard failure on any eligible id (a capacity or workflow refusal) rolls back the whole batch, so no sibling is deferred. | `defer proj-mta proj-5u2` → both deferred, `last-touched` `proj-5u2\n` · `undefer proj-7vm proj-mta` → `✓ Undeferred proj-7vm: …\n⊘ Skipped proj-mta: not deferred (status: open)\n`, `last-touched` `proj-7vm\n` | `src/cli/commands/defer.rs:332`–`339`, `:109`–`156` | (case to add: `defer_two` — `["@fx=basic","defer","proj-mta","proj-5u2"]`; `undefer_mixed` — `["@fx=basic","undefer","proj-7vm","proj-mta"]`) |
| S4.328 | **`defer`/`undefer` accept `--transition-comment <text>`, which adds a comment to each id whose status actually changes**, with the same id rule as S4.382. On `undefer` it is dropped when the status does not change. | — | `src/cli/mod.rs:2637`–`2639`, `src/cli/commands/defer.rs:614`–`617` | (case to add: `defer_transition_comment`) |

### S4.330–S4.349 `delete`

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.330 | **`delete` is a TOMBSTONE, not a removal: the record stays in the store.** A successful soft delete sets `status` to `tombstone`, `deleted_at` to the invocation instant, `deleted_by` to the resolved actor, `delete_reason` to the `--reason` value (default the literal `delete`), `original_type` to the issue's `issue_type` **as it was before**, and bumps `updated_at`. `issue_type` itself is left unchanged, as are `title`, `description`, `priority`, `assignee`, `created_at`, `labels` and `comments`. | `delete proj-5u2` → `{"id":"proj-5u2","title":"Write API docs","status":"tombstone","priority":3,"issue_type":"docs",…,"updated_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z","source_repo":"proj","source_repo_path":"/mnt/proj","deleted_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z","deleted_by":"tester","delete_reason":"delete","original_type":"docs","compaction_level":0,"original_size":0}` · `--reason Duplicate` → `"delete_reason":"Duplicate"` | `src/cli/mod.rs:1412`–`1414` (`default_value = "delete"`), `src/storage/sqlite.rs:7892`–`7983` | delete_basic, delete_json, delete_cascade |
| S4.331 | **Every dependency edge touching a deleted issue is removed, in BOTH directions and for ALL edge types**, before the tombstone is written. The deleted issue's own `dependencies` array disappears; every other issue that pointed at it loses that edge and has its `updated_at` bumped to the same instant, and is therefore rewritten. | `delete proj-5u2` → `proj-5u2`'s `related` edge to `proj-170` gone · `delete proj-mta --force` → `proj-170`'s `blocks` edge to `proj-mta` gone, `proj-170.updated_at` bumped, its `parent-child` edge kept · `delete proj-mta --cascade` → `proj-5u2` loses its `related` edge to the cascaded `proj-170` and is rewritten | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:12812`–`12880` (`remove_all_dependencies`), `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:276`–`290` | delete_basic, delete_with_dependents_force, delete_cascade |
| S4.332 | **"Dependents" for the refusal check are: every issue with a `blocks`/`conditional-blocks`/`waits-for` edge pointing AT a deleted id, plus the PARENT named by a `parent-child` edge whose child is a deleted id.** Ids already in the delete set are excluded; the list is sorted and deduplicated. `related` and the other non-blocking types never count. | `delete proj-mta` → dependents `[proj-170]` (a `blocks` edge) · `delete proj-5u2` → no dependents, so it deletes straight away | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:14326`–`14347` (`get_blocked_issue_ids`), `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:898`–`917` | delete_with_dependents, delete_basic |
| S4.333 | **With dependents present and neither `--force` nor `--cascade`, `delete` is a PREVIEW that writes nothing and exits 0.** Plain output, verbatim: `The following issues depend on issues being deleted:` then `  - <id>` per dependent; then, when the transitive closure is larger, a blank line, `<n> additional issue(s) would be transitively affected by --cascade:` and `  - <id>` per extra; then a blank line, `Use --force to orphan these dependents, or --cascade to delete them recursively.`, `--cascade would delete <n> total dependent(s).` and `No changes made (preview mode).`. Measured (2026-09-22): the preview wins over `--hard` and `--dry-run` alike (`delete_hard_preview`). Under `--json` the preview is one compact line, `{"preview":true,"would_delete":[<named ids, ascending>],"cascade_delete":[<the closure beyond the named ids>],"blocked_dependents":[<the direct dependents>],"orphaned_issues":[]}`, exit 0 (`delete_preview_json`, `delete_preview_json_many`). | `delete proj-mta` → the six-block preview naming `proj-170` as the dependent and `proj-uly` as transitively affected, `--cascade would delete 2 total dependent(s).`, exit **0**, no dumps | `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:141`–`194` | delete_with_dependents, delete_hard_preview, delete_preview_json, delete_preview_json_many |
| S4.334 | **`--force` deletes the named ids only and ORPHANS the dependents** (their edges to the deleted ids vanish, they themselves are untouched apart from the edge and `updated_at`). The orphan list appears in the plain output and in the JSON `orphaned_issues`. `--force` and `--cascade` are mutually exclusive at the parser. | `delete proj-mta --force` → `Deleted 1 issue(s):\n  - proj-mta\nRemoved 1 dependency link(s)\nOrphaned 1 issue(s):\n  - proj-170\n` | `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:293`–`295`, `src/cli/mod.rs:1425`–`1428` | delete_with_dependents_force |
| S4.335 | **`--cascade` deletes the transitive closure of dependents** (repeatedly applying S4.332's relation), excluding nothing, and reports `orphaned_issues` as empty. Edge removal is applied first over the whole set; the tombstones are then written in **ascending id order**, and the reported `deleted` array is in that same ascending id order — not the discovery order. | `delete proj-mta --cascade --json` → `{"deleted":["proj-170","proj-mta","proj-uly"],"deleted_count":3,"dependencies_removed":3,"labels_removed":0,"events_removed":0,"references_updated":0,"orphaned_issues":[]}`; `proj-uly` is reached because `proj-170` is its child via `parent-child` | `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:873`–`896`, `:263`–`267`, `:298`–`326` | delete_cascade |
| S4.336 | **JSON counters.** `deleted` = the ids tombstoned or purged, ascending. `deleted_count` = its length. `dependencies_removed` = the total number of dependency rows deleted, counting an edge once per endpoint pass (outgoing rows of each deleted id plus incoming rows pointing at each deleted id). `labels_removed` and `events_removed` are counted **only on the `--hard` path** and are `0` for every soft delete. `references_updated` is never incremented and is always `0`. `orphaned_issues` is non-empty only with `--force`. Measured (2026-09-22): on the `--hard` path `labels_removed` is the number of labels the removed records carry, and `events_removed` equals `dependencies_removed` in every captured case (comments do not count: `proj-ptp`, with one comment, purges with `events_removed` 0). Correction (2026-09-22, later the same day): that holds without `--cascade` only; under `--cascade --hard` the original's `events_removed` varies between runs (1, 2 or 3 of 3 for `delete proj-mta --cascade --hard --json` over twelve runs), and the port prints `dependencies_removed` (DISC-011). | `delete proj-5u2 --reason Duplicate --json` → `{"deleted":["proj-5u2"],"deleted_count":1,"dependencies_removed":1,"labels_removed":0,"events_removed":0,"references_updated":0,"orphaned_issues":[]}` although `proj-5u2` carries no labels and `proj-mta` (which has two) is untouched | `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:26`–`34`, `:277`–`301`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:12832` | delete_json, delete_cascade, delete_hard_force_json, delete_hard_cascade_json |
| S4.337 | **Plain output of a soft delete**, verbatim: `Deleted <n> issue(s):`, then `  - <id>` per id, then `Removed <n> dependency link(s)` when any edge was removed, then `Orphaned <n> issue(s):` and `  - <id>` per orphan when `--force` produced any. | `delete proj-5u2` → `Deleted 1 issue(s):\n  - proj-5u2\nRemoved 1 dependency link(s)\n` | `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:339`–`380` | delete_basic, delete_with_dependents_force |
| S4.338 | **`--dry-run` writes nothing** (no store, no `last-touched` removal) and prints `Dry-run: Would delete <n> issue(s):` then `  - <id>: <title>` per id, plus `Would also cascade delete <n> dependent(s):` / `Would orphan <n> dependent(s):` blocks when the corresponding flag was given. It is evaluated AFTER the dependents preview, so `--dry-run` on an issue with dependents and no `--force`/`--cascade` prints the *preview* text of S4.333 instead. Measured (2026-09-22): the `Would orphan <n> dependent(s):` (under `--force`: the direct dependents) and `Would also cascade delete <n> dependent(s):` (under `--cascade`: the closure beyond the named ids) blocks list `  - <id>` lines without titles and are absent when they would name nothing (`delete_dry_run_cascade_none`). Under `--json` a dry run prints the preview shape of S4.333: `--force` puts the dependents in `orphaned_issues`, `--cascade` the closure in `cascade_delete`, and `blocked_dependents` is empty under either (`delete_dry_run_force_json`, `delete_dry_run_cascade_json`). Under `--quiet` it prints nothing, exit 0. | `delete proj-5u2 --dry-run` → `Dry-run: Would delete 1 issue(s):\n  - proj-5u2: Write API docs\n`, exit 0, no dumps | `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:196`–`261` | delete_dry_run, delete_dry_run_json, delete_dry_run_quiet, delete_dry_run_force, delete_dry_run_force_json, delete_dry_run_cascade, delete_dry_run_cascade_json, delete_dry_run_cascade_none |
| S4.339 | **`--hard` purges instead of tombstoning: the record leaves the store entirely**, along with its comments, labels, edges and events. The plain output is identical to a soft delete's. With **no ids at all**, `--hard` purges every existing tombstone (`No tombstones to purge.` or `Purged <n> tombstone(s)` then `  - <id>` lines, tombstone ids in ascending order). Measured (2026-09-22): the other end of every edge is cleaned and its `updated_at` bumped as in S4.331; `--hard` on an id that is ALREADY a tombstone purges it (unlike S4.341), so the store is always rewritten (`scn_delete_hard_tombstone`). The purge prints `✓ Purged <n> tombstone(s)` (no colon) and `  - <id>` lines, or `✓ No tombstones to purge.`; its dry run `Dry-run: Would purge <n> tombstone(s):` with the lines, or `Dry-run: no tombstones to purge.`; its JSON the counters of S4.336 over the tombstones (an empty set writes nothing), its dry-run JSON the preview shape with the tombstones as `would_delete` (`scn_delete_purge`). | `delete proj-5u2 --hard` → the store loses the `proj-5u2` line completely; the remaining seven records are unchanged | `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:299`–`312`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:7998`–`8063`, `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:389`–`479` | delete_hard, delete_hard_quiet, delete_hard_touched, delete_hard_all, delete_hard_all_dry_run, scn_delete_hard_tombstone, scn_delete_purge, r8_delete_hard_from_file, scn_r9_purge_modes |
| S4.340 | **`--from-file <PATH>` appends ids read from a file**: one per line, each line trimmed of whitespace, lines that are empty or begin with `#` after trimming are skipped, and there is no inline-comment stripping (`proj-1 # note` is taken whole and then fails id resolution). The combined id list — positionals then file ids — is deduplicated and **sorted ascending** before resolution, so the command line order never reaches the output. With neither positionals nor a file and without `--hard`: `Error: Validation failed: ids: no issue IDs provided`, exit **4**. Measured (2026-09-22): the file ids join the positionals before the sort (`delete_from_file_json`: `proj-b75` and `proj-mta`); a file with no id left is the no-ids refusal (`error_delete_from_file_empty`, `/dev/null`); a line is resolved whole, so a JSON line fails as `Issue not found: <the line>` for the first line in ascending order (`error_delete_from_file_lines`). Reading the file: S4.397. | — | `src/cli/commands/delete.rs:850`–`871`, `:88`–`113` | usage_delete_alone, delete_from_file, delete_from_file_json, error_delete_from_file_empty, error_delete_from_file_lines |
| S4.341 | **Deleting an already-tombstoned issue is a silent no-op for that id** (the storage returns the record unchanged); no field is rewritten for it. | — | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:7903`–`7905` | scn_delete_twice (measured 2026-09-20: the second `delete proj-5u2` prints `Deleted 1 issue(s):` and `  - proj-5u2`, with no `Removed … dependency link(s)` line, and writes NOTHING — the record keeps the `updated_at`, `deleted_at`, `deleted_by` and `delete_reason` of the first delete) |

### S4.350–S4.369 `dep add` / `dep remove`

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.350 | **A new edge is written into the DEPENDENT issue's `dependencies` array** with exactly six fields: `issue_id` (the resolved dependent), `depends_on_id` (the resolved target or the verbatim `external:…` string), `type` (the canonicalized type, default `blocks`), `created_at` (the invocation instant), `created_by` (the resolved actor), `metadata` (the `--metadata` value verbatim when it is non-blank valid JSON, otherwise the literal `"{}"`), `thread_id` (always the empty string `""`, since the insert never names the column and the schema default is `''`). The target issue's record is NOT modified. Measured (2026-09-23): valid `--metadata` is stored VERBATIM (`"{"b": 1, "a": 2.50}"`, spaces and `2.50` kept); blank is `"{}"`; invalid is exit 4, `Validation failed: metadata: dependency metadata must be valid JSON for proj-ptp -> proj-mta (type=blocks); found "{bad": key must be a string at line 1 column 2`. | `dep add proj-5u2 proj-mta` → `{"issue_id":"proj-5u2","depends_on_id":"proj-mta","type":"blocks","created_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z","created_by":"tester","metadata":"{}","thread_id":""}` appended; `proj-mta` byte-identical · `--metadata '{"a":1}'` → `"metadata":"{\"a\":1}"` Amended (2026-09-24, OQ-136): the refused text is written as Rust's `{:?}` writes a string: `\0` `\t` `\n` `\r` `\"` `\\` by name, every other control character, DEL through U+00A0, U+00AD, the combining marks and every code point Rust's `is_printable` refuses (unassigned ones included) as `\u{<lower-case hex, no leading zeros>}`, all else raw (the table was measured over every scalar value, not typed from Unicode data); the position is `line <l> column <c>` where the line counts the line feeds before the error and the column counts bytes after the last of them (`{` LF ` x` → `key must be a string at line 2 column 2`; a text ending in a line feed → column 0; an error at a multi-byte character is at its first byte). | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:12505`–`12519`, `src/storage/schema.rs:358` | dep_add_blocks, dep_add_json, dep_add_related, (case to add: `dep_add_metadata` — `["@fx=basic","dep","add","proj-5u2","proj-mta","--metadata","{\"a\":1}","--json"]`), dep_add_metadata, error_dep_add_metadata_bad, dep_add_metadata_blank, error_dep_add_metadata_lines, error_dep_add_metadata_escape, error_dep_add_metadata_quoting, error_dep_add_metadata_prefix |
| S4.351 | **`dep add` bumps ONLY the dependent's `updated_at`**, and only when a row was actually inserted. `dep remove` likewise bumps only the dependent's `updated_at`, and only when a row was actually deleted. | `dep add proj-ptp proj-mta -t related` → `proj-ptp.updated_at` `2026-01-01T12:30:00Z` → `2026-01-02T03:04:05Z`, `proj-mta` unchanged | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:12525`–`12532`, `:12787`–`12795` | dep_add_related, dep_remove_basic |
| S4.352 | **The eleven accepted `--type` values for `dep add`, verbatim and in this order:** `blocks`, `parent-child`, `conditional-blocks`, `waits-for`, `related`, `discovered-from`, `replies-to`, `relates-to`, `duplicates`, `supersedes`, `caused-by`. The default is `blocks`. Matching is case-insensitive and the stored value is the canonical spelling. **Custom types are REJECTED** with message `Validation failed: type: Unknown dependency type: '<t>'. Allowed types: blocks, parent-child, conditional-blocks, waits-for, related, discovered-from, replies-to, relates-to, duplicates, supersedes, caused-by` and hint `Use --type with one of: blocks, parent-child, conditional-blocks, waits-for, related, discovered-from, replies-to, relates-to, duplicates, supersedes, caused-by.` (or, when a near match exists by normalization, unique prefix, or edit distance ≤ 2, `Did you mean --type <nearest>?`), exit **4**, store unchanged. Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-126): `BLOCKS` is accepted; `Parent_Child`, `paren`, `dup`, `waits`, `relate` (→ `related`), `block`, `blcks` hint `Did you mean --type <type>?`; `xyz` hints `Use --type with one of: <the eleven>.` | `dep add proj-ptp proj-mta -t inspired-by --json` → the pretty `VALIDATION_FAILED` block with that message and hint, `"retryable": true`, `"context": {"field":"type","reason":…,"hint":…}`, exit 4 | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:843`–`915`, `src/cli/mod.rs:2226` | dep_add_custom_type, dep_add_related, dep_add_type_parent_child, dep_add_type_paren, dep_add_type_relate, dep_add_type_dup, dep_add_type_waits, dep_add_type_blocks, dep_add_type_block, dep_add_type_blcks, dep_add_type_xyz |
| S4.353 | **Validation order for `dep add`**, each step refusing before the next: cross-project route check (skipped entirely for an `external:` target) → resolve the dependent id → resolve the target id (skipped for `external:`) → parse `--type` → self-dependency → (inside the write) `--metadata` must be valid JSON → `parent-child` endpoints must both be local → the dependent must exist and not be a tombstone → the target must exist and not be a tombstone (existence skipped for `external:`) → the single-parent rule for `parent-child` → **duplicate edge** → **cycle**. Because duplicate precedes cycle, re-adding an existing edge never reports a cycle. Measured (2026-09-22, OQ-124): a tombstoned target fails `Validation failed: depends_on_id: cannot depend on tombstone issue: <id>`, a tombstoned dependent `Validation failed: issue_id: cannot add dependency from tombstone issue: <id>`, both `VALIDATION_FAILED`, exit 4, nothing written (`scn_dep_tombstone`). | `dep add proj-mta proj-zzz` → target resolution fails first, exit 3 | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:82`–`105`, `:545`–`655`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:12412`–`12537` | error_dep_add_missing, error_dep_add_self, dep_add_duplicate, error_dep_add_cycle, usage_dep_add_one_arg, scn_dep_tombstone, scn_r9_dep_tombstone_quiet |
| S4.354 | **Cycle detection considers only the blocking edge types** `blocks`, `parent-child`, `conditional-blocks`, `waits-for`; a `related`, `discovered-from`, `replies-to`, `relates-to`, `duplicates`, `supersedes` or `caused-by` edge is never cycle-checked. For `parent-child` the direction is INVERTED relative to the stored row: a stored row is child → parent, and the blocker edge is parent → child, so the check runs from the parent to the child. The traversal is a breadth-first search from the target back towards the dependent over `depends_on_id` of `blocks`/`conditional-blocks`/`waits-for` rows unioned with `issue_id` of `parent-child` rows, bounded by a maximum depth beyond which no cycle is reported. | `dep add proj-mta proj-170` on fixture `basic` (where `proj-170` already blocks on `proj-mta`) → refused | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:6958`–`7039`, `:12488`–`12503` | error_dep_add_cycle, error_dep_add_cycle_json |
| S4.355 | **The cycle refusal never prints a path.** stderr: `Error: Cycle detected in dependencies: Adding dependency <dependent> -> <target> would create a cycle` then `Hint: Remove one dependency to break the cycle. Note: epic containment participates in blocking cycles — depending on an epic implies depending on its entire subtree, so the cycle may traverse parent-child edges that the message does not list (see docs/CLI_REFERENCE.md, ` + "`dep add`" + `)`. Exit **5**, store unchanged. In JSON mode the pretty block carries `"code": "CYCLE_DETECTED"`, that message, that hint, `"retryable": false` and `"context": {"cycle_path": "Adding dependency <dependent> -> <target> would create a cycle"}` — the "path" is the same sentence, not a list of ids. | `dep add proj-mta proj-170` → `Error: Cycle detected in dependencies: Adding dependency proj-mta -> proj-170 would create a cycle` | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:12500`–`12503`, `src/error/mod.rs:205`, `:459`–`465` | error_dep_add_cycle, error_dep_add_cycle_json |
| S4.356 | **Self-dependency is refused by identity of the RESOLVED ids**: stderr `Error: Issue cannot depend on itself: <id>` then `Hint: An issue cannot depend on itself`, exit **5**, store unchanged. | `dep add proj-mta proj-mta` → exit 5 | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:565`+, `src/error/mod.rs:213`, `:466` | error_dep_add_self |
| S4.357 | **An `external:<anything>` target skips route validation, id resolution and the existence check**, and is stored verbatim as `depends_on_id`. It is rejected only for `parent-child` (`parent-child dependencies must link local issues: <endpoint>`, exit 4). Measured (2026-09-22, OQ-124): the external-parent refusal is `VALIDATION_FAILED` with field `depends_on_id`, hint `null`, exit 4 (`error_dep_add_external_parent_json`). | `dep add proj-mta external:other-1` → `✓ Added dependency: proj-mta -> external:other-1 (blocks)` and the edge `{"issue_id":"proj-mta","depends_on_id":"external:other-1","type":"blocks",…}` | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:298`–`300`, `:557`–`561`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:12130`–`12167` | dep_add_external, error_dep_add_external_parent, error_dep_add_external_parent_json |
| S4.358 | **Adding an edge that already exists is a success no-op.** stdout `Dependency already exists: <dependent> → <target>` (U+2192 arrow), exit **0**, the store is NOT rewritten, no `updated_at` bump — but `.beads/last-touched` IS written with the dependent's id. JSON: `{"status":"exists","issue_id":…,"depends_on_id":…,"type":…,"action":"already_exists"}`. | `dep add proj-170 proj-mta` → `Dependency already exists: proj-170 → proj-mta\n`, last-touched `proj-170\n`, no store dump | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:12474`–`12486`, `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:597`–`648` | dep_add_duplicate |
| S4.359 | **`dep remove <issue> <target>` removes the single edge between the pair.** When several types connect the pair, `--type` is required: `Validation failed: <…> multiple dependency types connect <issue> to <target>; specify --type to remove one relationship`, exit **4**. `--type` on removal also accepts a custom type verbatim when an edge with exactly that type exists (so JSONL-imported custom edges can be removed). | `dep remove proj-170 proj-mta --json` → `{"status":"ok","issue_id":"proj-170","depends_on_id":"proj-mta","type":"blocks","action":"removed"}`, that edge gone, `proj-170.updated_at` bumped, the `parent-child` edge kept · `dep remove proj-170 proj-uly -t parent-child` → the parent-child edge gone | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:820`–`836`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:12758`–`12764` | dep_remove_basic, dep_remove_typed |
| S4.360 | **Removing an edge that is not there is a success no-op with a WARNING.** stdout is empty, stderr `Warning: Dependency not found: <issue> → <target>` (U+2192), exit **0**, the store is NOT rewritten — but `.beads/last-touched` IS written with the dependent's id. JSON: `{"status":"not_found","issue_id":…,"depends_on_id":…,"type":"<type or the literal unknown>","action":"not_found"}`. | `dep remove proj-mta proj-ptp` → stderr warning only, last-touched `proj-mta\n`, no store dump | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:711`–`806` | dep_remove_absent, dep_remove_absent_json (measured 2026-09-20: under `--json` the `not_found` payload goes to stdout and NO warning is written to stderr) |

JSON add: `{"status":"ok","issue_id":…,"depends_on_id":…,"type":…,"action":"added"}`. | `dep add proj-5u2 proj-mta` → `✓ Added dependency: proj-5u2 -> proj-mta (blocks)\n` | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:639`–`642`, `:786`–`797` | dep_add_blocks, dep_add_json, dep_add_related, dep_remove_basic |
| S4.362 | **Both `dep add` and `dep remove` write `.beads/last-touched` with the RESOLVED DEPENDENT id, unconditionally on any non-refusing path**, before the output is produced, and therefore also in `--json` and `--quiet` mode. The target id is never the last-touched id. | `dep add proj-mta external:other-1` → last-touched `proj-mta\n` | `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:594`, `:778` | dep_add_blocks, dep_add_external, dep_remove_basic, dep_remove_absent, dep_add_duplicate |

### S4.370–S4.379 `label add` / `label remove` / `label rename`

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S4.370 | **Positional split for `label add`/`label remove`.** When `-l/--label` is given, EVERY positional is an issue id. When it is not, there must be at least two positionals: the LAST one is always a label; the split point then walks left while the preceding positional does not parse as an id-shaped string, and never walks past index 1, so at least one leading issue always remains. `-l` is repeatable and comma-split by the parser; positionals are never comma-split and are not trimmed, while `-l` values are trimmed and blanks dropped. With fewer than two positionals and no `-l`: `Validation failed: arguments: usage: label add <issue...> <label...> or label add <issue...> -l <label>[,<label>]`, exit **4**. A positional meant as an issue that resolves to nothing gives `'<input>' is not an issue ID and does not match any issue` with hint `Labels go after all issue IDs: br label <subcommand> <issue...> <input>; or pass -l <input> to name the label explicitly.` Measured (2026-09-22, OQ-122, OQ-123): without `-l`, the FIRST positional is always an issue and the LAST always a label (`proj-b75` included, `label_add_label_looks_like_id`); walking left from the one before the last, a word is a label until the first id-shaped one, which and everything left of it are issues (`proj-mta 5u2 x7 beta` adds three labels). A word is id-shaped when its LAST `-` has a non-empty part of `a`–`z`, `0`–`9`, `_`, `.`, `-` before it and a non-empty part of `a`–`z`, `0`–`9`, `.` after it (`a-b`, `1-2`, `a-b-c`, `my_proj-abc`, `proj-mta.1` are; `Proj-mta`, `proj-MTA`, `ab-c_d`, `proj-`, `5u2` are not; `scn_label_shapes`). The issue words resolve left to right, `-l` or not; the first that resolves to nothing refuses the command: an id-shaped one with the `ISSUE_NOT_FOUND` of S4.212 (exit 3, `error_label_add_missing`), any other with `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `Validation failed: issues: '<w>' is not an issue ID and does not match any issue`, hint `Labels go after all issue IDs: br label <add|remove> <issue...> <w>; or pass -l <w> to name the label explicitly.`, the hint repeated as a third context member after `field` and `reason`, exit 4 (`error_label_remove_word_json`). A non-id-shaped word that does resolve is an issue (`5u2`, `label_add_partial_first`). The adds print label by label, each over every issue. Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-054): `label add proj-mta needs-review` takes the last positional as the label (`label_add_id_shaped`). | `label add proj-mta urgent` → issues `[proj-mta]`, labels `[urgent]` · `label add proj-mta proj-5u2 alpha beta` → issues `[proj-mta, proj-5u2]`, labels `[alpha, beta]` | `src/cli/commands/label.rs:181`–`222` (`parse_issues_and_labels`), `:418`–`430`, `src/cli/mod.rs:2337`–`2345` | label_add, label_remove, label_add_flag_two, (case to add: `label_add_many` — `["@fx=basic","label","add","proj-mta","proj-5u2","alpha","beta"]`), label_add_many, label_add_label_looks_like_id, label_add_middle_words, label_add_partial_first, error_label_add_word_before_id, error_label_add_not_an_issue, error_label_add_flag_word, error_label_add_missing, error_label_add_missing_json, error_label_remove_word_json, scn_label_shapes, r8_label_remove_walk, r9_label_add_walk_quiet, r9_error_label_not_an_issue_quiet, label_add_id_shaped |
| S4.371 | **The command iterates LABELS in the outer loop and ISSUES in the inner loop**, so the output and the per-pair JSON rows are label-major, not issue-major. | `label add proj-mta proj-5u2 alpha beta` → `✓ Added label alpha to proj-mta\n✓ Added label alpha to proj-5u2\n✓ Added label beta to proj-mta\n✓ Added label beta to proj-5u2\n`, `last-touched` `proj-5u2\n` | `src/cli/commands/label.rs:231`–`254` | (case to add: `label_add_many`) |
| S4.372 | **Each label must be 1–50 BYTES of ASCII alphanumerics, `-`, `_` or `:`.** Every named label is validated BEFORE any storage is opened, so an invalid label anywhere aborts the whole command with zero writes and zero output: `Error: Validation failed: label: invalid characters (only alphanumeric, hyphen, underscore, colon allowed)`, exit **4**. The empty and over-length messages are `Validation failed: label: cannot be empty` and `Validation failed: label: exceeds 50 characters`. An issue may carry at most **64** labels; exceeding it gives `Validation failed: labels: exceeds 64 labels`. Measured (2026-09-22, OQ-125): the ceiling is 64 DISTINCT labels per issue, `Validation failed: labels: exceeds 64 labels`, exit 4. `label add` runs label by label and, before each label goes onto any issue, checks every named issue: one that lacks the label and already holds 64 stops the command there, and the labels added before that point STAY WRITTEN (the store is rewritten, `.beads/last-touched` is not, no success line is printed): `label add proj-5u2 -l m01,…,m65` leaves `m01`–`m64` on `proj-5u2`; a label the issue already has passes even at 64. `update` (`--add-label` repeated, `--set-labels`) and `create --labels` refuse as a whole and write nothing; `create` with exactly 64 succeeds. `update --add-label` does not split commas (`a,b` is one label, refused for its characters) (`scn_label_ceiling`, `scn_label_ceiling_update`). | `label add proj-ptp "bad label!"` → exit 4, store unchanged | `src/validation/mod.rs:428`–`448`, `src/validation/mod.rs:24` (`ISSUE_LABEL_MAX_COUNT` = 64), `src/cli/commands/label.rs:231`–`233`, `:314`–`316` | error_label_add_invalid, error_create_bad_label, scn_label_ceiling, scn_label_ceiling_update, r8_update_bad_label_status, r8_create_labels_64, scn_r8_ceiling_json |
| S4.373 | **`label add` adds the label and bumps `updated_at`.** The stored array is always ascending by label text and never holds duplicates (the labels are read back ordered, so insertion order is not observable). Adding a label to a tombstoned issue is refused: `Validation failed: issue_id: cannot add label to tombstone issue: <id>`, exit **4**. | `label add proj-ptp urgent` → `proj-ptp` gains `"labels":["urgent"]`, `updated_at` `2026-01-01T12:30:00Z` → `2026-01-02T03:04:05Z` · `label add proj-mta proj-5u2 alpha beta` → `proj-mta` `["backend","db"]` → `["alpha","backend","beta","db"]` | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:13054`–`13118`, `:13663`–`13666`, `:13850`–`13858` | label_add, label_add_json |

Plain success line: `✓ Renamed label '<old>' to '<new>' on <n> issue(s)` with the trailing `s` suppressed when `n` is 1. ### S4.380–S4.389 `comments add`

| S4.n | clause | examples (input → output) | provenance | cases |

| S4.380 | **A new comment is appended to the issue's `comments` array with exactly five fields:** `id` (S4.382), `issue_id` (the resolved issue), `author` (S4.201), `text`, `created_at` (the invocation instant). The issue's `updated_at` is bumped to the same instant. | `comments add proj-mta "Looks good."` → `"comments":[{"id":2,"issue_id":"proj-mta","author":"tester","text":"Looks good.","created_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z"}]` and `updated_at` `2026-01-01T10:00:00Z` → `2026-01-02T03:04:05Z` | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:20975`–`20987` (`insert_comment_row`), `src/cli/commands/comments.rs:210`–`236` | comments_add, comments_add_json, comments_add_second |
| S4.381 | **Text precedence, first match wins: `-f/--file` (or `-f -` for stdin), then `-m/--message` (alias `--content`), then the positional words joined with a single ASCII space.** The file's content is used verbatim, so a trailing newline survives into the stored text; both stdin and a file are capped at 10485760 bytes (`<field> input exceeds maximum size of 10485760 bytes`) and must be valid UTF-8 (`<field> input must be valid UTF-8: <err>`). The chosen text is NEVER trimmed before storage — trimming is used only for the emptiness test, which refuses with `Error: Validation failed: text: comment text cannot be empty`, exit **4**, store unchanged. When no source at all is given the message is `Validation failed: text: comment text required`. Measured (2026-09-22): the file's text is taken untrimmed (`proj-mta\n`), `-f` wins over `-m` (`comments_add_file_and_message`), and an empty file is the empty-text refusal (`error_comments_add_file_empty`). Reading the file: S4.397. | `comments add proj-mta one two three` → `"text":"one two three"` · `comments add proj-mta positional -m flagform` → `"text":"flagform"` · `comments add proj-mta ""` → exit 4 | `src/cli/commands/comments.rs:431`–`447` (`read_comment_text`), `:411`–`429`, `:229`–`234` | comments_add, comments_add_author, error_comments_add_empty, comments_add_words, error_comments_add_no_text, comments_add_file, comments_add_file_json, comments_add_file_and_message, error_comments_add_file_empty, r8_comments_file_author, r9_comments_file_quiet, stdin_comments_add, stdin_comments_add_empty |
| S4.382 | **The comment's numeric `id` is one more than the LARGEST comment id anywhere in the store, across all issues** — not per issue. It comes from the auto-increment row id of the comments table into which the whole JSONL was imported with each comment's own id preserved. A store whose only comment has id `1` therefore produces `2` for a comment added to any issue, including a different one. An empty store produces `1`. Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-061): in a store with no comments the first id is 1 (`scn_comments_first`). | fixture `basic` (only comment is `proj-ptp`'s id `1`): `comments add proj-mta …` → `"id":2` · `comments add proj-ptp …` → `"id":2` · `reopen proj-mkh --reason Regressed` → its comment gets `"id":2` | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:20975`–`20987` (`INSERT … ; last_insert_rowid()`) | comments_add, comments_add_second, comments_add_author, actor_flag_comment, reopen_closed_json, scn_comments_first |
| S4.383 | **Output shapes.** Plain: `Comment added to <id>`. JSON: the comment object as one compact line, `{"id","issue_id","author","text","created_at"}`. | `comments add proj-mta "Looks good."` → `Comment added to proj-mta\n` · `--json` → `{"id":2,"issue_id":"proj-mta","author":"tester","text":"Looks good.","created_at":"2026-01-02T03:04:05Z"}` | `src/cli/commands/comments.rs:108`–`120` | comments_add, comments_add_json |

An unknown issue fails per S4.212 before anything is written. | `comments add proj-mta "Looks good."` → last-touched `proj-mta\n` · `comments add proj-zzz hi` → exit 3, nothing written | `src/cli/commands/comments.rs:108`–`120` | comments_add, error_comments_add_missing |

### S4.390–S4.399 `epic close-eligible`

| S4.390 | **An epic is "eligible" when it is a non-closed issue of type `epic` that has at least one child and every child is closed.** Candidate epics are the non-closed issues of type `epic`; deferred epics are candidates, closed ones are not. Children are the issues holding a `parent-child` edge whose `depends_on_id` is the epic (the child depends on the epic); template issues are excluded, and a dot-notation child with no such edge does NOT count here (that separate rule belongs to `close`, S4.292). A child counts as closed when its status is `closed` **or** `tombstone`; every other status counts as open. **An epic with zero children is never eligible.** | fixture `basic`: `proj-uly` has one child (`proj-170`, open) → not eligible; after `close proj-170` → `epic status` reports `Progress: 1/1 children closed (100%)` and `   Eligible for closure` | `src/cli/commands/epic.rs:260`–`288`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:12318`–`12346` | epic_status, epic_status_json, scn_lifecycle, scn_child_ids |

The edit always counts as a change (`updated_at` bumps, the store is rewritten), and it is applied after `--notes`, which the parser refuses together with it (`the argument '--notes <NOTES>' cannot be used with '--append-notes <TEXT>'`, exit 2). A file that cannot be read fails with the words of Rust's `io::Error`, `<strerror> (os error <n>)` (`No such file or directory (os error 2)`; a directory opens and then fails to read, `Is a directory (os error 21)`): for `delete` and `comments add` as `IO_ERROR`, `I/O error: <words>`, hint and context `null`, exit **8**; for `--description-file` as a validation failure `description_file: failed to read description file '<path>': <words>`, exit 4. `update` reads its file BEFORE resolving any id (`error_update_description_file_missing_id` reports the file, not the unknown id); `comments add` resolves its issue first (`error_comments_add_file_missing_id` reports `Issue not found`). The text of `--description-file` meets the accumulated-context guard of S4.266 exactly as a `--description` value would (`error_update_description_file_guard`). Measured (2026-09-23): `-` names standard input for `comments add -f -` and `--description-file -` (read to its end, untrimmed; empty input is the empty-text refusal), but NOT for `delete --from-file -`, which opens a file named `-` (`stdin_delete_from_file_dash`: `IO_ERROR`, exit 8). | `comments add proj-b75 -f nope.txt` → `Error: I/O error: No such file or directory (os error 2)`, exit 8 | `goldens/error_delete_from_file_missing.err:1`, `goldens/error_delete_from_file_dir.err:1`, `goldens/error_update_description_file_missing_json.out`, `goldens/comments_add_file.out` (measured 2026-09-22) | error_delete_from_file_missing, error_delete_from_file_missing_json, error_delete_from_file_dir, error_comments_add_file_missing, error_comments_add_file_missing_id, update_description_file, error_update_description_file_guard, error_update_description_file_missing_json, error_update_description_file_missing_id, create_description_file, error_create_description_file_missing, r8_update_description_file_json, r9_error_description_file_quiet, r9_error_delete_from_file_quiet, stdin_update_description, stdin_create_description, stdin_delete_from_file_dash |
| S4.398 | **`update --add-acceptance <TEXT>` (repeatable), `--check-acceptance <ITEMS>` and `--uncheck-acceptance <ITEMS>` (both repeatable) edit the checklist inside `acceptance_criteria` without rewriting the rest.** An ITEM is a line that, after leading whitespace, is `- ` or `* `, then `[ ]`, `[x]` or `[X]`, then a space and the text (`- [] Delta` and `1. [ ] Epsilon` are not items); items are numbered from 1 in line order and every other line is kept byte for byte. A selector is a comma list of 1-based numbers when every piece is digits (a repeat counts once), otherwise a text: case-insensitive, an exact match wins, else it must be a unique substring (`2,x` is a text). Every selector is judged against the items as they were BEFORE this run's additions, and every one is judged before anything is written. Refusals, exit 4, nothing written, the field named after the flag (`check-acceptance` or `uncheck-acceptance`): `<id>: acceptance item <n> does not exist (<m> items present)`; `<id>: acceptance item numbers are 1-based; 0 is not an item`; `<id>: no acceptance item matches "<sel>"; items are:` then every item line; `<id>: "<sel>" matches <k> acceptance items; use the item number instead:` then the matching item lines; and, with no item at all, always under the field `check-acceptance`: `<id>: acceptance_criteria has no checklist items to tick; append items first (--add-acceptance / add_acceptance) or set the whole field (--acceptance-criteria)`. A check writes `[x]` over `[ ]` and leaves `[x]`/`[X]` as they are; an uncheck writes `[ ]`; an addition appends `- [ ] <TEXT>`: alone onto empty criteria, after a `\n` onto text that does not end in one, and followed by `\n` onto text that does (`ends\n` + `B` → `ends\n- [ ] B\n`). A run that changes no byte writes no store. Plain output, after the `Updated` block: `<id> acceptance criteria (<n> item[s])`, one line `  [x] <i>  <text>` or `  [ ] <i>  <text>` per item after the run, then one summary line joined by ` · `: `checked <k> (<i,j>)`, `unchecked <k> (…)`, `added <k> (…)` for the flags given, then `<c> of <n> complete` and `<r> remaining`. JSON: the element of S5.39 gains `"acceptance_criteria":{"items":[{"index","text","checked"}…],"checked_count","total","remaining","checked":[…],"unchecked":[…],"added":[…]}`. `show --json` gains `"acceptance_items":[{"index","text","checked"}…]` as its LAST member when the criteria hold at least one item; `list --json` never does. `--acceptance-criteria` together with any of the three flags is a usage error. | `update proj-b75 --add-acceptance First --add-acceptance Second` → `proj-b75 acceptance criteria (2 items)` / `  [ ] 1  First` / `  [ ] 2  Second` / `added 2 (1,2) · 0 of 2 complete · 2 remaining` | `goldens/update_add_acceptance.out:2-4`, `goldens/scn_acceptance_list.out`, `goldens/scn_acceptance_text.out`, `goldens/scn_acceptance_prose.out`, `goldens/scn_acceptance_select.out` (measured 2026-09-22) | update_add_acceptance, update_add_acceptance_json, error_update_check_acceptance_none, error_update_check_acceptance_none_json, scn_acceptance_list, scn_acceptance_text, scn_acceptance_prose, scn_acceptance_select, r8_update_acceptance_comment, r9_acceptance_quiet |
| S4.399 | **`close --bypass-policy` and `--bypass-reason <REASON>` come as a pair, judged before anything else** (before the ids resolve and before the last-touched fallback): `--bypass-policy` without a reason, or with an empty or all-whitespace one, fails `Validation failed: bypass-reason: --bypass-policy requires --bypass-reason "<text>"`; `--bypass-reason` without the flag fails `Validation failed: bypass-policy: --bypass-reason was set without --bypass-policy`; exit 4, nothing written. With no close policy configured, a valid pair closes exactly as a plain `close` does: no `bypassed_policy`, `bypass_reason` or `policy_gates_fired` member is written, and the output is the same (`close_bypass`); skips and their refusals are unchanged. | `close proj-zzz --bypass-policy` → the bypass-reason refusal, not `Issue not found` | `goldens/error_close_bypass_no_reason.err:1`, `goldens/error_close_bypass_reason_alone.err:1`, `goldens/close_bypass.out:1` (measured 2026-09-22) | close_bypass, error_close_bypass_no_reason, error_close_bypass_no_reason_json, error_close_bypass_blank, error_close_bypass_reason_alone, error_close_bypass_no_ids, r9_bypass_quiet, r9_error_bypass_reason_alone_json |

## S5. Outputs

### S5.A `.beads/issues.jsonl` as it is written back

| S5.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S5.1 | A mutation rewrites the **whole** store, never part of it: every record that survives S5.16 is re-serialized from the loaded model, so records the command never named are normalized too. A command that changes nothing leaves the file byte-identical and the golden then carries no `--- .beads/issues.jsonl ---` dump | `src/config/mod.rs:4914`, `src/sync/mod.rs:11098` | edge_precision_rewrite, update_status_json, close_basic, update_noop_same_value |
| S5.2 | One record per line. Each line is the compact serialization of the record — no space after `:` or `,`, no indentation, no trailing space — followed by one `\n`. The last record also ends with `\n`, so a non-empty store ends with exactly one newline byte and never with a blank line. An export of zero records produces a zero-byte file | `src/sync/mod.rs:10795`, `:10868`, `:12963` | create_min, create_title_flag, create_unicode, edge_precision_rewrite, delete_cascade |
| S5.3 | Lines are ordered by `id` ascending in **byte** order — the query is `ORDER BY id ASC` over TEXT under SQLite's default binary collation, and the Rust fallback comparator used for large stores is `String::cmp`, which agrees. Digits therefore sort before lower-case letters: `proj-170`, `proj-5u2`, `proj-7vm`, `proj-b75`, `proj-bsx`, `proj-mkh`, `proj-mta`, `proj-ptp`, `proj-uly`. No tie-break exists or is needed, because a duplicate id was already refused by S2.40 | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:15006`, `src/sync/mod.rs:10613`, `:10636` | create_full_json, update_status_json, close_two, delete_cascade, edge_precision_rewrite |
| S5.4 | The members of an issue record appear in exactly this fixed order and no other, regardless of the order they had in the file that was read: `id`, `title`, `description`, `design`, `acceptance_criteria`, `prerequisites`, `notes`, `status`, `priority`, `issue_type`, `assignee`, `owner`, `estimated_minutes`, `created_at`, `created_by`, `updated_at`, `closed_at`, `close_reason`, `closed_by_session`, `bypassed_policy`, `bypass_reason`, `policy_gates_fired`, `due_at`, `defer_until`, `external_ref`, `source_system`, `source_repo`, `source_repo_path`, `agent_context`, `deleted_at`, `deleted_by`, `delete_reason`, `original_type`, `compaction_level`, `compacted_at`, `compacted_at_commit`, `original_size`, `sender`, `ephemeral`, `pinned`, `is_template`, `labels`, `dependencies`, `comments` | `src/model/mod.rs:462`–`:650` | create_full_json, delete_cascade, show_deferred_json, edge_precision_rewrite, create_ephemeral |
| S5.5 | Always present in every written record without exception: `id`, `title`, `status`, `priority`, `issue_type`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `compaction_level`, `source_repo`, `original_size` | `src/model/mod.rs:462`, `:471`, `:495`, `:499`, `:503`, `:519`, `:526`, `:623`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:19468`, `:19489` | create_min, edge_precision_rewrite, q_basic |
| S5.6 | `compaction_level` is written as a bare integer even when it was never set, because it is the one member with a hand-written serializer that emits `0` for "not set". A record that carried no `compaction_level` comes back with `"compaction_level":0` | `src/model/mod.rs:26`, `:623` | edge_precision_rewrite, create_min, create_dry_run_json |
| S5.7 | `source_repo` and `original_size` become non-empty by a different route: the record passes through the private in-memory database, whose insert binds `source_repo` as `"."` and `original_size` as `0` when the loaded record had neither. A record that carried neither therefore comes back with `"source_repo":"."` and `"original_size":0`, while a record minted by `br create` carries the workspace name, `"source_repo":"proj"`, and `"source_repo_path":"/mnt/proj"` | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:19468`, `:19489`, `src/storage/schema.rs:267` | edge_precision_rewrite, create_min, q_basic |
| S5.8 | Every other member is omitted when it is not set; `ephemeral`, `pinned` and `is_template` are omitted when false and written as `true` otherwise; `labels`, `dependencies` and `comments` are omitted when empty. `content_hash` is never written. `bypassed_policy`, `bypass_reason` and `policy_gates_fired` are sourced from a database-only table that the in-scope commands never populate, so they never appear either | `src/model/mod.rs:467`, `:545`, `:635`, `:645`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:15960` | create_min, create_ephemeral, show_deferred_json, edge_create_max_title |
| S5.9 | An optional string member whose loaded value is `""` survives a write-back only for `compacted_at_commit`. Every other optional string is read back out of the private database through an accessor that maps `""` to "not set" (`description`, `design`, `acceptance_criteria`, `prerequisites`, `notes`, `assignee`, `owner`, `created_by`, `close_reason`, `closed_by_session`, `source_system`, `source_repo`, `source_repo_path`, `agent_context`, `deleted_by`, `delete_reason`, `original_type`, `sender`), and `external_ref` loses its `""` even earlier, to the trim of S2.36. This is why the `precision` fixture's `"description":""` and `"assignee":""` vanish from the rewritten record while `"compacted_at_commit":""` is kept. [inference] from the accessor table, confirmed by the golden; resolves OQ-007 Corrected (2026-09-23, OQ-018): `""` in `agent_context`, `bypass_reason`, `source_repo` or `source_repo_path` never reaches a write-back: the import refuses it (S2.57). The rule here holds for the other members. | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:15945`, `:15970`, `:15977`, `:19430`, `:19488` | edge_precision_rewrite, edge_precision_show_json, edge_empty_optionals_list |
| S5.10 | String values are escaped by the `serde_json` rules and no others: `"` becomes `\"`, `\` becomes `\\`, and the control characters below U+0020 become `\b`, `\t`, `\n`, `\f`, `\r` where those short forms exist and `\u00XX` with four lower-case hex digits otherwise. Nothing else is escaped: U+007F (DEL) is written as the raw byte `7F`, U+00A0 as the raw bytes `C2 A0`, every other non-ASCII scalar as raw UTF-8 — `日本語のタイトル 🚀` occupies its own 25 bytes — and `/`, `<`, `>` and `&` are never escaped. There is no post-processing of the serialized text at all | `src/sync/mod.rs:10781`, `:10862`; the byte dump of `goldens/create_unicode.out` | create_unicode, show_unicode_json, list_json, search_unicode, edge_create_max_title |
| S5.11 | Numbers are written as bare JSON integers with no sign, no leading zero and no exponent: `priority`, `estimated_minutes`, `compaction_level` and `original_size` are 32-bit signed, a comment's `id` is 64-bit signed. No floating-point value ever reaches the store | `src/model/mod.rs:152`, `:516`, `:938` | create_full_json, comments_add_json, show_comments_json |
| S5.12 | Every timestamp is written as RFC 3339 with the literal `Z` zone, never `+00:00`, and with 0, 3, 6 or 9 fraction digits — the fewest of those four that represent the instant exactly: `…:05Z` for a whole second, `…:05.100Z` for one hundred milliseconds, `…:05.000000001Z` for one nanosecond. A source spelling of `…05.1Z` is therefore rewritten `…05.100Z`, and `12:00:04+02:00` is rewritten `10:00:04Z` Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-020): 3, 6 or 9 fraction digits, the fewest that hold the value: `.000001Z`, `.123456Z`, `.000000001Z` kept; `.1Z` and `.100000000Z` written `.100Z`. | `src/model/mod.rs:519` (chrono `DateTime<Utc>` serde) | edge_precision_rewrite, create_min, show_deferred_json, scn_last_touched, edge_precision_micros, edge_precision_micros_rewrite |
| S5.13 | `labels` inside a record is written sorted ascending by byte order and de-duplicated: the `precision` fixture's `["zeta","alpha","alpha"]` is rewritten `["alpha","zeta"]` | `src/sync/mod.rs:12294`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:13857` | edge_precision_rewrite, list_json, update_set_labels, update_add_label, update_remove_label |
| S5.14 | `dependencies` inside a record is written ordered by `issue_id`, then `depends_on_id`, then the dependency type's text, then `created_at`, then `created_by` ("not set" first), then `metadata`, then `thread_id`, all ascending; the sort is stable and nothing is de-duplicated at this point. Each dependency object's members appear in the fixed order `issue_id`, `depends_on_id`, `type`, `created_at`, `created_by`, `metadata`, `thread_id`, the first four always present and the last three omitted when not set. In practice the last three are always present after a write-back, because the insert binds a loaded dependency's missing `created_by` as `import`, its missing `metadata` as `{}` and its missing `thread_id` as the empty string, while a dependency minted by `dep add` or `create --deps` binds the acting actor, `{}` and the empty string Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-026): a dependency with four members comes back with `"created_by":"import"`, `"metadata":"{}"`, `"thread_id":""`. | `src/sync/mod.rs:12299`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:19963`, `:6875`, `src/model/mod.rs:896`–`:928` | update_status_json, create_with_deps, create_with_parent, dep_add_json, dep_remove_basic, dep_add_related, dep_add_blocks, edge_sparse_dependency_rewrite |
| S5.15 | `comments` inside a record is written ordered by `issue_id`, then `created_at`, then `author`, then body text, then `id`, all ascending, with a stable sort. Each comment object's members appear in the fixed order `id`, `issue_id`, `author`, `text`, `created_at`, all five always present; note the member is spelled `text` while the model calls it a body | `src/sync/mod.rs:12312`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:15200`, `src/model/mod.rs:933`–`:943` | comments_add_json, comments_add_second, show_comments_json, comments_add, comments_add_author |
| S5.16 | Three populations are excluded from the written file: a record with `ephemeral` true, a record whose `id` contains the substring `-wisp-`, and a tombstone past its retention window. The `--no-db` flush builds its export configuration with every default, and the default retention is "keep forever", so the third exclusion never fires on this path and tombstones are always written — a `delete` leaves `"status":"tombstone"` lines behind rather than removing lines. `br create --ephemeral` therefore leaves the store untouched, and with an empty workspace the file is not even created, while still writing the sidecar of S5.17 | `src/sync/mod.rs:12468`, `:10617`, `:10857`, `src/config/mod.rs:4928`, `src/model/mod.rs:848` | create_ephemeral, delete_cascade, delete_json |
| S5.17 | `.beads/last-touched` holds one issue id followed by one `\n` and nothing else. It is written with the file created if needed and truncated first, and on Unix with mode `0600`; every failure is swallowed. `proj-mta` is stored as the nine bytes `proj-mta\n` | `src/util/mod.rs:82`, `:94`, `:104` | create_min, update_status_json, scn_last_touched, update_last_touched |
| S5.18 | The sidecar is written by `create`, `q`, `update`, `close`, `reopen`, `defer`, `undefer`, `dep add`, `dep remove`, `label add`, `label remove` and `comments add`, naming the **last** issue the command acted on. It is written even when the command changed nothing: a duplicate `dep add`, a `label add` of an existing label, a `label remove` of an absent label, a `dep remove` of an absent edge and an `update` to the value already held all leave `issues.jsonl` untouched and still write it. It is *not* written by `delete`, which instead removes the file when it points at a deleted issue, nor by `label rename`. A command that refuses outright — `close` on an already-closed issue, `reopen` on an open issue, `undefer` on an undeferred issue — writes neither file. Under `--no-db` the sidecar write is deliberately ordered **after** the store flush succeeds, so a failed flush leaves no stale pointer | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:160`, `q.rs:216`, `update.rs:525`, `close.rs:747`, `reopen.rs:140`, `defer.rs:159`, `:465`, `dep.rs:595`, `:766`, `label.rs:253`, `:334`, `comments.rs:126`, `delete.rs:835` | update_noop_same_value, dep_add_duplicate, label_add_existing, label_remove_absent, dep_remove_absent, close_already_closed, reopen_open, undefer_not_deferred, label_rename, delete_json, defer_no_until |
| S5.19 | `br q` writes a record with `"source_repo":"."` and no `source_repo_path`, while `br create` writes `"source_repo":"proj"` and `"source_repo_path":"/mnt/proj"` in the same workspace: `q` never sets the two members, so the column defaults of S5.7 apply. Reproduce, do not repair (handover: S10) | `src/cli/commands/q.rs:216`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:19468` | q_basic, q_priority, create_min |

### S5.B The `--json` envelope: rules common to every command

| S5.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S5.20 | A successful `--json` payload is written to **stdout** in compact form — no spaces, no indentation — followed by exactly one `\n`, and nothing else reaches stdout. Diagnostics stay on stderr, which every successful golden shows empty | `src/output/context.rs:1039`, `:220` | list_json, show_json, ready_json, count_json, where_json, stats_json, blocked_json, search_json |
| S5.21 | A failing `--json` command writes the error envelope to **stdout** instead, and it is the one payload that is **pretty-printed**: two-space indentation per level, `": "` after each key, and one `\n` at the end. Its shape is fixed at five members in this order — `code` (a string), `message` (a string), `hint` (a string or `null`), `retryable` (a boolean), `context` (an object or `null`) — all five always present, wrapped in a single `error` member. An array inside `context` is printed one element per line at the next indentation level | `src/main.rs:1875`, `:1885`, `src/error/structured.rs:593` | error_no_workspace_list_json, error_create_bad_priority_json, error_show_missing_json, error_show_ambiguous_json, error_conflict_markers_json, error_malformed_jsonl_json, error_dep_add_cycle_json, dep_add_custom_type, update_status_closed |
| S5.22 | A command that produced output and *then* failed writes both documents to stdout in that order: the compact success payload with its newline, then the pretty envelope. `close` on a blocked issue writes `{"closed":[],"skipped":[…]}` and then the `NOTHING_TO_DO` envelope, exit code 3 | `src/cli/commands/close.rs:437`, `src/main.rs:1875` | error_close_blocked_json |
| S5.23 | `--quiet` suppresses the payload entirely: stdout is empty and the exit code is unchanged | `src/output/context.rs:1039` | list_quiet |
| S5.24 | `--fields` has no effect on a `--json` payload; it selects columns for CSV output only, so `list --json --fields id,title,status,priority` is byte-identical to `list --json` | `src/cli/commands/list.rs:258`, `:364` | list_fields, list_json |
| S5.25 | Repeating `--json` is accepted and changes nothing Resolved (2026-09-23, OQ-098): a repeated `--json` is refused by the parser, exit 2 (`usage_json_twice`), as S1.12 says. | `src/cli/mod.rs:1` (a clap flag) | usage_json_twice |

### S5.C The `--json` shape of each in-scope command

| S5.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S5.30 | `show --json` is an **array**, one element per requested id, in the order the ids were given. The issue's own `labels`, `dependencies` and `comments` are cleared before serialization, so each of those names appears at most once. Under `--no-db`, `show` reads `.beads/issues.jsonl` **directly** rather than the loaded model, so the element carries the record exactly as the file spells it — unsorted and duplicated labels, `""` optionals, and no `source_repo` or `original_size` when the file had none — which is why `show --json` and `list --json` disagree about the same issue | `src/format/output.rs:165`, `src/cli/commands/show.rs:506`, `:893` | show_json, show_comments_json, show_deferred_json, show_unicode_json, edge_precision_show_json, show_two, show_partial_hash, show_epic, show_closed, show_plain, show_comments, show_unicode |

<!-- from docs/spec-parts/S5b.md -->

| S5.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S5.100 | Five output modes exist: Rich, Plain, Json, Toon, Quiet. Under the pinned capture environment (`NO_COLOR=1` set, stdout a pipe) every run without `--json`/`--robot` and without `--quiet` is **Plain**; `--json` or `--robot` selects Json; `--quiet`/`-q` selects Quiet. Plain output carries no ANSI escape: every byte in the plain goldens is literal text | `src/output/mod.rs:1` (mode-detection doc), `src/output/context.rs:773` | `list_plain`, `list_quiet`, `list_json` |
| S5.101 | **Rich (TTY tables, panels, ANSI) and TOON (`--format toon`, `BR_OUTPUT_FORMAT=toon`) output are OUT OF SCOPE for the port** and are not specified here; every Rich branch named in the provenance below is unreachable while stdout is a pipe, and no captured case enters it | `docs/PLAN_TO_PORT_BEADS_RUST_TO_BEND2.md` §3 exclusions; `src/output/context.rs:895` | `list_plain`, `show_plain`, `stats_plain` (piped stdout takes the Plain branch; no golden `.out` or `.err` holds an escape byte); no case enters Rich or TOON: excluded surface |
| S5.102 | Every non-empty plain golden ends with exactly one `\n`; there is no case with a missing final newline and **no plain golden contains a trailing space on any line** (verified over all non-`*json*` `.out` and `.err` files). Padding is therefore always interior (`stats`), never at end of line | verified over `goldens/*.out`, `goldens/*.err`; `src/output/context.rs:947` uses `println!` throughout | `stats_plain`, `list_long`, `show_plain` |
| S5.103 | Stream split in Plain mode: ordinary results and the `✓` success lines go to **stdout**; `Warning: …` lines, `Error: …`/`Hint: …` lines and `[note] …` truncation notes go to **stderr**. A command may write both in one run (`dep_remove_absent` writes only stderr; `ready_limit` writes stdout results and a stderr note) | `src/output/context.rs:1236` (success→stdout), `:1252` (error→stderr), `:1266` (warning→stderr), `:1282` (info→stdout) | `dep_remove_absent`, `ready_limit`, `close_already_closed` |
| S5.104 | `ctx.success(m)` in Plain prints `✓ ` + `m` + `\n` to stdout (U+2713, one space). `ctx.warning(m)` prints `Warning: ` + `m` + `\n` to stderr. `ctx.error(m)` prints `Error: ` + `m` + `\n` to stderr. `ctx.info(m)` prints `m` + `\n` to stdout with **no** prefix or glyph. `ctx.print_line(m)` prints `m` + `\n` to stdout. `ctx.newline()` prints a bare `\n` to stdout | `src/output/context.rs:1236,1252,1266,1282,1309` | `create_min` (`✓ Created …`), `dep_list` (info header), `dep_remove_absent` (warning) |
| S5.105 | A terminating error is rendered by the top level as `Error: {message}` and, when a hint exists, a second line `Hint: {hint}`, both on **stderr**, followed by a single `\n`; stdout for that run holds only whatever was already written before the failure. In `close_already_closed`, `close_epic_with_open_child` and `error_close_blocked` stdout is **empty (0 bytes)** while stderr carries `Warning:` then `Error:` then `Hint:`, exit 3. (The error catalogue itself — messages, codes, exit codes — is S9's.) | `src/error/structured.rs:606`, `src/main.rs:1893` | `close_already_closed`, `error_close_blocked`, `error_show_missing` |
| S5.106 | In **Quiet** mode every mode-aware printer is a no-op: `print`, `print_line`, `print_styled_line`, `success`, `warning`, `info` and `newline` emit nothing; only `error` still writes (`Error: {message}` to stderr). A quiet read therefore produces a **0-byte stdout and a 0-byte stderr** with exit 0 | `src/output/context.rs:936,947,973,1236,1266,1282,1309` | `list_quiet` (both streams empty, exit 0) |
| S5.107 | Quiet is additionally short-circuited before rendering in `list`, `ready`, `blocked`, `search`, `show`, `count`, `stats`, `where`, `version`, `epic`, `comments`, `dep` (every subcommand), `label` (every subcommand), `update`, `defer`, `undefer` and `delete`: those commands return before building any text at all. A quiet **mutation** still performs its write and its `.beads/last-touched` update; only the report is suppressed | `list.rs:210`, `ready.rs:197`, `blocked.rs:235`, `search.rs:211`, `show.rs:314`, `count.rs:98,114`, `stats.rs:161`, `where.rs:41`, `version.rs:97`, `epic.rs:89`, `comments.rs:128,250`, `dep.rs:610,697,781,996,1736,2019`, `label.rs:489,517,545,576,622,656,683`, `update.rs:546`, `defer.rs:190,496`, `delete.rs:165,230,355,464,586,755` | `list_quiet`, create_quiet, dep_add_quiet, label_add_quiet, comments_add_quiet, delete_quiet, close_quiet, close_blocked_quiet, defer_quiet, undefer_quiet, reopen_quiet — measured 2026-09-20: each of those mutations writes its files and prints nothing, EXCEPT `reopen`, which prints its `✓ Reopened <id>: <title>` line under `--quiet` all the same (the list above omits `reopen` on purpose), and a `close` that skips every id, which drops the per-item `Warning:` lines (S9.26) but still writes the `Error:`/`Hint:` pair of S9.12 to stderr and exits 3; (case to add: `update_quiet` — `["@fx=basic","update","proj-mta","-p","0","--quiet"]`) |
| S5.108 | `br reopen` is the one in-scope command with **no** Quiet guard: its plain branch writes with bare `print!`/`println!`, which the mode never sees, so `br reopen --quiet` still prints `✓ Reopened …` on stdout. `br create --silent` behaves the same way and for the same reason (S5.205) [inference: no captured case exercises either combination] | `reopen.rs:168-190` (no `is_quiet` branch), `create.rs:171` | (case to add: `reopen_quiet` — `["@fx=basic","reopen","proj-mkh","--quiet"]`; `create_silent_quiet` — `["create","X","--silent","--quiet"]`) |
| S5.109 | Golden-file convention: when a run changed the store, the harness appends `--- .beads/issues.jsonl ---` (and, when it changed, `--- .beads/last-touched ---`) plus the file bodies to the captured **stdout**, and a scenario golden adds `$ [argv]` headers and `[exit N]` lines. Those bytes are the harness's, not the command's: the command's own stdout ends at the first `--- .beads/` line | `scripts/ws_inner.py:212`, `scripts/make-fixture.sh:26` | `close_basic`, `scn_lifecycle` |

### S5.110–S5.119 — Shared plain-text primitives

| S5.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S5.110 | Status icons, one per status, used wherever a status glyph appears: `○` U+25CB open · `◐` U+25D0 in_progress · `●` U+25CF blocked · `❄` U+2744 deferred **and** draft · `✓` U+2713 closed · `✗` U+2717 tombstone · `📌` U+1F4CC pinned · `?` U+003F any custom status | `src/format/text.rs:15-32,105-116` | `list_all` (`◐ ○ ✓ ❄`), `search_status` (`✓`) |
| S5.111 | Priority renders as `P` followed by the decimal priority number (`P0`…`P4`); the priority **badge** is `[● ` + that label + `]`, always with `●` U+25CF regardless of the issue's status. The type **badge** is `[` + the type label + `]` (`[task]`, `[bug]`, `[feature]`, `[epic]`, `[docs]`, `[chore]`, `[question]`, or the custom type verbatim) | `src/format/text.rs:120,184,190,196` | `list_plain`, `ready_plain`, `blocked_plain` |
| S5.112 | Every untrusted single-line field (id, title, label, author, type/status label, path, query echo) passes through an inline sanitizer that leaves printable text untouched and replaces **every** control character — including `\n`, `\t`, `\r` — with its Rust `escape_default` form (`\u{1b}`, `\r`, `\n`, `\u{7}`, `\u{9b}`, …). Free-text bodies (description, design, prerequisites, acceptance criteria, notes, comment body) use a second sanitizer that **preserves `\n` and `\t`** and escapes everything else | `src/format/text.rs:40,49,53` | `show_unicode` (a real tab survives inside the description body: `Line two<TAB>tabbed`) |
| S5.113 | Date rendering in plain text: `Created`, `Updated`, `Closed`, `Due`, `Deferred Until`/`Deferred until` all print `%Y-%m-%d` (e.g. `2026-01-01`) — the time of day is never shown. Comment timestamps print `%Y-%m-%d %H:%M UTC` (e.g. `2026-01-01 12:30 UTC`): minute resolution, the literal suffix ` UTC`, no seconds and no offset | `show.rs:1320,1345,1353,1376,1479`, `src/format/text.rs:371,375,381` | `show_plain`, `show_closed`, `show_comments`, `comments_list`, `list_long` |
| S5.114 | Long titles are **not truncated and not wrapped** in the captured (piped) mode. `list`, `search` and `ready` compute a max width only when stdout is a terminal, and pass none otherwise; `blocked` passes width 0 unless `--wrap`. The ellipsis form (`…` as three ASCII dots appended after cutting to `max_len - 3` display columns, by `unicode-width`) therefore never appears in any plain golden Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-063): a 306-character title prints whole in plain `list` (`scn_long_texts`). | `list.rs:265`, `search.rs:267`, `ready.rs:212`, `blocked.rs:244`, `src/format/text.rs:250-290` | `list_plain` (54-column title printed whole); (case to add: `list_long_title` on a fixture whose title exceeds 200 columns, to pin that no truncation happens when piped), scn_long_texts |
| S5.115 | Free-text bodies in `br show` ARE soft-wrapped, at `COLUMNS` when that variable parses to ≥ 20 and at **100** columns otherwise (the sandbox clears the environment, so 100 applies). Wrapping breaks on spaces only, never splits a word, preserves existing line breaks, and carries a line's leading indentation onto its continuations. `--no-wrap` disables it entirely Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-064): at 100 columns `Word ` repeated wraps into lines of 20 words, 99 characters (`scn_long_texts`). Amended (2026-09-24, bb-rvt): a body of any length is wrapped the same way; `show_long_texts` holds an 80 KB description of seven lines of words. | `show.rs:1196,1208-1245,1189` | `show_unicode` (body shorter than 100 columns, unwrapped); (case to add: `show_wrap_100` on a fixture description longer than 100 columns), scn_long_texts, show_long_texts |
| S5.116 | The one-line issue summary used by `list` (default), `search` and `list --long`'s first line is exactly `{icon} {id} [● P{n}] [{type}] - {title}`: single spaces between the five parts and the separator is space-hyphen-space | `src/format/text.rs:298-334` | `list_plain`, `search_plain`, `edge_precision_sort_title`, r10_n984_search_limit |
| S5.117 | The per-issue detail block used by `list --long` lists, in this fixed order and only when the field is present and non-empty: `Status: {status}`, `Priority: P{n}`, `Type: {type}`, `Assignee: {a}`, `Owner: {o}`, `Labels: {l1, l2}` (comma+space joined, already sorted in the store; amended 2026-09-20: golden `list_long` prints NO `Labels:` line for `proj-170`, which carries `auth` and `backend`, so under `--no-db` the rows of the text listing carry no labels and the line never appears; the JSON listing does carry them, S5.33), `Due: {date}`, `Deferred Until: {date}`, then always `Created: {date}` and `Updated: {date}`. `Status`, `Priority` and `Type` are always present | `src/format/text.rs:345-386` | `list_long` (the deferred row shows `Deferred Until: 2027-06-01` between `Type:` and `Created:`) |
| S5.118 | Pluralization is per-message and inconsistent across commands; each message's rule is stated in its own clause. Three families exist: true pluralization (`1 issue` / `3 issues`), the parenthesized escape (`issue(s)`, `dependency link(s)`, `epic(s)`), and hard-coded plurals kept for bd conformance even at count 1 (`Blocked by 1 open dependencies`, S5.141) | `ready.rs:248`, `label.rs:585`, `blocked.rs:441`, `delete.rs:363` | `ready_limit` vs `ready_plain`, `label_list_all`, `blocked_plain` |
| S5.119 | Indentation vocabulary in plain text: two spaces for a sub-item under a header (`  - `, `  -> `, `  <- `, `  backend`, `  status: …`), three spaces for the `epic status` progress lines, four spaces for `blocked --detailed` blocker bullets (`    • `). A node can carry BOTH flags at once — an already-expanded node with neighbours is `repeat` by S4.185 and, because it does not expand here either, `truncated` as well — and the `(truncated) ` prefix wins: `dep_tree_repeat` prints `├── (truncated) proj-in9` for the occurrence whose JSON (`dep_tree_repeat_json`) carries `"truncated":true,"repeat":true`. `└──` is never used by this renderer Resolved (2026-09-23, OQ-097): no header line; the tree starts with its root line (`dep_tree`). | `dep.rs:1756-1775` | `dep_tree`, `dep_tree_leaf`, `scn_child_ids` (root-only); `dep_tree_depth`, `dep_tree_deep`, `dep_tree_diamond`, `dep_tree_back_edge` (indent and glyphs at real depth); `dep_tree_repeat`, `dep_tree_max_depth`, `dep_tree_max_depth_1`, `dep_tree_max_depth_0` (both marker prefixes and their precedence); `dep_tree_up`, `dep_tree_both`, `dep_tree_up_basic` |

Indent is four spaces; there is no status and no `truncated`/`repeat` mark, and a repeated id is a node of its own. The label is the inline-sanitized id and title of S5.112 (a tab prints as `\t`) with every `"` replaced by `'`; `<`, `>`, `&`, `\`, `|`, `[`, `]` and non-ASCII text pass unchanged.
