### S5.245–S5.250 — Cross-cutting recap and negative space

| S5.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S5.245 | Complete inventory of glyphs that can appear in in-scope plain output, with meanings: `○` U+25CB open status / non-eligible epic · `◐` U+25D0 in progress · `●` U+25CF blocked status **and** the fixed bullet inside every priority badge · `❄` U+2744 deferred or draft · `✓` U+2713 closed status, eligible epic, and the success-line prefix · `✗` U+2717 tombstone (and the batch-create failure prefix on stderr) · `📌` U+1F4CC pinned · `?` unknown/custom status · `📋` U+1F4CB ready header · `🚫` U+1F6AB blocked header · `✨` U+2728 the "nothing to do" lines of `ready` and `blocked` · `📊` U+1F4CA stats header · `⏱` U+23F1 defer · `⊘` U+2298 reopen/defer/undefer skip · `·` U+00B7 `show` field separator · `→` U+2192 update field changes, duplicate/missing dependency messages · `—` U+2014 the `ready` empty line and the close "Nothing to do" summary | `src/format/text.rs:15-32`; `ready.rs:246,294`; `blocked.rs:388,393`; `stats.rs:1223`; `defer.rs:198`; `reopen.rs:183`; `update.rs:1286`; `dep.rs:649`; `show.rs:1299` | `list_all`, `ready_plain`, `blocked_plain`, `empty_ready`, `stats_plain`, `defer_no_until`, `reopen_open`, `update_status`, `dep_add_duplicate`, `show_plain` |
| S5.246 | Plain mode never emits an ANSI escape, a box-drawing table, a panel border or a progress spinner: those belong to Rich. The only box-drawing characters any in-scope plain path can emit are the `├──`/`└──`/`│` connectors of `list --tree`, `list --pretty` and `dep tree` | `src/output/context.rs:947` (Plain is a bare `println!`); `list.rs:826-829`; `dep.rs:1760` | `list_plain`, `dep_tree` |
| S5.247 | No in-scope plain command prints a column-aligned table; the only alignment in the entire plain surface is `stats`' fixed label padding (S5.168) and the capacity block's width specifiers (S5.172) | `stats.rs:1228,1315` | `stats_plain` |
| S5.248 | `[note] …` is the exact prefix of the stderr truncation notes of `list`, `ready` and `blocked` (square brackets, lower-case `note`, one space). `search`'s equivalent uses the different prefix `note: ` and goes to **stdout** (S5.160) | `list.rs:197`, `ready.rs:262`, `blocked.rs:217`, `search.rs:362` | `ready_limit` |
| S5.249 | Numbers in plain text are always plain decimal: no thousands separators, no padding, no sign for zero. The only fractional number in the plain surface is `stats`' average lead time, printed with exactly one fractional digit (S5.170) | `count.rs:109`, `stats.rs:1256-1260` | `count_plain`, `stats_plain` |
| S5.250 | Output is state-dependent, and the scenario goldens are the contract for that: the same argv produces different bytes after a mutation (`ready` gains and loses rows, `epic status` flips its glyph and gains the eligibility line, `blocked` shrinks its header count, `stats` moves from `2.0 hours` to `23.2 days`) | `goldens/scenarios/lifecycle.scn:4`–`goldens/scenarios/lifecycle.scn:16` (the steps). `ready`: `goldens/scn_lifecycle.out:2` → `goldens/scn_lifecycle.out:35` (gains `proj-170`, loses `proj-mta`) → `goldens/scn_lifecycle.out:67`. `epic status`: `goldens/epic_status.out:1` (`○`) → `goldens/scn_lifecycle.out:61` (`✓`) with `goldens/scn_lifecycle.out:63` (`Eligible for closure`). `blocked`: `goldens/blocked_plain.out:2` (`(2)`) → `goldens/scn_lifecycle.out:41` (`(1)`). `stats`: `goldens/stats_plain.out:13` (`2.0 hours`) → `goldens/scn_lifecycle.out:96` (`23.2 days`) | `scn_lifecycle`, `scn_child_ids`, `scn_last_touched`, `scn_dup_title` |
| S5.251 | Coverage clause for the plain failure surface: in every captured non-JSON failing run, **stdout is 0 bytes** and the whole report is on stderr in the two-line `Error:` / `Hint:` shape of S5.105 (a `Hint:` line is present only when the error carries one; `error_conflict_markers` instead carries a multi-line message whose continuation lines are part of the message text). No partial plain output precedes a failure in any of them | `src/error/structured.rs:606`, `src/main.rs:1893` | `error_close_missing`, `error_comments_add_empty`, `error_comments_add_missing`, `error_conflict_markers`, `error_dep_add_cycle`, `error_dep_add_missing`, `error_dep_add_self`, `error_label_add_invalid`, `error_no_workspace_create`, `error_no_workspace_list`, `error_reopen_missing`, `error_update_ambiguous`, `error_update_bad_priority`, `error_update_missing`, `error_create_bad_priority`, `error_defer_bad_until`, `error_update_blocked_claim`, `list_sort_bad`, `list_status_bad` |
| S5.252 | Argument-parser failures are a different surface: the message is lower-case `error: …`, it is followed by a blank line, a `Usage: …` line, another blank line and `For more information, try '--help'.`, all on **stderr** with stdout empty and exit 2. Those bytes are generated by the argument-parser library and belong to S1; this part records only the stream, the empty stdout and the fact that they never mix with the `Error:`/`Hint:` shape | `src/main.rs` clap dispatch; `src/error/structured.rs:606` (not used on this path) | `usage_none`, `usage_unknown_cmd`, `usage_unknown_flag`, `usage_empty_arg`, `usage_limit_noval`, `usage_limit_bad`, `usage_unexpected_flag`, `usage_dep_alone`, `usage_label_alone`, `usage_epic_alone`, `usage_search_alone`, `usage_dep_add_one_arg` |

## S6. Order-leak inventory

<!-- from docs/spec-parts/S6_S8_S10.md -->

Every place where an iteration or insertion order reaches an output on an
in-scope `--no-db` path.

**Determinism evidence.** `scripts/floor.sh --repeat 3` found exactly one
unstable case among the 231 captured cases (`show_partial_ambiguous`,
DISC-005). In addition, this extraction ran the oracle 5–8 times per probe
through `scripts/ws-run.sh --oracle` over outputs the corpus does not exercise
— `count --by-label/--by-assignee/--by-type/--by-status/--by-priority` (plain
and `--json`), `label list-all` (plain and `--json`), `stats` (plain and
`--json`), `blocked` (plain and `--json`), `dep cycles` (plain and `--json`),
`epic status --json`, `epic close-eligible`, `dep tree … --json`,
`dep list … --json`, `ready --json`, `list --json`, `search … --json`,
`close … --json`, `update … --claim`, `delete … --cascade --json`,
`close … --suggest-next --json`, `label rename … --json`,
`update … --set-labels z,a,m,b --json`, `create … -l zeta,alpha,mid,beta --json`,
`show <target with 18 tied dependent edges>` — plus the same probe set against a
24-issue store and a 20-issue synthetic store built inside the sandbox. Every
one of them was byte-identical across repeats. The single hash-random leak in
`--no-db` mode remains the ambiguous-partial-id candidate list (S6.31).

| S6.n | structure | order the original exhibits | reaches output via | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S6.1 | the `--no-db` backing store | `--no-db` is not an in-memory Rust collection: it opens a throwaway on-disk SQLite file and bulk-imports the whole JSONL into it on every invocation. Rows are inserted in JSONL line order, so SQLite rowid order equals file line order | every command; a `SELECT` without `ORDER BY` therefore yields JSONL line order | `src/config/mod.rs:5369`, `:5378` (`open_memory`), `:5415` (import); `src/storage/sqlite.rs:2694`–`:2714` | `list_plain`, `list_json` |
| S6.2 | JSONL write-back line order Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-079): `proj-uly.1`, `proj-uly.10`, `proj-uly.11`, `proj-uly.2` (`scn_child_export_order`). | `id` ascending in **byte** (BINARY) order; the `id` column is declared without a collation, so no case folding. Records whose `ephemeral` is true and ids containing `-wisp-` are excluded from the file entirely | `.beads/issues.jsonl` after every mutation | `src/sync/mod.rs:10613`–`10619`; `src/storage/sqlite.rs:14995`–`15006`; `src/storage/schema.rs:243` | `create_min` (new `proj-fyw` lands between `proj-b75` and `proj-mkh`), `create_ephemeral` (no `issues.jsonl` dump at all — only `last-touched` changed), `defer_until_date`, scn_child_export_order |
| S6.3 | `labels` array inside an exported record | byte-ascending and deduplicated, **case-sensitive** (`Mid` before `alpha` before `zeta`); the incoming array's order and duplicates are discarded | every rewritten record in `.beads/issues.jsonl` | `src/sync/mod.rs:12293`–12296 (sort then dedup) | `edge_precision_rewrite` (input `["zeta","alpha","alpha"]` comes back `["alpha","zeta"]`), `update_set_labels` (`z,a,m,b` stored ascending); (case to add: `edge_labels_mixed_case_rewrite` — `["Zulu","alpha","ALPHA","_under","9nine"]` in a fixture, then `update <id> -p 1`) |
| S6.4 | `dependencies` array inside an exported record | seven keys in order: `issue_id`, `depends_on_id`, `type`, `created_at`, `created_by`, `metadata`, `thread_id`, each ascending. The key is total, so no tie remains | every rewritten record | `src/sync/mod.rs:12300` | `create_with_parent`, `create_with_deps`, `dep_add_blocks`, `dep_add_related` |
| S6.5 | `comments` array inside an exported record | five keys in order: `issue_id`, `created_at`, `author`, `body`, `id`, each ascending. `created_at` before `id`, so an out-of-order comment id sorts by time first | every rewritten record | `src/sync/mod.rs:12311` | `comments_add`, `comments_add_second`, `comments_add_author` |
| S6.6 | `list` default order (no `--sort`) | `priority` ascending, then `created_at` **descending**, then `id` ascending. Stable in effect because the key is total (ids are unique). The Rust twin uses an unstable sort, which is safe for the same reason | stdout, plain and `--json` | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:18248` (`sort_list_default`), `:8475`, `:8481` (SQL twin) | `list_plain` (P1 group `proj-170` at 11:00 then `proj-mta` at 10:00), `list_all` (P2 group `proj-mkh` 13:00, `proj-b75` 10:00:06, `proj-uly` 10:00:04), `list_json`, `empty_list` |
| S6.7 | `list --sort priority` | `priority` ascending, then `created_at` descending, then `id` ascending — identical to the default | stdout | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:8458` | (case to add: `list_sort_priority` — `["@fx=basic","list","--sort","priority"]`) |
| S6.8 | `list --sort created_at` | `created_at` **descending** (newest first), then `id` ascending. The tie-break is id ascending even though the primary key is descending | stdout | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:8464` | `list_sort_created` (`b75` 10:00:06 first, `mta` 10:00:00 last), `edge_precision_sort_created` (`aab` `…05.100Z` before `aad` `…05.1Z` — equal instants, id ascending) |
| S6.9 | `list --sort updated_at` | `updated_at` descending, then `id` ascending | stdout | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:8467` | `list_sort_updated` (`proj-170` and `proj-5u2` both at 11:00:00, emitted id-ascending) |

Non-ASCII is compared by raw UTF-8 bytes and is never folded | stdout | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:8471` | `list_sort_title` (`Ünïcödé …` last), `edge_precision_sort_title` (`UPPER case title` between `one tenth short form` and `whole second`); probe: titles `apple match`, `hotel match`, `jackal match`, `ZULU match`, `İstanbul match` come out in exactly that order — full-Unicode folding would place `İstanbul` between `hotel` and `jackal`, so the fold is ASCII-only. | S6.36 | scenario step order and the state dumps | each step's stdout is followed by a dump of `.beads/issues.jsonl` and then `.beads/last-touched`, in that fixed order, and only for the files the step changed; the harness writes the step header to both stdout and stderr | the captured scenario goldens | `scripts/ws_inner.py:133` (`dump_changes`), `:23` (`STATE_FILES`, which fixes the `issues.jsonl`-then-`last-touched` order), `:136` (a file is dumped only when its hash moved), `:208`–209 (the step header goes to both streams); `goldens/scn_lifecycle.out:8`, `:17` (the two dumps, in that order), `goldens/scn_lifecycle.err:2` | `scn_lifecycle`, `scn_last_touched`, `scn_dup_title`, `scn_child_ids` |
| S6.37 | `list --limit N` paging path | with a limit and no other filter the rows are fetched one priority bucket at a time, each bucket ordered `created_at` descending then `id` ascending, and the buckets concatenated `P0`…`P4`. The result equals the global default order because priority is the leading key | stdout | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:8512`–8557, `:8546` | `list_limit_one`, `list_limit_offset`, `ready_limit` |
| S6.38 | `show <id> --json` label array | **not normalized**: the labels come back exactly as the JSONL holds them, unsorted and with duplicates intact, unlike every other reader | the `"labels"` array of `show --json` | `src/cli/commands/show.rs:487`, `:506` (the JSONL-direct branch) | `edge_precision_show_json` renders `"labels":["zeta","alpha","alpha"]` while `edge_precision_rewrite` writes `["alpha","zeta"]` for the same record |

## S7. Numeric inventory

<!-- from docs/spec-parts/S4a_S7.md -->

| S7.n | quantity | type in original | observed range / evidence | wrap or exact | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S7.1 | id seed field byte length | pointer-width unsigned (64-bit on the pinned target), printed as decimal | 0 (`0:` for an absent description) through 500-plus; corpus maximum 500 bytes for the 500-character ASCII title of `edge_create_max_title`, and 37 bytes for the 29-byte unicode title plus its prefix in `create_unicode` | exact; counts UTF-8 **bytes**, never characters (RS-09) | `src/util/id.rs:297` | create_unicode, edge_create_max_title |
| S7.2 | id seed timestamp | signed 64-bit nanoseconds since the Unix epoch, printed as a bare decimal with `-` only when negative | corpus values `1767323045000000000` (2026-01-02T03:04:05Z), `1770109200000000000` (2026-02-03T09:00:00Z), `1770195600000000000`–`1770195602000000000` (scn_child_ids) | exact within 1677-09-21T00:12:43.145224192Z … 2262-04-11T23:47:16.854775807Z; **outside that range the value becomes `0`** rather than wrapping or failing, which makes every out-of-range instant hash alike | `src/util/id.rs:283` | create_min, scn_dup_title, scn_child_ids |
| S7.3 | id nonce | unsigned 32-bit, printed as decimal | corpus reaches 0, 1 and 2 (`scn_dup_title`); the ladder can reach 9 at each length and 2000 on the fallback rung | exact; the fallback loop bound 2000 is a constant, and the ladder never exceeds it | `src/util/id.rs:281`, `src/util/id.rs:13` | scn_dup_title |
| S7.4 | id hash accumulator | unsigned 64-bit built from digest bytes 0..7, most-significant byte first | full 0 … 18446744073709551615; corpus values are opaque, e.g. the value behind `proj-fyw` | exact by construction: exactly eight 8-bit shifts, so no shift ever discards a bit and no wrap occurs | `src/util/id.rs:312` | create_min |
| S7.5 | base-36 digit extraction | unsigned 64-bit remainder and truncating division by 36 | remainder 0..35 | exact; both operands are non-negative so truncating and flooring division agree and the remainder is never negative (RS-03 does not bite) | `src/util/id.rs:337` | create_min |
| S7.6 | hash length | pointer-width unsigned | 3 in every captured case; the predicate's reachable values are 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8 and the fallback constant 12 | exact; the length never exceeds 8 on the adaptive path, and the fallback path is a constant 12 | `src/util/id.rs:103`, `src/util/id.rs:12` | create_min, create_full_json |
| S7.7 | issue count fed to the adaptive length | pointer-width unsigned, converted to a 64-bit binary float | 0 (empty fixture) and 8 (`basic`) and 11 (`basic` after three child creates in `scn_child_ids`) | the conversion is exact for every count below 2^53, far above any reachable store; the float comparison is the only float in id generation and its thresholds are the integers of S4.9 | `src/util/id.rs:104` | create_min, create_full_json |
| S7.8 | birthday-bound intermediates | 64-bit binary float: 36 raised to an integer power, a negated quotient, an exponential, one minus it, compared with 0.25 | 36^3 = 46656 through 36^8 = 2821109907456, each exact in a 64-bit float; the probability lies in 0.0 … 1.0 | the powers and the product 2 × space are exact; only the exponential is inexact, and the S4.9 band edges are far from any tie, so the table is stable | `src/util/id.rs:107` | create_min |
| S7.9 | child number | unsigned 32-bit, printed as decimal with no padding | corpus reaches 1 and 2 (`scn_child_ids`); the parser accepts 0 … 4294967295 | **saturating**: the next number is the maximum plus one with saturating addition, so 4294967295 stays 4294967295 instead of wrapping; the search for a free number stops 100 past the computed value | `src/util/id.rs:352`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:14406` | scn_child_ids |
| S7.10 | priority | signed 32-bit, serialized as a bare JSON integer | corpus reaches 0 (`proj-ptp`), 1, 2, 3, 4 (`proj-7vm`); accepted band 0..=4 | exact; a value outside 0..=4 is rejected before storage, and the parse itself rejects anything not fitting a signed 32-bit integer | `src/model/mod.rs:152`, `src/model/mod.rs:181` | list_priority_one, count_by_priority_json, error_create_bad_priority |
| S7.11 | estimated minutes | signed 32-bit option, serialized as a bare JSON integer, omitted when absent | corpus reaches 90 (`create_full_json`); accepted band 0 … 525960 | exact; negative and above-cap values are rejected by validation, and a value outside the signed 32-bit range is rejected by the argument parser with exit 2 | `src/model/mod.rs:515`, `src/validation/mod.rs:112` | create_full_json |
| S7.12 | comment id | signed 64-bit, serialized as a bare JSON integer | corpus reaches 1 (the `basic` fixture's existing comment on `proj-ptp`) and 2 (every `comments add`) | exact. In `--no-db` the ids are minted by a fresh in-memory auto-increment sequence rebuilt from the file on every invocation: an imported comment keeps the id written in the file, and a new comment takes **one more than the largest comment id present anywhere in the store**, not per issue. A comment id of 0 or less fails validation with `id: must be positive` | `src/model/mod.rs:933`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:20975` | comments_add (id 2 on `proj-mta`), comments_add_json, comments_add_author, comments_add_second (id 2 on `proj-ptp`), actor_flag_comment, comments_list_json |
| S7.13 | dependency and dependent counts | pointer-width unsigned, serialized as bare JSON integers | corpus reaches 0, 1 and 2 (`proj-170` has `"dependency_count":2,"dependent_count":1`) | exact | `src/format/output.rs:136` | list_json, list_priority_one |
| S7.14 | record timestamps | signed 64-bit seconds plus an unsigned 32-bit nanosecond field (a chrono UTC instant), rendered per S4.54 | corpus spans 2026-01-01T10:00:00Z through 2027-06-01T09:00:00Z, and `…05.000000001Z` exercises 1 nanosecond | exact. The 0/3/6/9 fraction rule is a rendering choice, not a loss: the stored nanosecond field is preserved. The **printable** instant range is wider than the nanosecond-count range of S7.2, which is why an id seed can silently see `0` | `src/model/mod.rs:519` | edge_precision_list_json, edge_precision_rewrite, defer_until_date |
| S7.15 | relative-duration amount | signed 64-bit | `+1h` reaches 1; the parser accepts −9223372036854775808 … 9223372036854775807 | **checked**, never wrapping: an amount that overflows the integer parse, a unit conversion that overflows, and an addition that leaves the instant range each produce `relative duration is out of supported range` | `src/util/time.rs:195`, `src/util/time.rs:208` | defer_relative |
| S7.16 | `--limit` / `--offset` | pointer-width unsigned (64-bit on the pinned target) | corpus reaches limit 0, 1, 2 and offset 0, 1 | exact; `has_more` uses a **saturating** addition of offset and limit, so the sum never wraps. `0` means unlimited and is what the envelope prints when the user passed no limit | `src/cli/mod.rs:1886`, `src/cli/commands/list.rs:224` | list_limit_one, list_limit_offset, list_json |
| S7.17 | envelope `total` and every `count` | pointer-width unsigned, serialized as bare JSON integers | corpus reaches 0 (`empty_list_json`), 3 (`scn_dup_title`), 6 (`count_json`), 7 (`list_json`), 8 (`stats_json`) | exact | `src/output/context.rs:161` | empty_list_json, count_json, list_json, stats_json |
| S7.18 | stats counters | pointer-width unsigned, serialized as bare JSON integers | corpus reaches 0 … 8 (`stats_json`: total 8, open 5, blocked 2, in-progress 1, closed 1, deferred 1, ready 3, the rest 0) | exact | `src/format/output.rs:254` | stats_json, empty_stats_json |
| S7.19 | epic children counts | pointer-width unsigned | corpus reaches 0 and 3 (`scn_child_ids`: `"total_children":3,"closed_children":0`) | exact | `src/model/mod.rs:889` | epic_status_json, scn_child_ids |
| S7.20 | **average lead time — the only float that reaches an output in scope** Measured (2026-09-23, OQ-078): the JSON number is the binary64 nearest `sum / count` in its shortest round-trip spelling (`1.5`, `1.3333333333333333`, `38.0`). The port computes the double exactly with limb arithmetic (core/float.bend) and prints it as ryu does. | 64-bit binary float option, omitted from JSON when absent | corpus reaches 2.0 (`stats_json`) and absence (`empty_stats_json`) | The per-issue lead time is `closed_at` minus `created_at` **truncated to whole hours toward zero** before it becomes a float, so `proj-mkh`'s 2 h 59 m 53 s contributes 2.0, not 2.998. The mean is the float sum divided by the float count. **JSON printing** is serde's shortest round-trip form, which renders a whole value with a `.0` suffix: `2.0`. **Plain printing** is one fraction digit: when the mean is 24.0 or more it is divided by 24 and printed as `<n.n> days`, otherwise `<n.n> hours`; the one-digit rounding is banker's rounding (RS-07/08, ties to even) | `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:428`, `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:479`, `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:1252` | stats_plain (`  Avg Lead Time:          2.0 hours`), stats_json (`"average_lead_time_hours":2.0`), empty_stats_json (key absent), scn_lead_time_half, scn_lead_time_third |
| S7.21 | percentage bars in `stats` | 64-bit binary float, printed with zero fraction digits and a `%` sign, plus a bar width rounded half-away-from-zero | never reached in scope | out of scope: the only float percentages live on the Rich rendering path, and every captured run is piped and therefore Plain (PLAN §3 excludes Rich). No captured byte depends on them | `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:1514`, `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:1545` | stats_plain (shows the Plain path, no bars) |
| S7.22 | content-hash field length prefix | pointer-width unsigned written as 8 bytes **little-endian** | not observable (S4.51) | exact; the writer copies the low 8 bytes of the length, so a pointer narrower than 64 bits is zero-extended | `src/util/hash.rs:198` | (case to add: none is possible — see S4.51) |
| S7.23 | compaction level and original size | signed 32-bit options; `compaction_level` is always serialized, as `0` when absent, and `original_size` is materialized to `0` on load | corpus shows `"compaction_level":0` and `"original_size":0` on every record | exact | `src/model/mod.rs:624`, `src/model/mod.rs:630` | create_min, edge_precision_rewrite |
| S7.24 | exit codes | signed 32-bit, the process status | corpus reaches 0, 2, 3, 4, 5 | exact; 2 is the argument parser and database-family failures, 3 is not-found / ambiguous / id-collision / invalid-id, 4 is validation, 5 is dependency errors | `src/error/structured.rs:233` | usage_limit_bad (2), error_show_missing (3), error_create_bad_priority (4), error_dep_add_cycle (5) |
| S7.25 | integer printing | every integer above is written by the JSON encoder or by Rust's default integer display | — | no thousands separator, no `+` sign, no padding, no exponent form; a negative value carries a single leading `-`. A JSON integer is never quoted and never gains a `.0` (contrast S7.20's float) | `src/output/context.rs:196` | list_json, count_json, comments_list_json |


## S8. Effect inventory

<!-- from docs/spec-parts/S6_S8_S10.md -->

Every effect on an in-scope `--no-db` path, when it happens, and its failure
behavior. "Read-only commands" means `where`, `version`, `list`, `ready`,
`blocked`, `show`, `search`, `count`, `stats`, `dep list`, `dep tree`,
`dep cycles`, `label list`, `label list-all`, `comments list`, `epic status`,
`epic close-eligible`.

| S8.n | effect | when | failure behavior | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| S8.1 | argv | parsed once at startup, before any file is touched. An unknown subcommand, an unknown flag, a repeated single-use flag or a bad flag value is refused by the argument parser with its own text on **stderr** and exit `2`, before the workspace is discovered | the command never runs; nothing is read or written | `src/main.rs:35` | `usage_none`, `usage_unknown_cmd`, `usage_unknown_flag`, `usage_empty_arg`, `usage_json_twice`, `usage_limit_noval`, `usage_limit_bad`, `usage_unexpected_flag`, `usage_dep_alone`, `usage_label_alone`, `usage_epic_alone`, `usage_search_alone`, `usage_show_alone`, `usage_close_alone`, `usage_update_alone`, `usage_delete_alone`, `usage_dep_add_one_arg` |
| S8.2 | environment: `BEADS_DIR` | read before the upward walk. A non-empty value replaces the walk entirely; the directory is used as-is | not a directory ⇒ `Error: Configuration error: BEADS_DIR not found or not a .beads directory: <path>` on stderr; a whitespace-only value is ignored and the walk runs | `src/config/mod.rs:691`, `:284`, `:707` | probe: `BEADS_DIR=/mnt/other/.beads br --no-db where` prints the redirected path and **omits the `prefix:` line entirely**. (case to add: `env_beads_dir_where` — needs a ws-run mode that sets one extra variable) |
| S8.3 | environment: `USER` | read to resolve the creator of a new issue, the `actor` of every mutation event and the `creator` field of the id seed. The chain is: the `--actor` flag or a configured `actor` key (which `BD_ACTOR` also populates through the generic `BD_<KEY>` sweep), then `USER`, then the literal | unset and unconfigured ⇒ the literal `unknown` | `src/config/mod.rs:7063`–7073, `:6019` | `create_min` (`"created_by":"tester"`, the sandbox's `USER`), `actor_flag_create` (`--actor robot-7` yields `"created_by":"robot-7"`); probe: `USER=zed` yields `"created_by":"zed"`, `env -u USER` yields `"created_by":"unknown"`; `BEADS_ACTOR`, `BR_ACTOR`, `BEADS_USER` and `BEADS_AUTHOR` have **no** effect on `created_by` |
| S8.4 | environment: the comment author chain | `comments add` resolves its author from `--author`, then the configured actor, then the first non-empty of `BD_ACTOR`, `BEADS_ACTOR`, `USER`, `LOGNAME`, `USERNAME`, then `unknown`. This chain is **not** the one used for `created_by` on issues | none of these unset is an error | `src/cli/commands/comments.rs:449`–477 | `comments_add_json` (`"author":"tester"`), `comments_add_author` (`--author zoe`), `actor_flag_comment` (`--actor robot-7` reaches the comment author) |
| S8.5 | environment: `NO_COLOR`, `TERM` | read at startup. A non-empty `NO_COLOR`, `TERM=dumb`, or a non-terminal stdout forces Plain output; a captured run is piped, so Plain is always what the goldens hold | no failure path | `src/logging.rs:29`–30; `src/output/context.rs:763`–768 | every golden, e.g. `list_plain` and `create_min` (neither `.out` holds an escape byte; the sandbox sets `NO_COLOR=1` at `scripts/ws-run.sh:35`); probe: removing `NO_COLOR` from a piped run changes nothing |
| S8.6 | environment: `RUST_LOG` | read once to build the tracing filter; all tracing output goes to **stderr** | an unparsable value silently falls back to the verbosity-derived default | `src/logging.rs:64`, `:83`–107 | the sandbox pins `RUST_LOG=error` (`scripts/ws-run.sh:35`), which is why no golden carries tracing noise — `create_min` and `list_plain` both have a zero-byte `.err`. (case to add: `env_rust_log_debug` — needs a ws-run mode that sets one extra variable, then `["@fx=basic","list"]`) |
| S8.7 | environment: `BR_OUTPUT_FORMAT`, `TOON_DEFAULT_FORMAT` | read at startup to pick the default output format and to decide whether errors are rendered as JSON on stdout. Values are trimmed and lowercased; accepted: `text`, `plain`, `json`, `csv`, `toon` | an unrecognized value falls through to the next source | `src/cli/mod.rs:1651`–1672; `src/main.rs:36` | probe: `BR_OUTPUT_FORMAT=json br --no-db count` prints `{"count":10}` where the flagless run prints `10`. (case to add: `env_output_format_json`) |
| S8.8 | environment: `BEADS_JSONL` | highest-priority override of the JSONL path, above `metadata.json` | a whitespace-only value is ignored | `src/config/mod.rs:5899` | (case to add: `env_beads_jsonl_elsewhere`) |
| S8.9 | environment: `BEADS_CACHE_DIR` | relocates `last-touched` out of `.beads/` | an empty value is ignored | `src/util/mod.rs:38`, `:48` | (case to add: `env_beads_cache_dir`) |
| S8.10 | environment: `BR_DISABLE_PARALLEL_JSONL_EXPORT` | read at export time; any value other than `0` or `false` (case-insensitive) forces a single-threaded export Measured (2026-09-24, OQ-087): the variable changes no byte of the export (`env_serial_export`, the same sha256 as the same `update` without it). | no failure path; the bytes written are identical either way | `src/sync/mod.rs:10846` | every mutating case exercises the default; (case to add: `env_serial_export` to pin that the bytes do not change), env_serial_export |
| S8.11 | environment: the remaining reads on in-scope paths | `HOME` (user-level config layers), `BD_<KEY>` for any key (each becomes a configuration key, including `BD_ACTOR`), `BD_DB` / `BD_DATABASE` / `BEADS_DB`, `BEADS_FLUSH_DEBOUNCE`, `BEADS_IDENTITY`, `BEADS_REMOTE_SYNC_INTERVAL`, `BEADS_AUTO_START_DAEMON`, `BR_HISTORY_MIN_INTERVAL_SECS`, `BR_HISTORY_MAX_BYTES`, `BR_SESSION`, `BR_INHERITED_CONTEXT`, `BR_STARTUP_CACHE`, `BR_STARTUP_CACHE_DIR`, `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, `COLUMNS`, `TOON_STATS`, `TMPDIR`, `COMPLETE` (the completion hook, which exits before anything else), and `PATH` (only `stats` uses it, to find `git`). `TZ` is never read by name but is consulted implicitly when a date argument is a keyword such as `today` or `next-week` | `HOME` unset ⇒ the user-level config layers are skipped, not an error. An existing but unparsable `config.yaml` at any layer is a hard error | `src/config/mod.rs:216`, `:231`, `:677`, `:6019`–6037, `:5599`, `:6375`, `:6661`; `src/sync/history.rs:712`; `src/cli/commands/mod.rs:298`; `src/inheritance.rs:67`; `src/format/text.rs:229`; `src/util/time.rs:68` | the sandbox clears the environment and passes only `PATH`, `HOME`, `USER`, `TZ`, `NO_COLOR`, `RUST_LOG`, plus the harness's own variables, so every golden runs with all of the above unset (`scripts/ws-run.sh:34`–35), e.g. `list_plain` and `create_min`; the implicit `TZ` read is exercised by the bare-date cases `defer_until_date` and `create_full_json` (09:00 local converted under `TZ=UTC`, `src/util/time.rs:44`–47). No existing case passes a keyword date: (case to add: `defer_until_tomorrow` — `["@fx=basic","defer","proj-ptp","--until","tomorrow","--json"]`) |
| S8.12 | current directory and the upward walk | the process's current directory is read once; the walk tries `<dir>/.beads`, then `<dir>/_beads`, then moves to the parent, and stops only at the filesystem root. There is no `$HOME` ceiling, no `.git` ceiling and no depth limit. The walk is lexical on the already-resolved current directory | no workspace anywhere ⇒ `Error: Beads not initialized: run 'br init' first` with `Hint: Run: br init` on stderr, exit `2`; in JSON mode the envelope `{"error":{"code":"NOT_INITIALIZED",…,"context":null}}` pretty-printed on stdout | `src/config/mod.rs:299`–364 | `error_no_workspace_list`, `error_no_workspace_list_json`, `error_no_workspace_create`; probe: running from `/mnt/proj/sub/deep` finds `/mnt/proj/.beads` |

A merge-conflict marker is refused before parsing. A malformed line aborts the whole command | `src/config/mod.rs:5395`, `:5415`; `src/sync/mod.rs:14631`, `:13501` | `empty_list`, `list_plain`, `error_conflict_markers` (exit `7`, the three marker lines named with their line numbers), `error_conflict_markers_json`, `error_malformed_jsonl` (`Error: Configuration error: Invalid JSON at line 2: EOF while parsing a string at line 1 column 40`, exit `7`), `error_malformed_jsonl_json`, edge_large_count, edge_large_list_limit, edge_large_create (fixture `large`: 512 records, 167,808 bytes — the store size at which a port that joins its read chunks with `List.concat` faults on the JS lane, DISC-008) |

| S8.15 | file read: `.beads/config.yaml` and the user-level layers | four layers merged lowest to highest: `$HOME/.beads/config.yaml`, then `$HOME/.config/beads/config.yaml` (or `$HOME/.config/bd/config.yaml` when the first is absent), then `<beads_dir>/config.yaml`, then the environment layer | a missing file is an empty layer; an existing but unreadable or unparsable file is a hard error | `src/config/mod.rs:6009`–6022, `:6197`–6227, `:6538` | the sandbox sets `HOME=/mnt/home`, which is empty, and the fixtures ship no `config.yaml`, so every golden runs on the built-in defaults; the harness copies `goldens/fixtures/<fx>.config.yaml` into place when one exists (`scripts/ws_inner.py` `setup`) — `scripts/ws_inner.py:62`; `create_min` and `list_plain` therefore run on the defaults. | S8.16 | file read: `.beads/redirect` | read during discovery, after a `.beads` directory is found. The content is trimmed; a relative target resolves against the `.beads` directory itself; chains are followed to depth 10 | absent ⇒ no redirect. Empty ⇒ a warning and no redirect. Larger than 4096 bytes ⇒ `Redirect file exceeds maximum size of 4096 bytes`; non-UTF-8 ⇒ `Redirect file must be valid UTF-8`; a loop ⇒ `Redirect loop detected: X -> Y`; depth 10 ⇒ `Redirect chain exceeds max depth (10)`; a missing or wrongly named target ⇒ `Redirect target not found` / `Redirect target must be a .beads or _beads directory` | `src/config/routing.rs:180`–310 | no fixture ships one. (case to add: `edge_redirect_relative` and `edge_redirect_loop`) |

| S8.23 | file written: the scratch database | **every** `--no-db` invocation, read-only ones included, creates `<temp dir>/beads_mem_<pid>_<counter>.db` together with its `-wal`, `-shm` and engine sidecars, imports the whole JSONL into it, and unlinks it when the storage object is dropped. The counter starts at `0` per process | creation failure is retried 8 times with a 2 ms→100 ms backoff, removing the partial files each time. A `SIGKILL`, a second `SIGINT` or a `SIGPIPE` kill leaks the files | `src/storage/sqlite.rs:2694`–2736 | probe: during `br --no-db list` the files `/tmp/beads_mem_<pid>_0.db`, `…-wal`, `…-shm`, `…-fsqlite-ns-gate`, `…-fsqlite-ns-use`, `….fsqlite-migration-state` exist and are gone afterwards. Out of the port's scope (PLAN §3 excludes the SQLite store) but it is the reason a "read-only" run is not read-only on the filesystem. The probe's argv is that of `list_plain`; no golden can show the files, which live outside the workspace |
| S8.24 | file written: `.beads/issues.jsonl` | only on a mutating command, and only when the store is actually dirty. The bytes are staged into `.beads/issues.jsonl.<pid>.tmp` created exclusively with mode `0600`, written record by record with a `\n` after each, flushed, `fsync`ed, re-hashed and compared, then published by a flag-carrying rename (no-replace when the destination was absent, exchange when it was present) followed by an `fsync` of the parent directory. After an exchange the old file sits at the temp name and is removed | a hash mismatch between the staged bytes and the streamed content ⇒ `Staged JSONL bytes do not match the export content hash`, the staged file retained. A filesystem without the rename flags falls back to a witness-checked plain rename with a warning. The bytes are the id followed by exactly one `\n` and nothing else (`proj-fyw\n`, 9 bytes for a 8-character id). Mode `0600`, truncating, no `fsync`, no temp file. In `--no-db` mode it is written **after** the JSONL flush succeeds; in database mode before | **every error is discarded silently** — a failure to open or write leaves the sidecar stale and the command still succeeds | `src/util/mod.rs:86`–106; ordering `src/cli/commands/create.rs:159`–167 | `create_min`, `create_silent`, `update_status`, `close_basic`, `label_add`, `comments_add`, `dep_add_blocks`, `scn_last_touched`; probe: `xxd .beads/last-touched` shows `70 72 6f 6a 2d 75 35 6d 0a` |

| S8.27 | files written: the lock sidecars | a mutating `--no-db` command creates `.beads/.br-jsonl-write-<24 hex>.lock` and holds an advisory lock on it for the whole command. A capture also shows `.beads/.br-db-write-<24 hex>.lock` and `.beads/.write.lock`. All three are zero bytes and are **never deleted**. The hex suffix is the first 24 characters of a SHA-256 over the artifact's path, so the same workspace always produces the same names | a contended lock queues through a `.write-waiters.lock/` directory holding one file per waiter | `src/sync/mod.rs:1374`, `:1389`, `:266`, `:1762`; acquisition `src/config/mod.rs:5386` | probe after `create` on `basic`: `.beads` holds `.br-db-write-ded993a0aa2bb60f132e9ae2.lock`, `.br-jsonl-write-c9f16ff8a0bce34cff66a1d1.lock`, `.write.lock`, all zero bytes. The harness dumps only `issues.jsonl` and `last-touched`, so no golden carries them. DISC-002, DISC-004. The probe's argv shape is that of `create_with_parent` (a `create` on `basic`). (case to add: `edge_beads_dir_listing_mutating` — needs a ws-run mode that lists `.beads/` recursively after the step) |
| S8.28 | files written: `.beads/.br_history/` backups | before every JSONL export. The backup is `issues.<YYYYMMDD>_<HHMMSS>_<9-digit nanoseconds>.jsonl` plus a sidecar `issues.<same stamp>.jsonl.meta.json`, both created exclusively, `fsync`ed, with the parent directory `fsync`ed. A name collision appends `.<1..1023>`. The snapshot is skipped when the newest backup's recorded hash equals the current source's, and when the newest backup is younger than 5 seconds (overridable). Retention: newest 100, maximum age 30 days, then a global 1 GiB byte budget pruned oldest-first, always keeping the globally newest pair | the directory being a symlink or a non-directory is refused; a partially written backup is removed | `src/sync/history.rs:209`–250, `:541`–614, `:676`–728 | probe after `create` at the pinned instant: `.beads/.br_history/issues.20260102_030405_000000000.jsonl` and `issues.20260102_030405_000000000.jsonl.meta.json`, the sidecar holding `{"target":{"kind":"relative","path":"issues.jsonl"},"protected":false,"raw_sha256":"03d6b9206c8032422636b97c676347f3b1ff3f5c9c4755b520b7a306e3616fd9"}`. DISC-004. The recorded hash is the SHA-256 of `goldens/fixtures/basic.jsonl`, so the probe's argv shape is that of `create_with_parent` (a `create` on `basic`). (case to add: `edge_beads_dir_listing_mutating` — the harness mode of S8.27) |

This settles the "do read-only commands ever create files" half of OQ-008 for `.beads/`; they do create the scratch database of S8.23 outside the workspace |
| S8.30 | the clock: one `create` | one reading of the wall clock supplies the id seed's nanosecond timestamp, `created_at`, `updated_at` and the `created_at` of every dependency edge the command generates. All of them are therefore equal | no failure path | `src/cli/commands/create.rs:456`; edges at `:553` | `create_min` (`created_at` and `updated_at` both `2026-01-02T03:04:05Z`), `create_with_parent` and `create_with_deps` (the edge's `created_at` equals the issue's); probe with the real clock: issue `created_at` `…29.124238920Z`, `updated_at` the same, both edges the same |
| S8.31 | the clock: one invocation observes several instants | a mutation takes a fresh reading for the record's `updated_at`, another for each event row and another for the dirty marker. `close` computes `closed_at` in the command layer and `updated_at` later in the storage layer, so `closed_at` is strictly earlier. `comments add` takes the comment's `created_at` from the database engine at second resolution and the issue's `updated_at` from the process clock at nanosecond resolution | no failure path | `src/cli/commands/update.rs:1823`; `src/storage/sqlite.rs:7813`, `:20978`, `:14153`, `:1815`, `:6562` | invisible under the pinned clock (`close_basic`, `comments_add_json` all show the one pinned instant); probe with the real clock: after `comments add`, the comment's `created_at` is `2026-09-20T06:12:29Z` while the issue's `updated_at` is `2026-09-20T06:12:29.705963839Z` — the same command, two different instants at two different precisions |
| S8.32 | the clock: monotonic readings | a monotonic clock is read only for lock budgets, import timing and poll deadlines; no monotonic value ever reaches an output | not applicable | `src/config/mod.rs:5427`; `src/sync/mod.rs:1641`, `:1661` | the sandbox sets `DONT_FAKE_MONOTONIC=1` precisely because a fully frozen monotonic clock hangs `br` (PLAN §2) — `scripts/ws_inner.py:79`; `create_min` and `list_json_again` run under it and carry no elapsed-time value |
| S8.33 | randomness | the id nonce is a deterministic counter, not a random value: nonces `0..9` are tried at each hash length, the length then grows, and a fallback ladder follows at length 12. The only entropy in a new id is the nanosecond timestamp and the actor string. No random number generator, no UUID and no entropy source is called on any in-scope path. The one real random value in the tree names a lock-waiter file and never reaches an output | exhausting the ladder ⇒ an id-collision error | `src/util/id.rs:213`–295 | `scn_dup_title` (three creates of `Same title` at one pinned instant yield `proj-86w`, `proj-e1f`, `proj-fa9`), `create_min` reproducing `proj-fyw` byte-for-byte across every capture |
| S8.34 | stdout and stderr: which stream | plain text and every successful `--json` document go to stdout. Warnings, notes and human-form errors go to stderr. In JSON mode the structured error envelope goes to **stdout**, deliberately, so that a machine reader sees one parseable stream. Tracing output always goes to stderr | not applicable | `src/output/context.rs:1261`, `:1277`; `src/main.rs:1875`–1896; `src/logging.rs:33` | `error_show_missing` (message and hint on stderr, empty stdout, exit `3`), `error_show_missing_json` (envelope on stdout, empty stderr, exit `3`), `ready_limit` (`[note] Showing 1 of 3 ready issues. Use --limit 0 for all results.` on stderr while the list is on stdout), `dep_remove_absent` (`Warning: Dependency not found: proj-mta → proj-ptp` on stderr, exit `0`) |
| S8.35 | stdout buffering and flush order | plain text is printed through the unlocked global handle with no explicit flush; JSON is streamed through a locked buffered writer that ends with a `\n` and an explicit flush. Every deliberate exit funnels through one routine that flushes stdout and then stderr before the process dies. On a human-mode error path the unbuffered stderr message therefore reaches a pipe **before** any buffered stdout content, so the relative interleaving of the two streams is not fixed when stdout is redirected | a serialization failure is recorded and reported on stderr and turns into a non-zero exit code | `src/output/context.rs:225`–228, `:1042`; `src/shutdown.rs:207`–233 | the harness captures the two streams separately, so no golden depends on the interleaving; `error_close_blocked_json` is the one case where stdout carries two JSON documents in order — the command result `{"closed":[],"skipped":[…]}` and then the pretty error envelope |
| S8.36 | process exit codes | the full ladder is `0` success · `1` internal error · `2` database and not-initialized errors, and every argument-parser refusal · `3` issue not found, ambiguous id, id collision, invalid id, nothing-to-do, close-incomplete · `4` validation failure, invalid status, invalid type, invalid priority, required field, policy violation, capacity exceeded · `5` cycle detected, dependency not found, has dependents, self-dependency, duplicate dependency · `6` JSONL parse error, prefix mismatch, import collision, sync conflict, conflict markers, path traversal · `7` configuration errors · `8` I/O, JSON and YAML errors · `130` cooperative shutdown after an interrupt. The corpus exercises `0`, `2`, `3`, `4`, `5` and `7`. Note that conflict markers and a malformed JSONL line are classified as **configuration** errors (`7`), not as the JSONL-parse errors of `6`. Exit is by an explicit call that skips destructors, which is why the storage handle is dropped before returning | not applicable | `src/error/structured.rs:220`–278; `src/main.rs:57`–60, `:1896`; `src/shutdown.rs:207` | `create_min` (`0`), `error_no_workspace_list` (`2`), `usage_unknown_cmd` (`2`), `error_show_missing` (`3`), `close_already_closed` (`3`), `error_create_bad_priority` (`4`), `list_sort_bad` (`4`), `error_comments_add_empty` (`4`), `update_status_closed` (`4`), `error_dep_add_cycle` (`5`), `error_dep_add_self` (`5`), `error_conflict_markers` (`7`), `error_malformed_jsonl` (`7`) |
| S8.37 | signals: `SIGPIPE` | the default disposition is restored at startup for every command **except** those producing structured output. In plain mode a closed downstream pipe therefore kills the process at the first write, with status `141` and no message, exactly as a stream filter behaves. In `--json` mode the signal stays ignored and the broken pipe is absorbed as a non-error with exit `0` | the kill skips destructors, so the scratch database of S8.23 is leaked | `src/shutdown.rs:78`–103; gate `src/main.rs:1844`–1857 | no golden exercises a closed pipe; the harness captures both streams fully. (case to add: `edge_sigpipe_plain` — needs a harness mode that closes stdout early) |
| S8.38 | signals: `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM`, `SIGHUP` | a handler thread is installed before argument parsing. The first signal records `128 + signal` and sets a flag the main thread checks once, after the command finishes and before any auto-flush. The second matching signal exits immediately without flushing or unlinking anything | if the handler cannot be installed the default action stands; that failure is only logged | `src/shutdown.rs:57`–176 | no golden exercises a signal. (case to add: `edge_sigint_during_mutation` — needs a harness mode that sends `SIGINT` to the running step, on `["@fx=basic","update","proj-mta","-p","0"]`) |
| S8.39 | git and the network | **none on the in-scope surface, with one qualification.** No in-scope command opens a socket; no HTTP client is compiled into the pinned binary except behind the self-update feature. Worktree resolution and repository detection parse `.git` files by hand rather than spawning git. The qualification: `stats` spawns `git log` and `git diff` for its activity section unless `--no-activity` or quiet mode is in force, and `version --check` spawns `curl`. PLAN §3 excludes commit activity in `stats`, and every failure is swallowed, so the activity section is simply absent | a missing `git` binary silently omits the activity section; a missing `curl` for `version --check` ⇒ an external-command error and exit `2` | `src/cli/commands/stats.rs:717`–731, `:1158`, `:280`; `src/cli/commands/version.rs:284`–291; `src/config/mod.rs:433` | `stats_plain` and `stats_json` (no activity section at all — the sandbox has no git repository), `empty_stats`, `version_plain`, `version_json` (plain `version` spawns nothing) |
| S8.40 | telemetry, log files and caches | no telemetry of any kind. No log file: the logging subsystem supports one but no command-line flag or environment variable supplies a path, so all logging is stderr-only and ephemeral. The startup cache is the only cache file and it is off unless `BR_STARTUP_CACHE` is truthy, so by default no cache file is written | not applicable | `src/main.rs:39`; `src/logging.rs:44`; `src/config/mod.rs:6647` | every golden, e.g. `create_min` and `list_plain` (zero-byte `.err`, no file dumped beyond the two state files); probe confirms nothing under `/mnt/home` |
| S8.41 | the completion hook | before any other work, the argument parser's dynamic-completion hook runs and, when its environment variable is set, exits before the workspace is discovered | not applicable | `src/main.rs:26` | no golden sets it; PLAN §3 excludes `completions`. (case to add: `env_complete_hook` — needs a ws-run mode that sets `COMPLETE=bash`, then `["list"]`) |
| S8.42 | which commands write the store, and which write only the sidecar | a command writes `.beads/issues.jsonl` exactly when it actually changed a record, and writes `.beads/last-touched` whenever it addressed one, whether or not anything changed. The harness dumps a file only when its hash moved, so a golden with an `issues.jsonl` section changed the store and one with only a `last-touched` section did not | see S8.24, S8.25; the sidecar write is unconditional at `src/cli/commands/label.rs:253`, `:334`, `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:595`, `:766`, `src/cli/commands/update.rs:525`, and skipped on a dry run at `src/cli/commands/create.rs:165`; the store is exported only when dirty, `src/config/mod.rs:4921`; the harness dumps a file only when its hash moved, `scripts/ws_inner.py:136`; `goldens/label_add_existing.out:2` (the only dump is `last-touched`) | store **and** sidecar: `create_title_flag`, `create_status_in_progress`, `create_unicode`, `update_status_json`, `update_priority`, `update_assignee`, `update_claim`, `close_suggest_next`, `close_blocked_force`, `reopen_closed`, `reopen_closed_json`, `undefer_basic`, `dep_add_json`, `dep_remove_basic`, `label_add_json`, `label_rename`. Sidecar only: `label_add_existing`, `label_remove_absent`, `dep_add_duplicate`, `dep_remove_absent`, `update_noop_same_value`, `create_ephemeral`. Neither: `create_dry_run`, `create_dry_run_json`, `delete_dry_run`, and every read-only case |
| S8.43 | a refused command touches nothing | when validation or resolution fails, no record is written, `last-touched` is not written, and the process exits before any export. The harness proves it by dumping no state section at all | not applicable | `src/config/mod.rs:4918` (a clean store skips the flush) | `error_create_empty_title`, `error_create_blank_title`, `error_create_long_title`, `error_create_bad_priority`, `error_create_bad_label`, `error_create_missing_parent`, `error_update_missing`, `error_update_bad_priority`, `error_update_blocked_claim`, `error_close_missing`, `error_reopen_missing`, `error_delete_missing`, `error_dep_add_missing`, `error_dep_add_self`, `error_dep_add_cycle`, `error_dep_add_cycle_json`, `error_comments_add_empty`, `error_comments_add_missing`, `error_label_add_invalid`, `error_defer_bad_until`, `update_status_closed`, `close_already_closed`, `error_close_blocked`, `error_close_blocked_json`, `close_epic_with_open_child` |

## S9. Error and edge behavior

<!-- from docs/spec-parts/S1_S9.md -->

### S9.1 The two error renderings

| S9.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S9.1 | Every error that survives argument parsing is turned into one structured value with five fields — `code`, `message`, `hint`, `retryable`, `context` — and then rendered in exactly one of two ways, chosen by the same test that selects JSON output (S1.6) *plus* `--format json`/`--format toon` on the command: human, or JSON. There is never both. The process exits with the code the error's category gives (S9.20) and no further output follows. | `src/main.rs:1875`–`src/main.rs:1896`, `src/main.rs:1816`–`src/main.rs:1830` | error_show_missing, error_show_missing_json |
| S9.2 | **Human rendering** goes to **stderr** and is `Error: ` + message, and when a hint exists, LF + `Hint: ` + hint; one trailing LF closes the whole thing. Stdout receives nothing from the error itself. Control characters in the message and hint other than LF and TAB are replaced by their Rust escape spelling (`\u{7}` and the like) before printing; the JSON rendering does not do this. | `src/error/structured.rs:606`–`src/error/structured.rs:631`, `src/format/text.rs:49`–`src/format/text.rs:82` | error_no_workspace_list, error_close_missing |
| S9.3 | **JSON rendering** goes to **stdout** (not stderr), pretty-printed with two-space indentation and one trailing LF, as a single object whose only key is `error`, whose value has exactly these five keys in this fixed order: `code`, `message`, `hint`, `retryable`, `context`. An absent hint or context is the literal `null` — the keys are always present. Example, `error_no_workspace_list_json`: `{` LF `  "error": {` LF `    "code": "NOT_INITIALIZED",` LF `    "message": "Beads not initialized: run 'br init' first",` LF `    "hint": "Run: br init",` LF `    "retryable": false,` LF `    "context": null` LF `  }` LF `}` LF. | `src/error/structured.rs:593`–`src/error/structured.rs:603`, `src/main.rs:1884`–`src/main.rs:1891` | error_no_workspace_list_json, error_conflict_markers_json, error_malformed_jsonl_json |
| S9.4 | A clap parse failure never uses either rendering: it is plain text on stderr with exit `2` and empty stdout, whatever the output mode (S1.30). The port's error table therefore has two disjoint halves: exit `2` from parsing (S1.15–S1.24) and exit `1`–`8` from the structured errors below. | `src/main.rs:35` | usage_limit_bad, usage_json_twice |
| S9.5 | `retryable` is a function of the code alone, except for a write-lock timeout, which carries its own flag. The codes reachable in `--no-db` mode that set `retryable` to `true` are `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `INVALID_STATUS`, `INVALID_TYPE`, `INVALID_PRIORITY`, `REQUIRED_FIELD` and `AMBIGUOUS_ID`; every other reachable code sets it to `false`. | `src/error/structured.rs:190`–`src/error/structured.rs:206`, `src/error/structured.rs:317`–`src/error/structured.rs:322` | error_create_bad_priority_json, error_show_ambiguous_json, error_show_missing_json |

### S9.6 The exit-code map

| S9.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|

### S9.8 The error table

Each row gives the trigger, the verbatim human stderr, the JSON envelope's
`code`/`hint`/`retryable`/`context` keys, and the exit code. Stdout is empty
for every row unless the row says otherwise. The hint, where one exists, is
the second stderr line and the `hint` value; where the row says "none" the
`Hint:` line is absent and `hint` is `null`.

### S9.26 Warnings, tolerant no-ops and edge behavior

| S9.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S9.26 | A per-item skip inside a batch is a **warning on stderr**, written before the summary error (when one follows) and **suppressed by `--quiet`** (amended 2026-09-20: the first text said "never suppressed"; running the original shows `--quiet` drops the `Warning:` lines, `src/output/context.rs:1278`; OQ-015): `Warning: Skipped <id>: <reason>` LF. The reasons observed are `already closed`, `blocked by: <id> — close the open blocker(s) first, or use --force to close anyway`, and `epic has <n>/<m> open children (use --force to close anyway)`. A missing dependency edge on `dep remove` warns as `Warning: Dependency not found: <from> → <to>` LF, with `→` (not `->`), and the run still exits `0`. | Goldens: `goldens/error_close_blocked.err:1` (`blocked by:` reason), `goldens/close_already_closed.err:1` (`already closed`), `goldens/close_epic_with_open_child.err:1` (`epic has 1/1 open children`), `goldens/dep_remove_absent.err:1` with `goldens/dep_remove_absent.exit:1` = `0`. Source: `src/output/context.rs:1277` (Plain mode writes `Warning: ` + message to stderr), `src/cli/commands/close.rs:469` (`Skipped <id>: <reason>`), `src/cli/commands/close.rs:766` (one warning per skipped item, before the summary error is returned), reasons at `src/cli/commands/close.rs:940` (`already <status>`), `src/cli/commands/close.rs:1109` (`blocked by: …`), `src/cli/commands/close.rs:959` (`… open children …`); `src/cli/commands/dep.rs:801` (`Dependency not found: … → …`). Conflict to settle (OQ-015): `src/output/context.rs:1278` makes the warning a no-op in Quiet, JSON and TOON modes, and the sandbox run of 2026-09-20 (`close proj-170 --quiet` on `@fx=basic`) wrote only the `Error:` and `Hint:` lines to stderr — the `Warning:` line WAS suppressed, contrary to this clause's "never suppressed by `--quiet`" | error_close_blocked, close_already_closed, close_epic_with_open_child, dep_remove_absent |
| S9.27 | In JSON mode a partially-successful batch prints **two** JSON documents to stdout: first the command's own compact result document with its trailing LF, then the pretty-printed error envelope. Golden `error_close_blocked_json` is `{"closed":[],"skipped":[{"id":"proj-170","reason":"blocked by: proj-mta — close the open blocker(s) first, or use --force to close anyway"}]}` LF followed by the envelope of S9.3 with code `NOTHING_TO_DO` — eleven lines here, because a non-null `context` adds a nested object (the whole `.out` file is twelve lines; the `NOT_INITIALIZED` envelope with `"context": null` is nine). Stderr is empty. A consumer that reads one document per stream is wrong about this case. | `src/main.rs:1884`; golden bytes | error_close_blocked_json |
| S9.28 | Several conditions that read like errors are tolerant no-ops with exit `0` and a message on **stdout**: `dep add` of an existing edge prints `Dependency already exists: <from> → <to>` LF; `label add` of a label already present prints `✓ Label <name> already exists on <id>` LF; `label remove` of an absent label prints `✓ Label <name> not found on <id> (no-op)` LF; `reopen` of an already-open issue prints `⊘ Skipped <id>: already open` LF; `undefer` of a non-deferred issue prints `⊘ Skipped <id>: not deferred (status: <status>)` LF. None of these produces `DUPLICATE_DEPENDENCY` or any other exit-5 code, although those codes exist in the source. The GOLDEN wins over the code's category names. | goldens' `.out`/`.exit` bytes; `src/error/structured.rs:161` (`DUPLICATE_DEPENDENCY` unreached) | dep_add_duplicate, label_add_existing, label_remove_absent, reopen_open, undefer_not_deferred |
| S9.29 | `delete` of an issue that has dependents is likewise not an error: without `--force`/`--cascade` it prints the dependent report to stdout and exits `0` (`The following issues depend on issues being deleted:` LF `  - <id>` LF …), and with `--force` it deletes and orphans, also exit `0`. `HAS_DEPENDENTS` (exit 5) is therefore unreached in scope. | goldens' `.out`/`.exit`; `src/error/mod.rs:209` | delete_with_dependents, delete_with_dependents_force, delete_cascade |

DISC-005 canonicalizes the list to ascending byte order of the id in all three places, applied identically to the original and to the port, and the goldens are captured through that canonicalizer. The canonical order for `show m` on `@fx=basic` is `["proj-7vm", "proj-mkh", "proj-mta"]` — note the `, ` separator with a space, and the double quotes inside the human message. | DISC-005; `src/error/mod.rs:69` (`{matches:?}`), probe RS-01 | show_partial_ambiguous, error_show_ambiguous_json, error_update_ambiguous |
| S9.35 | **Partial output at the moment of failure.** A validation or resolution failure is raised before any store write, so the workspace is untouched and no golden for such a case carries a `--- .beads/issues.jsonl ---` dump. A batch failure of S9.14 is the opposite: the successful items are already written when the error is printed, and the golden's stdout continues with the rewritten store. `create --dry-run` and `delete --dry-run` print their preview and write nothing. | goldens: absence/presence of the dump; `src/main.rs:1875` (`handle_error` runs after destructors are in scope) | error_create_bad_priority, delete_dry_run, create_dry_run, (case to add: `error_close_partial`) |
| S9.36 | **Order of checks on startup.** Argument parsing (exit 2, S9.4) precedes workspace discovery (S9.8, exit 2), which precedes the conflict-marker scan (S9.23, exit 7), which precedes JSON parsing of the store (S9.24, exit 7), which precedes id resolution (S9.9–S9.11, exit 3), which precedes field validation (S9.17, exit 4), which precedes graph checks (S9.21–S9.22, exit 5). Evidence: fixture `conflict` holds four syntactically valid JSON lines and two marker lines, and reports the markers rather than a parse error; `usage_limit_bad` fails with exit 2 on `@fx=` default rather than reaching the store. | `src/main.rs:35`, `src/main.rs:70`, `src/sync/mod.rs:10352` | usage_limit_bad, error_conflict_markers, error_malformed_jsonl, error_show_missing |
| S9.37 | **Streams never interleave within one run.** Human errors, warnings and the `tracing` log all go to stderr; every command result and every JSON error envelope goes to stdout. A command therefore never writes an error to stdout in human mode, and never writes an error to stderr in JSON mode. The sandbox captures the two streams into separate files, so no golden pins their relative ordering. | `src/main.rs:1877`–`src/main.rs:1894`, `src/logging.rs` | error_close_blocked (stderr only), error_close_blocked_json (stdout only) |

### S9.38 Coverage note

| S9.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S9.38 | Five cases of `cases.tsv` exercise no behavior of S1 or S9 and are cited by no clause here, deliberately: `edge_precision_list_json`, `edge_precision_sort_created`, `edge_precision_sort_title`, `edge_precision_show_json` and `edge_precision_rewrite` pin timestamp ordering and store rewriting (S4/S5/S6, OQ-001 and OQ-002), and the three scenarios `scn_lifecycle`, `scn_dup_title`, `scn_child_ids` pin multi-step state and id generation (S4/S8). Their argv is nonetheless covered by the option inventories of S1.31, S1.35, S1.39 and by the write-path edge rule of S9.35. | The argv of each case as listed: `goldens/cases.tsv:228` (edge_precision_list_json), `goldens/cases.tsv:230` (edge_precision_sort_created), `goldens/cases.tsv:231` (edge_precision_sort_title), `goldens/cases.tsv:232` (edge_precision_show_json), `goldens/cases.tsv:233` (edge_precision_rewrite), `goldens/cases.tsv:243` (scn_lifecycle), `goldens/cases.tsv:245` (scn_dup_title), `goldens/cases.tsv:246` (scn_child_ids); OQ-001, OQ-002 | edge_precision_list_json, edge_precision_sort_created, edge_precision_sort_title, edge_precision_show_json, edge_precision_rewrite, scn_lifecycle, scn_dup_title, scn_child_ids |


## S10. Known bugs and oddities (to be reproduced, not fixed)

<!-- from docs/spec-parts/S6_S8_S10.md -->

| S10.n | behavior | why it is a bug | reproduce with case | decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S10.1 | `""` for `description` and `assignee` vanishes on a rewrite, while `""` for `compacted_at_commit` survives | three optional string fields of the same shape are treated differently: two empty strings are normalized away, the third is preserved verbatim. A reimplementer who applies one rule to all optional strings loses or gains bytes | `goldens/edge_precision_rewrite` — the input record for `proj-aaf` carries all three as `""`; the rewritten line keeps `compacted_at_commit` and drops the other two. `goldens/edge_precision_show_json` shows all three as `""` before the rewrite (`goldens/edge_precision_rewrite.out:8`, `goldens/edge_precision_show_json.out:1`; `src/storage/sqlite.rs:15946` and `:15954` read `description` and `assignee` back through the non-empty reader, `:15977` reads `compacted_at_commit` verbatim; the import list `src/sync/mod.rs:14403`–14420 names the first two and not the third) | bug-compatible (default) · OQ-002 resolved; the per-field table is OQ-007 |
| S10.2 | every mutation rewrites and normalizes **all** records, not only the one that changed | an unrelated record loses its unknown fields, gains defaults it never had, and has its timestamps and label arrays rewritten, purely because some other issue was touched | `goldens/edge_precision_rewrite` — `update proj-aaf -p 1` rewrites all eight records: `unknown_field` is dropped everywhere, `…05.1Z` becomes `…05.100Z`, `12:00:04+02:00` becomes `10:00:04Z`, and records gain `"source_repo":"."`, `"compaction_level":0`, `"original_size":0` | bug-compatible (default) · OQ-002 |
| S10.3 | a bare date for `--until` and `--due` lands at 09:00:00, not at midnight | `2027-06-01` becomes `2027-06-01T09:00:00Z`. Midnight is the obvious reading and is what a reimplementer writes | `goldens/defer_until_date` (`"defer_until":"2027-06-01T09:00:00+00:00"`), `goldens/create_full_json` (`--due 2026-03-01` becomes `"due_at":"2026-03-01T09:00:00Z"`) | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.4 | the same instant is spelled two different ways in one command's output | the command's JSON result spells an instant with a numeric offset (`+00:00`) while the JSONL line spells the same instant with `Z` | `goldens/defer_until_date` — the result says `"defer_until":"2027-06-01T09:00:00+00:00"`, the rewritten record says `"defer_until":"2027-06-01T09:00:00Z"`. Same in `goldens/close_basic` and `goldens/close_reason_json` for `closed_at` | bug-compatible (default) |
| S10.5 | the JSON error envelope is pretty-printed with two-space indentation on **stdout**, while every successful JSON document is compact | two different serializers on the same stream. ## S11. Performance characteristics of the original

<!-- from docs/spec-parts/ARCHITECT_S3_S11_S12.md -->

| S11.n | clause | provenance | cases |
|---|---|---|---|
| S11.1 | In `--no-db` mode every invocation opens a private in-memory SQLite database and imports the whole of `issues.jsonl` into it before answering, so the cost of every command, reads included, grows with the size of the store | `src/config/mod.rs:4458` (the `--no-db` open path; S2.A) | `list_json`, `ready_json` |
| S11.2 | Every mutating invocation rewrites the whole store, normalizing every record, whatever the number of records it changed (OQ-002) | `src/sync/mod.rs:10613`, `src/sync/mod.rs:12293` (export and normalization; S5.A) | `edge_precision_rewrite`, `update_priority` |
| S11.3 | The hot computations of the in-scope surface are: JSON decoding and encoding of every record; SHA-256 for each id candidate; the sort of `list`, `ready` and `blocked`; the BLOCKED relation's propagation over parent-child edges. These are the Phase 5 hotspot candidates; none is measured in Phase 1 | `src/util/id.rs:306`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:10977`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:18186`, `src/sync/mod.rs:10779` | `list_json`, `blocked_json`, `create_min` |
| S11.4 | No timing of the original is recorded in Phase 1. The measured baseline is produced in Phase 5 by `scripts/incumbent-bench.sh --pin`, against the pinned binary inside the same sandbox, at thread parity (PLAN §5) | `docs/PLAN_TO_PORT_BEADS_RUST_TO_BEND2.md:103` (PLAN §5) | `list_json` |
