## Appendix A. Handover notes, as the extractors wrote them

### from docs/spec-parts/S1_S9.md


- **S3 (data model).** The `error` envelope is a five-field record with a fixed
  key order and `null`-preserving optionals; `context` is an untyped JSON value
  whose shape varies per code (the key sets are listed per row in S9.8). The
  `where` output is a five-field record with *omitted* optionals — the opposite
  optional policy from the error envelope. `--limit`/`--offset` are
  pointer-width unsigned; `--priority-min`/`--priority-max` are 8-bit unsigned;
  `--estimate` is signed 32-bit; `--lock-timeout` and `--activity-hours` are
  unsigned 64- and 32-bit.
- **S5 (outputs).** S9.30 quotes the empty-store bytes only as edge evidence;
  the general Plain and JSON layouts of `list`, `ready`, `blocked`, `stats`,
  `count`, `search`, `show`, `where` — including the glyph vocabulary
  (`✓ ○ ● ⊘ ✨ 📊`), the pagination envelope
  `{issues,total,limit,offset,has_more}`, the bare-array shape of `ready --json`
  and the two-space-indented pretty-printing used *only* by the error envelope —
  belong to S5. S5 also owns the observation that command results are compact
  JSON with one trailing LF while the error envelope is pretty-printed.
- **S6 (order).** Two order leaks surface through my sections: the
  ambiguous-id candidate list (hash-ordered in the original, canonicalized to
  byte order by DISC-005, S9.34) and the fixed declaration order of the
  subcommand list in `usage_none` and of the option lines in the `Options:`
  block (S1.16, S1.28). The `context` object's key order follows the literal
  construction order, not any sort.
- **S7 (numerics).** The argument-parse error texts expose the parsers:
  `invalid digit found in string` for the integer types and
  `<n> is not in 0..=255` for the 8-bit range check (S1.21, S1.22). `--limit +1`
  parses; `--limit -1` does not reach the parser at all. `0` is a sentinel
  meaning "unlimited" for `list --limit`, `ready --limit`, `blocked --limit` and
  `search --limit`, which S7 needs as a range note.
- **S8 (effects).** The error path performs exactly two effects: one write to
  stderr or stdout and one process exit with the code of S9.6. Environment
  reads are enumerated in S1.57–S1.67. `.beads/last-touched` is read by
  `show`/`update`/`close`/`reopen` when no id is given (S1.55). A run that
  reaches S9.14 has already written `issues.jsonl` and `last-touched` before it
  exits non-zero.
- **S10 (oddities).** Four candidates found here: (1) the usage line of
  `usage_unknown_flag` names the *suggested* option next to the already-consumed
  one (`Usage: br --no-db --no-auto-flush <COMMAND>`), and `--li` likewise
  produces `Usage: br list --limit <LIMIT>` (S1.15, S1.11); (2) the literal
  backticks around `SQLite`, `br close`, `dep add` and the like inside help and
  error text, which come from doc comments written for a lint (S1.28, S9.20,
  S9.21); (3) `INVALID_PRIORITY` duplicates its hint inside `context.hint`
  (S9.15); (4) the comma-splitting of label options is inconsistent across
  `create -l` (splits), `label add -l` (splits), `update --set-labels` (splits,
  by hand), `update --add-label` (does not), `list -l` (does not) — S1.33,
  S1.40, S1.52. Also: several codes that exist with an exit-5 or exit-6
  category are never reached, because the corresponding situation is a
  stdout no-op or is reported as a configuration error (S9.7, S9.28, S9.29).
- **S4 (algorithms).** The partial-id resolution rule that decides between
  `ISSUE_NOT_FOUND`, `AMBIGUOUS_ID` and a hit (S1.56) is an S4 algorithm; S9
  only records its three outcomes.


### from docs/spec-parts/S2_S5a.md


| fact | owner |
|---|---|
| The `ORDER BY id ASC` of S5.3, the intra-record orderings of S5.13–S5.15, and the `show` dependency and dependent sort of S5.31 are order leaks | S6 |
| The comparators behind `list`, `ready`, `blocked` and `search`, and the chronological-versus-textual timestamp question settled by OQ-001 | S6 |
| The widths behind S5.11 — 32-bit signed priority, estimate, compaction level and original size; 64-bit signed comment id; unsigned counts — and the single 64-bit float of S5.54 | S7 |
| The birthday-bound id-length table, the nonce ladder that gives `scn_dup_title` three distinct ids, and the `<parent>.<n>` child-id rule that `scn_child_ids` exercises | S4, S7 |
| Effects: reading the store, writing the store, writing and removing `last-touched`, creating `.beads/` — with the `--no-db` ordering "flush first, sidecar second" of S5.18 | S8 |
| Every verbatim error message with its stream and exit code, including the validation texts of S2.43–S2.56 and the per-error contents of the S5.21 envelope | S9 |
| `br q` writing `"source_repo":"."` while `br create` writes the workspace name (S5.19); `--fields` silently ignored under `--json` (S5.24); `show` reading the file while every other read command reads the loaded model, so the two disagree about the same issue (S5.30, S2.39); a stored custom dependency type loading while `dep add -t` refuses one (S2.33) | S10 |
| The 44-member issue record, the 7-member dependency record and the 5-member comment record, with which members are optional and which are forced non-empty by the round trip (S5.5, S5.7, S5.9) | S3 |
| The Plain-text rendering of every command | S5.100+ |


### from docs/spec-parts/S4a_S7.md


- **S6 (order):** the substring-match candidate list is produced in ascending id order by the store's own `ORDER BY id` over a TEXT column (byte order), and the harness sorts it again — the two agree, but the underlying order is the store's. See S4.44 and DISC-005.
- **S6 (order):** `parse_priority_filter` returns its selection through an ordered set, so filter values are always ascending and de-duplicated regardless of the order typed (S4.67).
- **S6 (order):** labels collected at `create` keep first-occurrence order in memory, while the JSONL write-back sorts and de-duplicates them (`edge_precision_rewrite` shows `["alpha","zeta"]`). The write-back rule belongs to S5.
- **S5 (outputs):** the exact plain-text shapes quoted here (`✓ Created <id>: <title>`, `Dry run: would create issue <id>`, `  Avg Lead Time:          2.0 hours`, the `Error:`/`Hint:` pair) are cited only as evidence for the algorithm; their column widths, glyphs and trailing newlines are S5's to pin.
- **S5 (outputs):** the `defer` JSON payload's `+00:00` offset spelling versus the store's `Z` (S4.64) is a rendering split worth an S5 clause of its own.
- **S3 (data model):** `content_hash` is present on the record type but skipped by its JSON representation (S4.51); the model section owns that field's existence, this section owns its arithmetic.
- **S3 (data model):** `original_size` is declared as an optional signed 32-bit field yet appears as `0` on every exported record, so the loader materializes it — that materialization is S2/S5's.
- **S8 (effects):** the clock is read once per create attempt (S4.52) and again on each of the up-to-ten insert retries (S4.14); the retry path also sleeps 10, 20, … milliseconds, which is unreachable in `--no-db`.
- **S8 (effects):** `--no-db` rebuilds the child-counter table from the file on every invocation (S4.25); that rebuild is an effect of the load, not of `create`.
- **S9 (errors):** every error string quoted here carries its stream and exit code inline; S9 owns the full table.
- **S10 (oddities):** a non-numeric priority is reported **uppercased** (`-p x` → `got: X`) because the uppercase precedes the parse (S4.66). Reproduce, do not fix.
- **S10 (oddities):** a filter token such as `-1` splits at its first `-` into an empty left part and therefore falls through the range branch to the single-value branch, where it is reported as `-1` (S4.67).
- **S10 (oddities):** id lookup lowercases with full Unicode rules, not ASCII rules (S4.36), so a non-ASCII uppercase input can change byte length before the lookup.
- **S10 (oddities):** the id seed measures fields in bytes while the title limit measures in characters (S7.1 versus S4.75) — a 500-character CJK title is 1500 seed bytes and still valid.
- **S10 / DISC-005:** the ambiguous-candidate order is canonicalized by the harness (S4.44).


### from docs/spec-parts/S4b.md


**Order → S6.** Every ordering below is a sort or an iteration order that reaches stdout.

| structure / sort | order exhibited | reaches output via | clause |
|---|---|---|---|
| `list` default | priority ASC, `created_at` DESC (chronological), id ASC (bytes); total, no observable stability | `list`, `search`, `epic status`, the JSON page | S4.115, S4.110, S4.111 |
| `list --sort <key>` | see the four key orders; `created_at`/`updated_at` are DESC un-reversed; id ASC always last | `list`, `search` | S4.116, S4.117 |
| title collation | ASCII-only case-insensitive; any ≥ 0xC2 byte after all ASCII | `--sort title` | S4.112 |
| `ready` hybrid | bucket(priority ≤ 1) ASC, `created_at` ASC, id ASC — priority is *not* a key inside a bucket | `ready` | S4.174 |
| `ready` priority / oldest | priority ASC then `created_at` ASC then id ASC / `created_at` ASC then id ASC | `ready --sort` | S4.175, S4.176 |
| `blocked` | priority ASC, **blocker count DESC**, `created_at` DESC, id ASC | `blocked` | S4.169 |
| blocker ref list | ascending byte order of the annotated ref, then adjacent dedup | `blocked_by`, the text `[…]` list | S4.162 |
| `count` groups | ascending byte order of the group key string (`(` before letters) | `count --by-*` | S4.149 |
| `dep list` | JSON keeps the query order (endpoint priority ASC with missing = 2, `created_at` DESC, id ASC); text re-sorts by priority, `issue_id`, `depends_on_id`, type | `dep list` | S4.180 |
| `dep tree` | depth-first pre-order; siblings by priority, status rank, title, id | `dep tree` | S4.183, S4.184 |
| `dep cycles` | components sorted; witnesses sorted as id vectors; nodes inside a witness follow the walk from the lowest id | `dep cycles` | S4.187 |
| `show` | requested input order across targets; labels ASC; deps/dependents by S4.180; comments `created_at` ASC then id ASC | `show` | S4.194, S4.195 |
| BLOCKED build | three ordered steps — direct, propagate, epic rollup — and the order is observable | `ready`, `blocked`, `stats` | S4.161 |

**Numerics → S7.** `priority` is a small signed integer confined to `0..=4` by every parser (S4.107, S4.108). Counts (`total`, `count`, `blocked_by_count`, group counts, `total_children`) are unsigned machine words; the corpus reaches 8. The epic percentage is an integer truncating division computed in 128-bit width (S4.191). The average lead time is the only floating-point figure: whole-hour integers (a truncating conversion from a duration, S4.154) summed and divided by the count; the corpus reaches 2.0 and 558.0 hours, and the 558.0/24 = 23.25 case lands exactly on a ties-to-even boundary when printed to one decimal (`23.2`, not `23.3`) — S5 owns the printing, S7 owns the fact that the value is exact in binary.

**Effects → S8.** Every command in this section is read-only: it reads `.beads/issues.jsonl` (and `.beads/policy.yaml` for the ready status group and the custom-status vocabulary, and `.beads/last-touched` when `show` has no positional id), reads the wall clock once (S4.113), and writes only stdout/stderr. The stale blocked-cache projection is recomputed in memory and never persisted on a read path. Whether a read-only command ever creates a file under `.beads/` is OQ-008.

**Oddities → S10.** (1) `list --fields` is silently ignored outside CSV (S4.134). (2) `list --deferred` reads as "only deferred" but is a no-op on the default corpus (S4.131). (3) `count`'s default corpus hides deferred issues while `list`'s shows them (S4.144). (4) `--title-contains` and `search` fold ASCII case only, while `--desc-contains`/`--notes-contains` fold Unicode case (S4.109, S4.129, S4.137). (5) `search -p 0-1` is an error while `list -p 0-1` is accepted — `search` parses each `--priority` value as a single priority with no ranges or comma lists (S4.140). (6) The `blocked` text line says `open dependencies` even for one blocker and even when the blocker ref is a `:parent-blocked` or `:child-open` rollup rather than a dependency. (7) `stats` counts templates and every custom-status issue in `total_issues` but in none of the per-status counters (S4.151). (8) `ready`'s hybrid default can list a P3 above a P2 (S4.174). (9) `--blocking-only` on `dep cycles` changes nothing (S4.186).

**Data model → S3.** Fields this section reads: `id`, `title`, `description`, `notes`, `status`, `priority`, `issue_type`, `assignee`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `closed_at`, `defer_until`, `due_at`, `ephemeral`, `pinned`, `is_template`, `labels`, `dependencies` (`issue_id`, `depends_on_id`, `type`), `comments` (`id`, `author`, `text`, `created_at`). Absent booleans (`ephemeral`, `pinned`, `is_template`) behave as false everywhere. Absent optional strings never match a containment filter. A dependency target that is not a stored issue is legal and carries the synthetic status `unknown` in a blocker ref (S4.157). `search`'s corpus includes comment text, so comments are part of the queryable surface.


### from docs/spec-parts/S4c.md


**To S6 (order).**
- Store line order after every mutation is ascending byte order of `id` (S4.206); hierarchical ids sort as plain strings, so `proj-uly.1.1` precedes `proj-uly.2`.
- Multi-id `update`/`close` emit their per-id output blocks in command-line order; `reopen`/`undefer`/`defer` emit all successes before all skips (S4.314, S4.327).
- `label add`/`label remove` iterate labels in the outer loop and issues in the inner loop (S4.370).
- `delete` sorts its id set ascending BEFORE resolution and again before tombstoning, so the command-line order never reaches the output; the `--cascade` edge-removal pass iterates a hash set in unspecified order, but that pass produces no ordered output (S4.335, S4.340).
- The `dependencies` array of a record created with several `--deps` is re-ordered by the export, not by the command (S4.229).
- `dep cycles` is the only surface that prints an actual cycle witness; `dep add` never does (S4.355).
- The ambiguous-id candidate list is hash-random in the original and is canonicalized to byte order by `scripts/ws_inner.py` (DISC-005) — S4.275.

**To S7 (numerics).**
- `priority` is an integer 0–4 inclusive; out-of-range is refused (S4.239). Default `2`.
- `estimated_minutes` is a signed 32-bit integer; negatives are refused (`cannot be negative`) and values above `525960` are refused (`exceeds maximum (525960 minutes / ~1 year)`).
- `TITLE_MAX_CHARS` = **500**, counted in Unicode scalars, not bytes (S4.222).
- Label length limit = **50** bytes; `external_ref` limit = **200** bytes with no whitespace allowed.
- `ISSUE_LABEL_MAX_COUNT` = **64** (`src/validation/mod.rs:24`); exceeding it gives `exceeds 64 labels`. The corpus never reaches it.
- `comments add -f` / `-f -` caps its input at **10485760** bytes (10 MiB) and requires valid UTF-8 (S4.381).
- A bare date given to `--until`, `--due` or `--defer` resolves to **09:00:00 local time** of that day, not midnight; the keyword `today` resolves to **17:00:00 local time** (S4.322). Under `TZ=UTC` local equals UTC.
- The cap on `create -f` bulk import and on `delete --from-file` input sizes is unmeasured.
- `compaction_level` and `original_size` default to `0` on every created record; `--dry-run --json` omits `original_size` (S4.235).
- Comment ids are a monotone integer sequence over the whole store, `max + 1` (S4.382).
- `close` reports `closed_at` with a `+00:00` offset in JSON while the store writes `Z` (S4.298); `defer` does the same for `defer_until` (S4.322).
- The cycle search is depth-bounded (`DEPENDENCY_TRAVERSAL_MAX_DEPTH`, `src/storage/sqlite.rs:6976`); beyond that depth a cycle is not reported.

**To S8 (effects).**
- Files written by this surface: `.beads/issues.jsonl` (one full rewrite per invocation, only when something is dirty — S4.202, S4.203) and `.beads/last-touched` (S4.207–S4.211).
- `.beads/last-touched` is created with mode `0600`, truncated, written best-effort (a failure never changes the exit code), and holds `<id>\n`.
- `delete` never creates `last-touched`; it removes the file when the stored id was deleted (S4.210).
- `label rename` writes the store but not `last-touched` (S4.211).
- `create --ephemeral` writes `last-touched` but not the store (S4.234).
- `create --dry-run`, `delete --dry-run` and `epic close-eligible --dry-run` write nothing at all (S4.235, S4.338, S4.393).
- Reads: `--description-file <path>` and `comments add -f <path>` read a file (or stdin for `-`) exactly once, before any mutation; `create -f`, `delete --from-file`, `--agent-context @path` likewise (S4.225, S4.237, S4.340).
- Clock: every mutating command reads the wall clock once per issue update for `updated_at`/`closed_at`/`deleted_at`/`created_at`; in a pinned run all of them equal the pinned instant.
- Environment read on this surface: `USER`, plus `BD_ACTOR`, `BEADS_ACTOR`, `LOGNAME`, `USERNAME` for the comment-author ladder, plus `BR_AGENT_NAME`, `BR_HARNESS`, `BR_MODEL` (recorded on events only, never in the JSONL — S4.214).
- Excluded effects observed in the source but not on this surface: `.br_history/` backups, the lock files, `close_metadata`, `policy.yaml` gates.

**To S10 (oddities, reproduce, do not fix).**
- `update --status <same value>` bumps `updated_at` and rewrites the store, while `-p <same value>` does not (S4.262 vs S4.264).
- `close` exits **3** on a partially applied batch although it did write the closures (S4.294) — the only non-zero exit with a rewritten store.
- `reopen` has no `NOTHING_TO_DO`: an all-skipped reopen exits **0** while an all-skipped close exits **3** (S4.313 vs S4.293).
- `delete` with dependents and no flag is a silent preview that exits **0** (S4.333).
- `references_updated` is always `0`; `labels_removed`/`events_removed` are always `0` for a soft delete even when the issue has labels (S4.336).
- `create --dry-run --json` omits `original_size` although a real create emits it (S4.235).
- `create --deps` accepts `blocked-by` and lists it in its error text; `dep add --type` does not (S4.229 vs S4.352).
- `q` writes no `source_repo`/`source_repo_path`, so its records end up with `"source_repo":"."` while `create`'s carry the directory basename (S4.251).
- `dep add`/`dep remove` write `last-touched` even when they changed nothing (S4.209, S4.358, S4.360).
- `close` prints `closed_at` with `+00:00` in JSON but writes `Z` to the store (S4.298).
- `create --status tombstone` produces a tombstone without `delete_reason` or `original_type`, unlike `delete` (S4.231 vs S4.330).
- `epic close-eligible` writes `close_reason` `All children completed` while `close` without `--reason` writes `done` — two different defaults for the same field (S4.391 vs S4.291).
- `defer` WITHOUT `--until` clears an existing `defer_until` rather than leaving it alone (S4.323).
- `label remove` of an absent label prints the SUCCESS glyph `✓` on stdout, while `dep remove` of an absent edge prints `Warning:` on stderr (S4.375 vs S4.360).
- `epic close-eligible --dry-run` prints `Would close 0 epic(s):` where the non-dry run prints `No epics eligible for closure` (S4.392 vs S4.393).
- `label add`'s positional split makes the LAST positional a label even when it is id-shaped (S4.370).

**To S3 (data model).**
- Fields this surface writes and their exact JSON key order as the store shows them: `id`, `title`, `description`, `acceptance_criteria`, `status`, `priority`, `issue_type`, `assignee`, `owner`, `estimated_minutes`, `created_at`, `created_by`, `updated_at`, `closed_at`, `close_reason`, `closed_by_session`, `due_at`, `defer_until`, `external_ref`, `source_repo`, `source_repo_path`, `deleted_at`, `deleted_by`, `delete_reason`, `original_type`, `compaction_level`, `original_size`, `notes`, `labels`, `dependencies`, `comments`, `ephemeral`.
- Dependency edge record: `issue_id`, `depends_on_id`, `type`, `created_at`, `created_by`, `metadata` (default the two-character string `"{}"`), `thread_id` (default the empty string `""`) — S4.350.
- Comment record: `id`, `issue_id`, `author`, `text`, `created_at` — S4.380.
- `content_hash` is recomputed on every mutation but never exported (S4.214).
- `status` reaches the values `open`, `in_progress`, `closed`, `deferred`, `tombstone` on this surface; `blocked`, `draft` and `pinned` appear only in skip messages.

---


### from docs/spec-parts/S5b.md


Facts discovered here that belong to another section's owner:

- **S5a (JSON / the JSONL store).** Every `*_json` case, the `{"error":{…}}` pretty-printed envelope that `dep_add_custom_type` and `update_status_closed` put on **stdout** with exit 4 (a plain run would have put `Error:` on stderr instead), and the `--- .beads/issues.jsonl ---` bodies. `close --suggest-next`, `close --force`, `reopen`, `defer`, `undefer` and `delete` emit a JSON document even without `--json` when `use_json` is set internally (`close.rs:635`, `reopen.rs:143`, `defer.rs:181`); `close_blocked_force` proves it. Also: `dep add`/`dep remove`/`label`/`comments`/`count` use the *pretty* JSON writer while `create`/`q`/`ready`/`list` use the compact one.
- **S6 (order).** Row order in `list`, `ready`, `blocked`, `search`, `dep list`, `count --by-*` group order, `label list-all` order, `show`'s dependency/dependent/comment order, `epic status` order, and the `close`/`update` per-id report order (argv order, `update.rs:1417`; `delete`'s ids are sorted, `delete.rs:636`). `blocked` sorts by priority ascending then blocker count descending (`blocked.rs:208`). `list --tree` sorts sibling groups by the numeric trailing id segment (`list.rs:775-788`).
- **S7 (numerics).** The epic percentage is integer truncation of `closed * 100 / total` computed in 128-bit (`epic.rs:435-443`). The average lead time is an `f64` printed with `{:.1}`, switching unit at exactly 24.0 hours (`stats.rs:1252-1260`) — the rounding mode at a `.x5` boundary is a NUMERIC_PLAN row. Display widths for truncation use `unicode-width`, not byte or char counts (`src/format/text.rs:258`), which matters only on the excluded TTY path.
- **S8 (effects).** `create --dry-run` and `delete --dry-run` and the `delete` dependents preview write no file. `dep remove` on a missing edge still rewrites the store (`dep_remove_absent`). `show`'s deferred countdown and `list --overdue` read the wall clock at render time. `br show` may open a second transient storage handle for inherited context (`show.rs:340-352`).
- **S9 (errors).** The full `Error:`/`Hint:` catalogue, exit codes, and the clap usage texts of `usage_*`. This part fixes only the stream (stderr), the two-line shape, and the fact that stdout is left empty or partial.
- **S10 (oddities to reproduce, not fix).** (a) `Blocked by 1 open dependencies` is never singularized. (b) `br reopen` ignores `--quiet` and prints anyway; `br create --silent` overrides every mode including `--json`. (c) `show` says `Deferred until:` while `list --long` says `Deferred Until:`. (d) `dep add` uses an ASCII `->` for success and a Unicode `→` for "already exists"; `dep remove` does the same split. (e) `search`'s truncation note goes to stdout while every other truncation note goes to stderr. (f) `blocked`'s header count is the pre-truncation total while `ready`'s is the post-truncation count. (g) `delete` has two renderers whose bytes differ (S5.238). (h) `show`'s `Owner:` line invents a value from `$USER` when the record has none.
- **S3 (data model).** The plain surface reveals: status is an open set (a custom status renders with `?` and its own lower-case name), issue type is an open set (`create_custom_type` stores `spike`), priority is a small integer rendered `P{n}`, and comment bodies may carry interior newlines and tabs.


### from docs/spec-parts/S6_S8_S10.md


Facts discovered here that belong to another section's table.

| fact | owner |
|---|---|
| `list` default filtering excludes `closed` and `tombstone` but includes `deferred`; `--all` adds closed (`list_plain` 7 rows, `list_all` 8 rows, `count_plain` `6` versus `count_include_closed` `8` — `count` excludes deferred as well as closed) | S4 |
| the `list` envelope is `{"issues":[…],"total":N,"limit":L,"offset":O,"has_more":B}`; `search`'s is `{"issues":[…],"hidden_closed_count":N,"limit":50,"offset":0,"has_more":B}`; `blocked`'s is the `list` envelope with `limit` `50` | S5 |
| the exit-code table `0`/`2`/`3`/`4`/`5`/`7` with the error codes `NOT_INITIALIZED`, `ISSUE_NOT_FOUND`, `AMBIGUOUS_ID`, `NOTHING_TO_DO`, `VALIDATION_FAILED`, `INVALID_PRIORITY`, `CYCLE_DETECTED`, `CONFIG_ERROR` and their verbatim messages and hints | S9 |
| blocking dependency types are `blocks`, `conditional-blocks` and `waits-for`; `related`, `parent-child`, `discovered-from`, `replies-to`, `relates-to`, `duplicates`, `supersedes` and `caused-by` do not block. Probe: an issue with four dependencies of which one is `related` is reported as `Blocked by 3 open dependencies` | S4 |
| a child id is `<parent>.<n>` with `n` starting at `1` (`create_with_parent` yields `proj-uly.1`; a grandchild of `proj-uly.1` is `proj-uly.1.1` in `scn_child_ids`) | S4 |
| the id seed is length-prefixed `title`, `description`, `creator`, the `created_at` nanosecond count and the nonce; the only entropy is the clock and the actor | S4 |
| the average lead time is an `f64` computed from per-issue integer hours; `average_lead_time_hours` is absent from the JSON when no issue is closed | S7 |
| `estimated_minutes` is an integer (`create_full_json` stores `90`); `compaction_level` and `original_size` are integers always emitted on an exported record | S7 |
| timestamps are rendered with `Z` in exported records and with `+00:00` in some command results; fractional digits follow a 0/3/6/9 rule (`…05Z`, `…05.100Z`, `…05.000000001Z`) | S5, S7 |
| `--sort title` folds only ASCII `A`–`Z`; non-ASCII is compared by raw UTF-8 bytes | S4 |
| `search` matches title and description, case-insensitively (`search_description`, `search_case_insensitive`, `search_unicode`) | S4 |
| labels are validated as alphanumeric, hyphen, underscore and colon (`error_create_bad_label`, `error_label_add_invalid`) | S2 |
| titles are limited to 500 characters (`error_create_long_title` at 501, `edge_create_max_title` at 500) | S2 |
| the `empty` fixture is a zero-byte `issues.jsonl`; `@fx=none` means no `.beads` directory at all | S2 |
